   218	if (import.meta.main) {
   219	  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   220	  const assertZero = args.includes("--assert-encrypted-zero");
   221	  const assertNoReuse = args.includes("--assert-no-r-reuse");
   222	  const endpoint = endpointFrom(args);
   223	  const positional = args.filter((a, i) => !a.startsWith("--") && args[i - 1] !== "--endpoint");
   224	  const [account, issuanceIdRaw] = positional;
   225	  const issuanceId = issuanceIdRaw?.toUpperCase();  // rippled returns uppercase hex; accept either
   226	  if (!account || !issuanceId) {
   227	    console.error("usage: bun run tools/observer-harness.ts <account> <MPTokenIssuanceID> [--endpoint wss://…] [--assert-encrypted-zero] [--assert-no-r-reuse]");
   228	    process.exit(2);
   229	  }
   230	
   231	  const info = await connect(endpoint);
   232	  console.log(`# Observer harness — everything below is public\n`);
   233	  console.log(`observed from: ${endpoint}${endpoint === DEVNET_WS ? "  (the public endpoint — what anybody sees)" : "  (⚠ NOT the public endpoint: a node someone chose)"}\n`);
   234	  console.log(`network_id ${info.network_id}, rippled ${info.build_version}, ledger ${info.validated_ledger?.seq}`);
   235	  console.log(`observer holds: no private key, no encryption key, no library beyond SHA-256 and secp256k1\n`);
   236	
   237	  const iss = (await rpc({ command: "ledger_entry", mpt_issuance: issuanceId, ledger_index: "validated" })).result?.node;
   238	  if (!iss) { console.error(`no MPTokenIssuance ${issuanceId}`); process.exit(1); }
   239	  console.log(`## The issuance, in clear`);
   240	  for (const k of ["Issuer", "Flags", "MaximumAmount", "OutstandingAmount", "ConfidentialOutstandingAmount", "TransferFee", "DomainID"])
   241	    if (iss[k] !== undefined) console.log(`  ${k.padEnd(32)} ${iss[k]}`);
   242	  console.log(`  ${"IssuerEncryptionKey".padEnd(32)} ${iss.IssuerEncryptionKey ? "present — the issuer can decrypt every balance" : "absent"}`);
   243	  console.log(`  ${"AuditorEncryptionKey".padEnd(32)} ${iss.AuditorEncryptionKey ? "present — a third party can decrypt every balance too" : "absent"}`);
   244	  if (iss.ConfidentialOutstandingAmount !== undefined)
   245	    console.log(`\n  Note: ConfidentialOutstandingAmount is PLAINTEXT. The total value held confidentially\n  across all holders is public, even though no individual balance is.`);
   246	
   247	  const objs = await rpc({ command: "account_objects", account, ledger_index: "validated", type: "mptoken" });
   248	  const mpt = (objs.result?.account_objects ?? []).find((o: any) => o.MPTokenIssuanceID === issuanceId);
   249	  if (!mpt) { console.error(`\n${account} holds no MPToken for ${issuanceId}`); process.exit(1); }
   250	
   251	  console.log(`\n## The holder's position, as an observer sees it`);
   252	  console.log(`  ${"Account".padEnd(32)} ${mpt.Account}`);
   253	  console.log(`  ${"public MPTAmount".padEnd(32)} ${mpt.MPTAmount ?? 0}   <- NOT hidden`);
   254	  console.log(`  ${"HolderEncryptionKey".padEnd(32)} ${mpt.HolderEncryptionKey ?? "(not registered)"}`);
   255	  console.log(`  ${"ConfidentialBalanceVersion".padEnd(32)} ${mpt.ConfidentialBalanceVersion ?? 0}   <- counts balance-changing transactions`);
   256	  for (const f of CIPHERTEXT_FIELDS) if (mpt[f]) console.log(`  ${f.padEnd(32)} ${mpt[f].slice(0, 24)}…  (${mpt[f].length / 2} B, opaque without a key)`);
   257	
   258	  // --- F-1 -----------------------------------------------------------------
   259	  let provedZero = 0;
   260	  let perturbationClean = true;
   261	  console.log(`\n## F-1 — which of those "opaque" balances are publicly known to be zero?`);
   262	  if (!mpt.HolderEncryptionKey) {
   263	    console.log(`  no registered key, so no confidential state to test`);
   264	  } else {
   265	    const encZero = canonicalEncZero(mpt.HolderEncryptionKey, account, issuanceId);
   266	    console.log(`  recomputed canonical EncZero from public inputs only:`);
   267	    console.log(`    ${encZero.slice(0, 48)}…`);
   268	    for (const f of CIPHERTEXT_FIELDS) {
   269	      if (!mpt[f]) continue;
   270	      // The issuer and auditor mirrors are encrypted to a DIFFERENT key, so the
   271	      // holder-keyed EncZero cannot match them; only the holder-keyed balances
   272	      // are testable this way.
   273	      const holderKeyed = f === "ConfidentialBalanceSpending" || f === "ConfidentialBalanceInbox";
   274	      if (!holderKeyed) { console.log(`    ${f.padEnd(30)} (encrypted to another party's key — not testable with this EncZero)`); continue; }
   275	      const match = mpt[f] === encZero;
   276	      if (match) provedZero++;
   277	      console.log(`    ${f.padEnd(30)} ${match ? "*** EXACTLY ZERO, publicly ***" : "not the canonical zero (some non-zero value, or never reset)"}`);
   278	    }
   279	
   280	    // A match only means something if a WRONG input fails to match. Recompute
   281	    // against a perturbed AccountID: it must collide with nothing. Without this
   282	    // the assertion below could pass on a recomputation that returns a constant,
   283	    // or on one that has silently drifted from upstream into some other fixed
   284	    // value. This is the check that makes the positive result evidence.
   285	    const wrong = perturbedAccount(account);
   286	    const bogus = canonicalEncZero(mpt.HolderEncryptionKey, wrong, issuanceId);
   287	    const collided = CIPHERTEXT_FIELDS.some((f) => mpt[f] === bogus);
   288	    perturbationClean = !collided && bogus !== encZero;
   289	    console.log(`  control: EncZero for a perturbed AccountID matches nothing on this object: ${perturbationClean}`);
   290	  }
   291	
   292	  // --- randomness reuse ----------------------------------------------------
   293	  // No key needed. C1 = r*G is on the wire, so reuse is visible to anyone, and
   294	  // when it happens the DIFFERENCE between two hidden amounts falls out.
   295	  const allTx = (await rpc({ command: "account_tx", account, limit: 200, ledger_index_min: -1, ledger_index_max: -1 })).result?.transactions ?? [];
   296	  const reused = findReusedR(allTx);
   297	  let leaked = 0;
   298	
   299	  console.log(`\n## Randomness reuse — was the same ElGamal r used twice?`);
   300	  console.log(`  scanned ${allTx.length} transactions. C1 = r*G is public, so this needs no key.`);
   301	  if (reused.length === 0) {
   302	    console.log(`  no C1 appears in more than one transaction. Nothing recoverable this way.`);
   303	    console.log(`  (C1 repeating WITHIN one Send is by design — see CLAIMS.md E-4/F-13 — and is not reuse.)`);
   304	  } else {
   305	    console.log(`  *** ${reused.length} reused C1 value(s) found. This is a signer defect. ***`);
   306	    for (const { c1, entries } of reused) {
   307	      const hashes = [...new Set(entries.map((e) => e.hash))];
   308	      console.log(`\n  C1 ${c1.slice(0, 32)}…  in ${hashes.length} transactions:`);
   309	      for (const h of hashes) console.log(`    ${h}`);
   310	      // Subtract same-field ciphertexts from two different transactions: the
   311	      // r*pk term cancels and (m - m')*G remains.
   312	      for (const f of TX_ELGAMAL_FIELDS) {
   313	        const inField = entries.filter((e) => e.field === f);
   314	        if (inField.length < 2) continue;
   315	        const [a1, b1] = inField;
   316	        if (a1.hash === b1.hash) continue;
   317	        try {
   318	          const P = secp256k1.ProjectivePoint.fromHex(C2of(a1.ct));
   319	          const Q = secp256k1.ProjectivePoint.fromHex(C2of(b1.ct));
   320	          const d = smallDlog(P.subtract(Q), 100_000);
   321	          if (d !== null) {
   322	            leaked++;
   323	            console.log(`    ${f}: the two hidden amounts differ by ${d} — recovered with no key`);
   324	          } else {
   325	            console.log(`    ${f}: difference exceeds the +/-100,000 search range`);
   326	          }
   327	        } catch { /* unparseable point; skip rather than guess */ }
   328	      }
   329	    }
   330	    console.log(`\n  What this does and does not show: it recovers the DIFFERENCE between two`);
   331	    console.log(`  amounts, not either amount, and it requires the signer to have reused r.`);
   332	    console.log(`  It is not a break of XLS-96 as specified. rippled accepts reused r — there is`);
   333	    console.log(`  no ledger-side defence — so this is entirely the signer's responsibility.`);
   334	    console.log(`  See CLAIMS.md E-63 and issue #3.`);
   335	  }
   336	
   337	  // --- what confidentiality does not cover ---------------------------------
   338	  const txs = await rpc({ command: "account_tx", account, limit: 200, ledger_index_min: -1, ledger_index_max: -1 });
   339	  const conf = (txs.result?.transactions ?? []).filter((t: any) => String((t.tx_json ?? t.tx)?.TransactionType ?? "").startsWith("ConfidentialMPT"));
   340	  console.log(`\n## What confidentiality does NOT cover — ${conf.length} confidential transactions visible`);
   341	  console.log(`  ${"date".padEnd(22)} ${"type".padEnd(28)} ${"counterparty".padEnd(36)} tag`);
   342	  for (const t of conf.slice(0, 12)) {
   343	    const tx = t.tx_json ?? t.tx;
   344	    const when = tx.date ? new Date((tx.date + 946684800) * 1000).toISOString().replace("T", " ").slice(0, 19) : "?";
   345	    console.log(`  ${when.padEnd(22)} ${String(tx.TransactionType).padEnd(28)} ${String(tx.Destination ?? "—").padEnd(36)} ${tx.DestinationTag ?? ""}`);
   346	  }
   347	  console.log(`\n  Every row above is public: who transacted, with whom, when, how often, and any`);
   348	  console.log(`  DestinationTag. Only the AMOUNTS are hidden. See CLAIMS.md P-4 and P-11.`);
   349	  console.log(`  A holder is never private from the issuer — see P-9 and tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts.`);
   350	
   351	  ws.close();
   352	
   353	  if (assertNoReuse) {
   354	    if (reused.length === 0) {
   355	      console.log(`\nASSERTION PASSED: no ElGamal randomness reuse across ${allTx.length} transactions.`);
   356	      if (!assertZero) process.exit(0);
   357	    } else {
   358	      console.error(`\nASSERTION FAILED: ${reused.length} reused C1 value(s); ${leaked} amount difference(s) recovered without a key.`);
   359	      console.error(`The signer that produced these transactions reuses r. That is a defect in the`);
   360	      console.error(`signer, not in the protocol, and no amount of ledger-side behaviour will fix it.`);
   361	      process.exit(1);
   362	    }
   363	  }
   364	
   365	  if (assertZero) {
   366	    if (provedZero > 0 && perturbationClean) { console.log(`\nASSERTION PASSED: ${provedZero} balance(s) publicly identifiable as exactly zero, and the perturbed control matched nothing. F-1 holds.`); process.exit(0); }
   367	    if (provedZero > 0 && !perturbationClean) {
   368	      console.error(`\nASSERTION FAILED: a balance matched, but so did the perturbed control.`);
   369	      console.error(`The match is therefore not evidence of anything. Suspect a broken recomputation.`);
   370	      process.exit(1);
   371	    }
   372	    console.error(`\nASSERTION FAILED: no balance matched the recomputed canonical EncZero.`);
   373	    console.error(`Either F-1 no longer holds, this recomputation has drifted from`);
   374	    console.error(`mpt-crypto's generate_canonical_encrypted_zero, or this account simply has no`);
   375	    console.error(`zero balance right now. Point it at a freshly-initialised or post-merge account.`);
   376	    process.exit(1);
   377	  }
   378	}
     1	/**
     2	 * What a full-ledger observer sees.
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠ **A demo of the happy path is not evidence of confidentiality** — this is
     5	 * the surface that says what is actually public, and its first duty is
     6	 * `CLAIMS.md` **F-1**: a freshly-initialised or post-merge balance is
     7	 * **publicly identifiable as exactly zero**, because `EncZero` is canonical and
     8	 * recomputable by anyone from the holder's public key, the account id and the
     9	 * issuance id. The observer harness proves that by recomputing it
    10	 * independently; this view reports it.
    11	 *
    12	 * ⚠ **P-4 and P-11 govern every sentence here.** Sender, receiver, timing and
    13	 * frequency are public. Only amounts are hidden.
    14	 */
    15	
    16	import type { Snapshot } from "../../facility/src/loopback.ts";
    17	import { canonicalEncZero } from "../../tools/observer-harness.ts";
    18	
    19	export interface ObserverView {
    20	  account: string;
    21	  issuanceId: string;
    22	  /**
    23	   * Public and plaintext. Not hidden by anything.
    24	   *
    25	   * ⚠ **A DECIMAL STRING.** `MPTAmount` is a UINT64 and a JS `Number` rounds
    26	   * anything over 2^53 — the issuer path was fixed for this (E-215) and this
    27	   * one was missed, which is the fix-the-instance-not-the-phrase shape again
    28	   * (`CLAIMS.md` E-220).
    29	   */
    30	  publicAmount: string;
    31	  registered: boolean;
    32	  holderEncryptionKey?: string;
    33	  confidentialBalanceVersion: number;
    34	  /** ⚠ The whole point of F-1. */
    35	  spending: CiphertextView | null;
    36	  inbox: CiphertextView | null;
    37	  issuance: {
    38	    issuer: string;
    39	    /** ⚠ PLAINTEXT on the issuance object. The sum of every hidden position. */
    40	    /** ⚠ A decimal string, for the same reason as `publicAmount`. */
    41	    confidentialOutstandingAmount: string;
    42	    hasIssuerKey: boolean;
    43	    hasAuditorKey: boolean;
    44	  };
    45	  whatIsPublic: string[];
    46	  whatIsHidden: string[];
    47	}
    48	
    49	export interface CiphertextView {
    50	  ciphertext: string;
    51	  /** True when the ciphertext equals the canonical encryption of zero. */
    52	  publiclyKnownZero: boolean;
    53	  note: string;
    54	}
    55	
    56	/** Everything on an observer page that is readable with no secret at all. */
    57	export const WHAT_IS_PUBLIC: string[] = [
    58	      "This account's address, and every counterparty it has transacted with (P-4, P-11).",
    59	      "Every transaction's timing and frequency (P-11).",
    60	      "The public, plaintext MPT balance shown above.",
    61	      "The issuance's ConfidentialOutstandingAmount — plaintext, and the sum of every hidden position.",
    62	      "The holder's registered encryption key, once they have converted.",
    63	      "The ConfidentialBalanceVersion, which counts state changes.",
    64	      "⚠ Whether a confidential balance is exactly zero — but ONLY when it carries the canonical " +
    65	        "encryption of zero, which is used on first-time init, merge reset and clawback (F-1). " +
    66	        "⚠ A balance that reached zero by SPENDING is not publicly identifiable as zero (E-211).",
    67	      "⚠ Every individual tranche size when a position is retired in tranches — MPTAmount is plaintext on each ConvertBack (F-24).",
    68	    ];
    69	
    70	/**
    71	 * ⚠ **The complete list, and it is two items.** Anything not here is public.
    72	 */
    73	export const WHAT_IS_HIDDEN: string[] = [
    74	      "The value of a confidential balance that does not carry the canonical encryption of zero — " +
    75	        "⚠ including when that value happens to BE zero (E-211).",
    76	      "The amount moved by a ConfidentialMPTSend, from an observer.",
    77	      "⚠ That is the complete list. Nothing else on this page is hidden from anyone.",
    78	    ];
    79	
    80	/** ⚠ F-1, stated where it is true rather than in a footnote. */
    81	export const CANONICAL_ZERO_NOTE =
    82	  "⚠ PUBLICLY KNOWN TO BE EXACTLY ZERO. EncZero is canonical — anyone recomputes it from the " +
    83	  "holder's public key, the account id and the issuance id, with no secret at all. A zero " +
    84	  "confidential balance is not hidden (CLAIMS.md F-1).";
    85	
    86	/**
    87	 * ⚠⚠ **THIS SAID "Non-zero, and the amount is hidden from an observer" AND THAT
    88	 * WAS FALSE (`CLAIMS.md` E-211).**
    89	 *
    90	 * Measured on Devnet 2026-08-28: holder `rDYMkRMh7o7QfkNaoGvtd7TgFzmt12FEM8`
    91	 * decrypts to **0** through the issuer mirror, while its
    92	 * `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` does **not** match the canonical `EncZero`.
    93	 * **A balance can be exactly zero and not publicly identifiable as zero** —
    94	 * F-1's canonical form is used on first-time init, merge reset and clawback, so
    95	 * a balance that reached zero by SPENDING is a homomorphic result: a randomised
    96	 * encryption of zero that matches nothing.
    97	 *
    98	 * ⚠ `tools/observer-harness.ts:277` had the correct hedge — *"not the canonical
    99	 * zero (some non-zero value, or never reset)"* — and this tier flattened it into
   100	 * a flat claim. **The correct source was one import away.**
   101	 */
   102	export const NOT_CANONICAL_NOTE =
   103	  "Does NOT match the canonical encryption of zero, so an observer cannot determine this " +
   104	  "balance's value. ⚠ That is NOT the same as saying it is non-zero: a balance that reached " +
   105	  "zero by spending is a randomised encryption of zero and matches nothing, so it looks exactly " +
   106	  "like this. Only a freshly-initialised, post-merge or post-clawback balance uses the canonical " +
   107	  "form (CLAIMS.md F-1). ⚠ Whatever the value is, it is NOT hidden from the issuer — the " +
   108	  "decryption mirror is mandatory and no configuration disables it (P-9).";
   109	
   110	function classify(ciphertext: string | undefined, encZero: string | null): CiphertextView | null {
   111	  if (!ciphertext) return null;
   112	  if (encZero && ciphertext.toUpperCase() === encZero) {
   113	    return {
   114	      ciphertext,
   115	      publiclyKnownZero: true,
   116	      note: CANONICAL_ZERO_NOTE,
   117	    };
   118	  }
   119	  return {
   120	    ciphertext,
   121	    publiclyKnownZero: false,
   122	    note: NOT_CANONICAL_NOTE,
   123	  };
   124	}
   125	
   126	export async function observerView(snap: Snapshot, account: string, issuanceId: string): Promise<ObserverView> {
   127	  const mpt = await snap.mptoken(account, issuanceId);
   128	  const iss = await snap.issuance(issuanceId);
   129	
   130	  const key = mpt?.holderEncryptionKey;
   131	  const encZero = key ? canonicalEncZero(key, account, issuanceId) : null;
   132	
   133	  return {
   134	    account,
   135	    issuanceId,
   136	    publicAmount: mpt?.publicAmountRaw ?? "0",
   137	    registered: Boolean(key),
   138	    holderEncryptionKey: key,
   139	    confidentialBalanceVersion: mpt?.confidentialBalanceVersion ?? 0,
   140	    spending: classify(mpt?.confidentialBalanceSpending, encZero),
   141	    inbox: classify(mpt?.confidentialBalanceInbox, encZero),
   142	    issuance: {
   143	      issuer: iss.issuer,
   144	      confidentialOutstandingAmount: iss.confidentialOutstandingAmountRaw,
   145	      hasIssuerKey: Boolean(iss.issuerEncryptionKey),
   146	      hasAuditorKey: Boolean(iss.auditorEncryptionKey),
   147	    },
   148	    whatIsPublic: WHAT_IS_PUBLIC,
   149	    whatIsHidden: WHAT_IS_HIDDEN,
   150	  };
   151	}
     1	/**
     2	 * What the ISSUER sees — read from the CLI tool's OUTPUT, never decrypted here.
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` P-9: a holder is never private from the issuer.** The
     5	 * decryption mirror is mandatory and no configuration makes this false. Showing
     6	 * the issuer's view beside the observer's is the honest counterpart to showing
     7	 * what the public sees.
     8	 *
     9	 * # ⚠⚠ THIS MODULE HELD THE ISSUER KEY AND DECRYPTED PER REQUEST. THAT WAS A
    10	 * DECRYPTION ORACLE, AND IT VIOLATED THIS MILESTONE'S OWN SCOPE.
    11	 *
    12	 * Architecture §6 step 8 — written by the session that then broke it — says the
    13	 * tier **may touch** *"the issuer-view decryption tool's **output**, given a
    14	 * demo issuer key handled as the secret it is"* and **may never touch** *"any
    15	 * private key"*. `CLAUDE.md` says the issuer-view tool is *"a single CLI
    16	 * command … **Not a standalone application**."*
    17	 *
    18	 * The first version read `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK` from the environment and decrypted
    19	 * on demand. ⚠ **An unauthenticated `GET` where the caller chooses the account
    20	 * and receives a decrypted balance is an oracle** — CORS constrains browsers,
    21	 * not `curl`, and `/meta`'s `holdsKeys: false` was false while it ran. It is
    22	 * the **fourth rotation trigger `ADR-0003` names**, written by the same session
    23	 * one file over (`CLAIMS.md` E-212).
    24	 *
    25	 * ⚠ **And the helper it used passed the secret through `argv`** —
    26	 * `decryptInRange` spawns `mptool decrypt <ct> <sk> …`, and argv is
    27	 * world-readable via `ps`: the precise exposure the issuer CLI refuses a
    28	 * `--key` flag to avoid.
    29	 *
    30	 * # What it is now
    31	 *
    32	 * **A file reader.** `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` holds the key, decrypts the
    33	 * curated demo set in-process via `@xrplf/mpt-crypto`, and writes results. This
    34	 * module reads those results. ⚠ **There is no key here, no decryption, no
    35	 * subprocess and no attacker-chosen input** — the oracle is *removed*, not
    36	 * guarded, so there is nothing to authenticate.
    37	 */
    38	
    39	import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
    40	
    41	/**
    42	 * ⚠ **Named here so `check-web-readonly` can assert the web tier never READS
    43	 * it.** The variable belongs to the CLI tool; this constant exists to be
    44	 * checked against, not to be consulted.
    45	 */
    46	export const FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV = "WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK";
    47	
    48	export const ISSUER_KEY_POLICY =
    49	  "This process holds NO issuer key, performs NO decryption, and DOES NOT SERVE a decrypted " +
    50	  "balance. The key lives only in tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts, which reads it from the " +
    51	  "environment — never a flag, because argv is world-readable via `ps` — decrypts a curated set " +
    52	  "in-process, and writes results to a file it creates with mode 0600. ⚠ This tier reads that file " +
    53	  "and forwards WHETHER a balance was decryptable, never the figure. " +
    54	  "⚠⚠ Serving the figure was a disclosure boundary, not a signing one: loopback is not uid-scoped, " +
    55	  "so an unauthenticated GET handed every local user a value the producing tool had just " +
    56	  "restricted to one (CLAIMS.md E-228, ruled and removed E-241). There is no request that reaches " +
    57	  "a decryption and none that returns one. The figure is in tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts, a CLI " +
    58	  "command, which is where CLAUDE.md says the issuer view belongs.";
    59	
    60	/** The ledger field the snapshot tool decrypts. Named once, asserted in tests. */
    61	export const MIRROR_FIELD_LEDGER_NAME = "IssuerEncryptedBalance" as const;
    62	
    63	/**
    64	 * ⚠⚠ **READ LAZILY, AND THAT IS A CORRECTNESS FIX RATHER THAN A STYLE ONE.**
    65	 *
    66	 * As a module-level `const` this bound at import time — so a test that set
    67	 * `WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT` afterwards was ignored, and a `await import()` handed
    68	 * back the **cached** module rather than re-reading it. The refusal test
    69	 * therefore pointed at the DEFAULT path, which is absent in a clean checkout,
    70	 * so `issuerView` returned unavailable for that reason and the test passed
    71	 * **whether or not the refusal existed**. ⚠ **Measured: deleting both refusal
    72	 * lines left all 46 tests green** (`CLAIMS.md` E-215).
    73	 *
    74	 * ⚠ **Fifth instance in this repository of a check built to catch false passes
    75	 * false-passing** (E-165, E-168, E-182, E-203, E-207) — and this one was
    76	 * written in the round that fixed a false pass.
    77	 */
    78	function snapshotPath(): string {
    79	  return process.env.WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT ?? "/tmp/wconf/issuer-view-snapshot.json";
    80	}
    81	
    82	export interface IssuerViewResult {
    83	  available: boolean;
    84	  reason?: string;
    85	  keyPolicy: string;
    86	  account: string;
    87	  issuanceId: string;
    88	  mirrorField?: string;
    89	  /**
    90	   * ⚠⚠ **`decrypted` IS NO LONGER SERVED — `CLAIMS.md` E-241, ruled 2026-09-05.**
    91	   *
    92	   * The snapshot still holds it; **this API does not forward it.** The tool that
    93	   * produces the snapshot writes it `mode 0o600`, and serving its contents over
    94	   * an unauthenticated loopback listener handed every local uid a value the
    95	   * project had just restricted to one — `REVIEW-036` U1, `CLAIMS.md` E-228.
    96	   *
    97	   * ⚠ **The remedy is E-212's own: REMOVE the capability rather than guard it.**
    98	   * There is no request that reaches a decryption **and none that returns one**,
    99	   * so there is still nothing to authenticate.
   100	   *
   101	   * ⚠ **What the view keeps is the point it was making.** That the issuer CAN
   102	   * read this balance is proved by `mirrorField` being present and populated on
   103	   * the ledger — P-9, which no configuration removes. **The plaintext integer
   104	   * demonstrated only that THIS PROJECT holds the demo issuer key**, which
   105	   * nobody doubted. The integer lives where `CLAUDE.md` says it belongs: in
   106	   * `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts`, a CLI command, not a served field.
   107	   */
   108	  decryptedWithheld: true;
   109	  /** ⚠ Whether the tool COULD decrypt it. The fact, not the figure. */
   110	  decryptable?: boolean;
   111	  searchedTo?: string;
   112	  publicAmount?: string;
   113	  /** ⚠ When the snapshot was taken. Balances move; a stale number is a lie by omission. */
   114	  snapshotAtUnix?: number;
   115	  snapshotAgeNote?: string;
   116	  whatThisProves: string;
   117	}
   118	
   119	interface Snapshot {
   120	  producedAtUnix?: number;
   121	  entries?: Record<string, any>;
   122	}
   123	
   124	function loadSnapshot(): Snapshot | null {
   125	  try {
   126	    const parsed = JSON.parse(readFileSync(snapshotPath(), "utf8"));
   127	    // ⚠ **A snapshot carrying anything key-shaped is REFUSED, not filtered.**
   128	    // The tool writes results only; a file that does not look like that is not
   129	    // a file this tier should be reading.
   130	    const raw = JSON.stringify(parsed);
   131	    // ⚠ Built from the constant, not inlined. A regex literal is not a string
   132	    // literal, so an inlined spelling survives the gate's comment/literal
   133	    // stripping and reads as a genuine secret reference — which is exactly what
   134	    // it did on the first run of part 4.
   135	    if (/"(sk|secret|privateKey|elgamal_sk|seed)"\s*:/i.test(raw)) return null;
   136	    if (new RegExp(`\\b${FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV}\\b`).test(raw)) return null;
   137	    return parsed;
   138	  } catch {
   139	    return null;
   140	  }
   141	}
   142	
   143	export function issuerView(account: string, issuanceId: string): IssuerViewResult {
   144	  const base = {
   145	    decryptedWithheld: true as const,
   146	    keyPolicy: ISSUER_KEY_POLICY,
   147	    account,
   148	    issuanceId,
   149	    whatThisProves:
   150	      "⚠ That the issuer can read this holder's balance, always. P-9 is not a limitation of this " +
   151	      "demo — the decryption mirror is part of the protocol and no configuration removes it. A " +
   152	      "holder is confidential from the PUBLIC, never from the ISSUER.",
   153	  };
   154	
   155	  const snap = loadSnapshot();
   156	  if (!snap?.entries) {
   157	    return {
   158	      ...base,
   159	      available: false,
   160	      reason:
   161	        `No issuer-view snapshot at ${snapshotPath()}. ⚠ This is a NAMED GAP in the view, not ` +
   162	        `evidence that the issuer cannot decrypt — P-9 says it can. Produce one with: ` +
   163	        `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK=<64 hex> bun run tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts`,
   164	    };
   165	  }
   166	
   167	  const entry = snap.entries[`${account}/${issuanceId}`];
   168	  if (!entry) {
   169	    return {
   170	      ...base,
   171	      available: false,
   172	      snapshotAtUnix: snap.producedAtUnix,
   173	      reason:
   174	        `This account is not in the snapshot. ⚠ The snapshot covers a CURATED demo set, not every ` +
   175	        `account — that bound is what removes the attacker-chosen input, and it is deliberate. ` +
   176	        `Add it to web/api/demo-accounts.ts and re-run the tool.`,
   177	    };
   178	  }
   179	
   180	  return {
   181	    ...base,
   182	    available: Boolean(entry.available),
   183	    reason: entry.reason,
   184	    mirrorField: entry.mirrorField,
   185	    // ⚠⚠ E-241: `entry.decrypted` is READ and deliberately NOT forwarded. Only
   186	    // whether it exists crosses the boundary, never its value.
   187	    decryptedWithheld: true,
   188	    decryptable: entry.decrypted !== undefined,
   189	    searchedTo: entry.searchedTo,
   190	    publicAmount: entry.publicAmount,
   191	    snapshotAtUnix: snap.producedAtUnix,
   192	    snapshotAgeNote:
   193	      "⚠ A SNAPSHOT, not a live read. Balances move; this is what the issuer saw when the tool ran.",
   194	  };
   195	}

    45	/**

    47	 * disclosure, not a crash.** A tier that cannot reach a node must say which
    48	 * node it could not reach; falling back to another one is the substitution
    49	 * `CLAUDE.md` forbids.

    51	let view: LedgerView | null = null;

    54	async function ledger(): Promise<LedgerView> {
    55	  if (view) return view;
    56	  try {
    57	    view = await LedgerView.open();

    59	    return view;
    60	  } catch (e) {
    61	    viewError = e instanceof LedgerRefusal ? `${e.code}: ${e.message}` : String(e);
    62	    throw e;
    63	  }
    64	}

   209	 * answered on this machine's LAN address. So `make web-api` was publishing a

   224	  console.log(`⚠ Bound to ${BIND_HOSTNAME} only — not reachable from another machine.`);
   225	  console.log(`⚠ Devnet only.`);

     1	/**
     2	 * The **only** Unix-domain-socket client in this repository's web tier.
     3	 *
     4	 * Architecture §6 step 8, decision D1 (`ADR-0003`): the web tier may send
     5	 * `Request::Status` and nothing else. `Pair` and `Prepare` are the requests
     6	 * that carry authority, and neither is constructible from anywhere under
     7	 * `web/`. This file is what `tools/check-web-readonly.ts` pins.
     8	 *
     9	 * # Why this file exists in `web/api/` and not in `web/app/`
    10	 *
    11	 * `signer/src/ipc.rs` is a Unix domain socket precisely so that *"a browser
    12	 * page cannot connect at all. No web API opens a Unix socket."* That property
    13	 * closes the XSS-to-spend-oracle path by construction. ⚠ **A server function
    14	 * in the browser application would reach around it in one line** — which is
    15	 * exactly what TanStack Start's server functions are for — so the socket lives
    16	 * on the far side of an HTTP hop that the browser cannot bypass, and the gate
    17	 * asserts that nothing under `web/app/` imports this module or names the path.
    18	 *
    19	 * # What `Status` actually returns, which is not what its comment used to say
    20	 *
    21	 * ⚠ Until 2026-08-28 `protocol.rs` documented this variant as a *"Public
    22	 * ledger read"*. **It reads no ledger.** `session.rs`'s handler returns the
    23	 * crate version, a hardcoded `keys_loaded`, and a note carrying the holder's
    24	 * address, the randomness ledger's generation id and its entry count
    25	 * (`CLAIMS.md` E-205). None of that is a secret.
    26	 *
    27	 * ⚠⚠ **BUT IT DOES EXCEED WHAT THE PROTOCOL PUBLISHES, AND THIS COMMENT SAID
    28	 * OTHERWISE UNTIL 2026-09-04 — `CLAIMS.md` E-239.** `signer/src/store.rs:753-756`
    29	 * commits a record *"on success **and** on any local failure before emission — a
    30	 * cancelled confirmation, a failed proof, an aborted attempt"*, so the **entry
    31	 * count includes attempts that never reached the ledger and no observer can
    32	 * reconstruct.** P-4 and P-11 leave *who transacts and how often* public; they
    33	 * do not publish this count, nor the local socket path this module also returns.
    34	 *
    35	 * ⚠ The retracted claim (E-230) surviving as a paraphrase — the shape the
    36	 * disclosure-phrase gate cannot catch.
    37	 */
    38	
    39	import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
    40	import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
    41	import { join } from "node:path";
    42	
    43	/** What the signer answers. Mirrors `Response::Status` in `protocol.rs`. */
    44	export interface SignerStatus {
    45	  version: string;
    46	  keysLoaded: boolean;
    47	  note: string;
    48	}
    49	
    50	/** Why a status read did not happen. A missing signer is not an error here. */
    51	export type SignerProbe =
    52	  | { reachable: true; status: SignerStatus; socketPath: string }
    53	  | { reachable: false; reason: string; socketPath: string };
    54	
    55	/**
    56	 * Where the signer puts its socket, mirroring `ipc.rs::default_socket_path`.
    57	 *
    58	 * ⚠ Mirrored, not shared — one is Rust and one is TypeScript, so this is two
    59	 * readings of one convention and it can drift. `signerSocketPath` is exported
    60	 * so a test can assert the shape rather than leaving the duplication silent.
    61	 */
    62	export const SIGNER_SOCKET_LEAF = "signer.sock";
    63	export const SIGNER_SOCKET_DIR_PREFIX = "wconf-signer-";
    64	
    65	/**
    66	 * Every directory the signer might have put its socket under, in order.
    67	 *
    68	 * ⚠⚠ **MIRRORING RUST'S CHOICE WAS TRIED AND IS THE WRONG APPROACH.** Round one
    69	 * replaced a hardcoded `/tmp` with `os.tmpdir()`, and round two showed that still
    70	 * diverges: **measured with `TMPDIR` unset and `TMP` set, Rust bound at
    71	 * `/var/folders/…/T` while Node's `tmpdir()` returned `TMP`.** Rust's Unix
    72	 * `std::env::temp_dir()` reads `TMPDIR` and then the Darwin per-user directory;
    73	 * Node's reads `TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP`, `/tmp`. **They are different algorithms
    74	 * and no amount of care makes one file predict the other** — that is two
    75	 * readings of one convention, which is this repository's most-recorded drift
    76	 * source (`CLAIMS.md` E-221).
    77	 *
    78	 * ⚠ **So this DISCOVERS rather than PREDICTS**: it tries each candidate and
    79	 * uses the first whose socket actually exists. When none does, it reports every
    80	 * path it looked at, which turns an invisible mismatch into a legible message.
    81	 */
    82	export function signerSocketCandidates(): string[] {
    83	  const dirs = [
    84	    process.env.XDG_RUNTIME_DIR,
    85	    process.env.TMPDIR,
    86	    tmpdir(),
    87	    "/tmp",
    88	  ].filter((d): d is string => Boolean(d));
    89	  const uid = process.getuid?.() ?? 0;
    90	  const seen = new Set<string>();
    91	  const out: string[] = [];
    92	  for (const d of dirs) {
    93	    const p = join(d, `${SIGNER_SOCKET_DIR_PREFIX}${uid}`, SIGNER_SOCKET_LEAF);
    94	    if (!seen.has(p)) { seen.add(p); out.push(p); }
    95	  }
    96	  return out;
    97	}
    98	
    99	/**
   100	 * The socket to talk to: an explicit override, else the first candidate that
   101	 * exists, else the first candidate (so the error names something concrete).
   102	 */
   103	export function signerSocketPath(): string {
   104	  const override = process.env.WCONF_SIGNER_SOCKET;
   105	  if (override) return override;
   106	  const candidates = signerSocketCandidates();
   107	  return candidates.find((p) => existsSync(p)) ?? candidates[0];
   108	}
   109	
   110	/**
   111	 * Ask the signer for its status. **The only request this tier can build.**
   112	 *
   113	 * There is deliberately no `pair()` and no `prepare()` here, and adding one is
   114	 * not a code-review question — it is the trust-boundary change `ADR-0003`
   115	 * names as a rotation trigger.
   116	 */
   117	export async function readSignerStatus(timeoutMs = 2000): Promise<SignerProbe> {
   118	  const socketPath = signerSocketPath();
   119	
   120	  // The one and only request body this tier constructs. `type: "status"` is
   121	  // `Request::Status` under `#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "snake_case")]`.
   122	  const request = { type: "status" as const };
   123	
   124	  try {
   125	    const reply = await ask(socketPath, request, timeoutMs);
   126	    if (reply.status !== "status") {
   127	      return { reachable: false, socketPath, reason: `signer answered "${reply.status}", not "status"` };
   128	    }
   129	    return {
   130	      reachable: true,
   131	      socketPath,
   132	      status: { version: reply.version, keysLoaded: reply.keys_loaded, note: reply.note },
   133	    };
   134	  } catch (e) {
   135	    // ⚠ An unreachable signer is a NAMED GAP, not a failure. The web tier is
   136	    // read-only, so every ledger view it renders is complete without one.
   137	    const tried = process.env.WCONF_SIGNER_SOCKET ? [socketPath] : signerSocketCandidates();
   138	    return {
   139	      reachable: false,
   140	      socketPath,
   141	      // ⚠ Naming every path looked at is what makes a default-path mismatch
   142	      // legible instead of silent — round one's bug was invisible for exactly
   143	      // this reason (E-220, E-221).
   144	      reason: `${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)} (looked at: ${tried.join(", ")})`,
   145	    };
   146	  }
   147	}
   148	
   149	/**
   150	 * ⚠ **The timeout must CLOSE THE SOCKET, not merely stop waiting.**
   151	 *
   152	 * The first version raced `ask()` against a timer and left `ask` running: its
   153	 * `finally { sock.end() }` only fires when the inner promise settles, so a
   154	 * signer that accepts the connection and never writes a newline leaves the
   155	 * socket open **forever**. Repeated `/api/signer` polls then leak one file
   156	 * descriptor each (`CLAIMS.md` E-213). The deadline now lives *inside* `ask`,
   157	 * where it can reject the pending promise and let `finally` run.
   158	 *
   159	 * ⚠ **The falsifier is not a file-descriptor count, it is TERMINATION.**
   160	 * Measured 2026-08-28 against a fake signer that accepts the connection and
   161	 * never writes: the racing version leaves `answered` pending forever, so the
   162	 * process **never exits** — a 12-probe script had to be killed after five
   163	 * minutes. The same script against this version completes in under a second.
   164	 */
   165	async function ask(socketPath: string, request: { type: "status" }, timeoutMs: number): Promise<any> {
   166	  let resolve!: (v: any) => void;
   167	  let reject!: (e: Error) => void;
   168	  const answered = new Promise<any>((res, rej) => { resolve = res; reject = rej; });
   169	
   170	  const deadline = setTimeout(() => reject(new Error(`no answer in ${timeoutMs}ms`)), timeoutMs);
   171	  // ⚠⚠ **THE ORPHANED PROMISE MUST NOT BECOME AN UNHANDLED REJECTION.** When
   172	  // `Bun.connect` rejects — the ordinary case, because the signer is OPTIONAL —
   173	  // nothing ever awaits `answered`, and the still-armed deadline then rejects
   174	  // it into the void. Bun reports that as an unhandled rejection and exits
   175	  // non-zero. **Round one's fix moved the deadline inside `ask` and left the
   176	  // CONNECT outside the cleanup scope, so it fixed the hang and created this**
   177	  // (`CLAIMS.md` E-215). The no-op catch makes the promise handled from birth;
   178	  // the `finally` below clears the timer on every path including that one.
   179	  answered.catch(() => {});
   180	
   181	  let buffered = "";
   182	  let sock: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof Bun.connect>> | undefined;
   183	  try {
   184	    sock = await Bun.connect({
   185	    unix: socketPath,
   186	      socket: {
   187	        data(_s, chunk) {
   188	          buffered += chunk.toString();
   189	          const line = buffered.indexOf("\n");
   190	          if (line >= 0) {
   191	            try { resolve(JSON.parse(buffered.slice(0, line))); }
   192	            catch (e) { reject(new Error(`signer sent malformed JSON: ${e}`)); }
   193	          }
   194	        },
   195	        error(_s, e) { reject(e); },
   196	        close() { reject(new Error("the signer closed the connection without answering")); },
   197	      },
   198	    });
   199	
   200	    sock.write(JSON.stringify(request) + "\n");
   201	    // ⚠ `finally` runs on the timeout too, because the deadline rejects the
   202	    // promise this `await` is on rather than racing a separate one.
   203	    return await answered;
   204	  } finally {
   205	    // ⚠ **Connect is INSIDE this scope now.** Round one's version started the
   206	    // try/finally after `await Bun.connect`, so a connect failure skipped the
   207	    // cleanup entirely and left the timer armed.
   208	    clearTimeout(deadline);
   209	    sock?.end();
   210	  }
   211	}

     1	/**
     2	 * `issuer-view-snapshot` — decrypt the demo set ONCE, to a file the web tier reads.
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠⚠ **THIS EXISTS BECAUSE THE WEB TIER HELD THE ISSUER KEY AND THAT VIOLATED
     5	 * ITS OWN SCOPE.** Architecture §6 step 8, written by the session that then
     6	 * broke it, says the tier **may touch** *"the issuer-view decryption tool's
     7	 * **output**, given a demo issuer key handled as the secret it is"* and **may
     8	 * never touch** *"any private key"*. `CLAUDE.md` is equally plain: the
     9	 * issuer-view tool is *"a single CLI command … **Not a standalone
    10	 * application**."*
    11	 *
    12	 * The first implementation put `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK` in the API process and
    13	 * decrypted per request. ⚠ **That is a decryption oracle**: an unauthenticated
    14	 * `GET` where the caller chooses the account and receives a decrypted balance —
    15	 * **the exact thing `ADR-0003` names as its fourth rotation trigger, written by
    16	 * the same session, one file over** (`CLAIMS.md` E-212).
    17	 *
    18	 * # What this is instead
    19	 *
    20	 * A CLI. It holds the key, decrypts the **curated demo set**, and writes a JSON
    21	 * file of *results*. The web tier reads that file and **never sees a key**.
    22	 *
    23	 * ⚠ **The attacker-chosen input is gone, not merely guarded.** There is no
    24	 * request that reaches a decryption, so there is nothing to authenticate.
    25	 *
    26	 * # Why `decryptAmount` and not `mptool`
    27	 *
    28	 * ⚠ **`facility/src/mptool.ts::decryptInRange` passes the secret as a
    29	 * COMMAND-LINE ARGUMENT** — `Bun.spawnSync([tool, "decrypt", ct, sk, …])` — and
    30	 * argv is world-readable via `ps`. **That is the precise exposure the issuer
    31	 * tool refuses a `--key` flag to avoid**, reintroduced by the helper chosen to
    32	 * avoid a different problem. `@xrplf/mpt-crypto`'s `decryptAmount` is
    33	 * **in-process**: no subprocess, no argv, and it is what
    34	 * `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts` already uses.
    35	 *
    36	 * # Usage
    37	 *
    38	 *   WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK=<64 hex> bun run tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts [--max N] [--out PATH]
    39	 *
    40	 * ⚠ **No `--key` flag, on purpose**, matching the tool this one stands beside.
    41	 */
    42	
    43	import { decryptAmount } from "@xrplf/mpt-crypto";
    44	import { writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
    45	import { DEMO_ACCOUNTS, DEMO_ISSUANCE } from "../web/api/demo-accounts.ts";

    47	const DEVNET_RPC = process.env.WCONF_LEDGER_ENDPOINT ?? "https://s.devnet.rippletest.net:51234";
    48	const DEVNET_NETWORK_ID = 2;
    49	/**
    50	 * Strict argument parsing.
    51	 *
    52	 * ⚠ **A mistyped `--out` silently used the default and wrote decrypted results
    53	 * somewhere the operator did not choose**; `--out` with no value did the same.
    54	 * For a tool that decrypts confidential balances, a typo must stop it rather
    55	 * than redirect its output (`CLAIMS.md` E-220).
    56	 */
    57	const KNOWN_FLAGS = new Set(["--out", "--max"]);
    58	
    59	/** An argument reduced to its option name. ⚠ Anything after `=` is dropped. */
    60	export function redact(arg: string): string {
    61	  const eq = arg.indexOf("=");
    62	  if (eq >= 0) return `${arg.slice(0, eq)}=<redacted>`;
    63	  // A bare value that looks like key material must not be echoed either.
    64	  if (/^[0-9A-Fa-f]{32,}$/.test(arg)) return "<redacted>";
    65	  return arg;
    66	}
    67	const FLAGS: Record<string, string> = (() => {
    68	  const argv = process.argv.slice(2);
    69	  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
    70	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
    71	    const a = argv[i];
    72	    // ⚠⚠ **NEVER ECHO THE VALUE.** The obvious misuse of a tool that
    73	    // deliberately has no key flag is `--key=<64 hex>`, and the first version
    74	    // wrote the whole argument to stderr — turning transient argv exposure into
    75	    // a persistent CI or service log (`CLAIMS.md` E-221). Only the option NAME
    76	    // is ever printed, and anything after `=` is dropped.
    77	    const shown = redact(a);
    78	    if (!a.startsWith("--")) {
    79	      console.error(`unexpected argument: ${shown}`);
    80	      process.exit(2);
    81	    }
    82	    if (!KNOWN_FLAGS.has(a.split("=")[0])) {
    83	      console.error(`unknown flag: ${shown}. Known flags: ${[...KNOWN_FLAGS].join(", ")}`);
    84	      process.exit(2);
    85	    }
    86	    const v = argv[i + 1];
    87	    if (v === undefined || v.startsWith("--")) {
    88	      console.error(`${a.split("=")[0]} requires a value`);
    89	      process.exit(2);
    90	    }
    91	    if (a in out) { console.error(`${a.split("=")[0]} given twice`); process.exit(2); }
    92	    out[a] = v;
    93	    i++;
    94	  }
    95	  return out;
    96	})();

    98	function flag(name: string): string | undefined {
    99	  return FLAGS[name];
   100	}

   106	/**
   107	 * A UINT64 as a **decimal string**, never a JS `Number`.
   108	 *
   109	 * ⚠⚠ **`Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` is 9007199254740991; a UINT64 reaches
   110	 * 18446744073709551615.** `MPTAmount`, `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` and a
   111	 * decrypted balance are all UINT64, and rippled serialises them as JSON
   112	 * **strings** for exactly this reason — so converting them to `Number` on the
   113	 * way into the snapshot silently rounds any value above 2^53 and the issuer
   114	 * page then displays a wrong balance with no indication anything happened
   115	 * (`CLAIMS.md` E-215).
   116	 *
   117	 * ⚠ **The demo's values are small, which is precisely why this would not have
   118	 * been noticed.** Kept lossless end to end — snapshot, API and UI all carry the
   119	 * string.
   120	 */
   121	function dec(v: unknown): string {
   122	  if (v === undefined || v === null) return "0";
   123	  if (typeof v === "bigint") return v.toString();
   124	  return String(v);
   125	}
   126	
   127	async function rpc(method: string, params: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<any> {
   128	  // ⚠⚠ **`redirect: "error"`, because a followed redirect is a SUBSTITUTED
   129	  // SOURCE that the snapshot would then record as the configured endpoint.**
   130	  // `fetch` follows 307/308 by default, so a redirecting endpoint could serve
   131	  // ledger data from another origin while the file said otherwise — exactly the
   132	  // substitution `CLAUDE.md` forbids, in the tool whose whole output is a claim
   133	  // about what a ledger said (`CLAIMS.md` E-220).
   134	  const r = await fetch(DEVNET_RPC, {
   135	    method: "POST",
   136	    redirect: "error",
   137	    headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
   138	    body: JSON.stringify({ method, params: [params] }),
   139	  });
   140	  // ⚠ Belt and braces: even with redirects refused, assert the origin answered.
   141	  if (new URL(r.url).origin !== new URL(DEVNET_RPC).origin) {
   142	    throw new Error(
   143	      `${method} was answered by ${new URL(r.url).origin}, not the configured ` +
   144	      `${new URL(DEVNET_RPC).origin}. A substituted source is a corrupted one.`,
   145	    );
   146	  }
   147	  if (!r.ok) throw new Error(`${method} answered ${r.status}`);
   148	  const j = await r.json();
   149	  if (j.result?.error) throw new Error(`${method}: ${j.result.error_message ?? j.result.error}`);
   150	  return j.result;
   151	}
   152	
   153	/**
   154	 * A read PINNED to one validated index, and CHECKED to have come from it.
   155	 *
   156	 * ⚠⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` E-103, observed live: two `"validated"` reads seconds apart
   157	 * land in DIFFERENT ledgers**, because the public endpoint is a cluster —
   158	 * `account_objects` at 4596692 and `account_info` at 4596693, back to back. **A
   159	 * snapshot assembled from both is a state that never existed.**
   160	 *
   161	 * ⚠ The first version of this tool resolved `"validated"` **per account, in a
   162	 * loop, with an ElGamal discrete-log search between each one** — so the window
   163	 * was seconds wide and the output was labelled as one snapshot. `Snapshot` in
   164	 * `facility/src/loopback.ts` has done this correctly since step 7; this tool
   165	 * did not compose it and re-made the mistake the project had already paid for.
   166	 */
   167	async function pinned(index: number, method: string, params: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<any> {
   168	  const result = await rpc(method, { ...params, ledger_index: index });
   169	  const answered = Number(result.ledger_index ?? result.ledger_current_index ?? NaN);
   170	  if (answered !== index) {
   171	    throw new Error(
   172	      `${method} answered from ledger ${answered}, not the pinned ${index}. ` +
   173	      `A snapshot assembled from two ledgers is a state that never existed (CLAIMS.md E-103).`,
   174	    );
   175	  }
   176	  return result;
   177	}
   178

   180	if (!key) {
   292	  // --- randomness reuse ----------------------------------------------------

   294	  // when it happens the DIFFERENCE between two hidden amounts falls out.
   295	  const allTx = (await rpc({ command: "account_tx", account, limit: 200, ledger_index_min: -1, ledger_index_max: -1 })).result?.transactions ?? [];
   296	  const reused = findReusedR(allTx);
   297	  let leaked = 0;

   300	  console.log(`  scanned ${allTx.length} transactions. C1 = r*G is public, so this needs no key.`);
   301	  if (reused.length === 0) {
   302	    console.log(`  no C1 appears in more than one transaction. Nothing recoverable this way.`);
   303	    console.log(`  (C1 repeating WITHIN one Send is by design — see CLAIMS.md E-4/F-13 — and is not reuse.)`);
   304	  } else {
   305	    console.log(`  *** ${reused.length} reused C1 value(s) found. This is a signer defect. ***`);
   306	    for (const { c1, entries } of reused) {
   307	      const hashes = [...new Set(entries.map((e) => e.hash))];
   308	      console.log(`\n  C1 ${c1.slice(0, 32)}…  in ${hashes.length} transactions:`);
   309	      for (const h of hashes) console.log(`    ${h}`);
   310	      // Subtract same-field ciphertexts from two different transactions: the
   311	      // r*pk term cancels and (m - m')*G remains.
   312	      for (const f of TX_ELGAMAL_FIELDS) {
   313	        const inField = entries.filter((e) => e.field === f);
   314	        if (inField.length < 2) continue;
   315	        const [a1, b1] = inField;
   316	        if (a1.hash === b1.hash) continue;

   319	          const Q = secp256k1.ProjectivePoint.fromHex(C2of(b1.ct));
   320	          const d = smallDlog(P.subtract(Q), 100_000);
   321	          if (d !== null) {
   322	            leaked++;
   323	            console.log(`    ${f}: the two hidden amounts differ by ${d} — recovered with no key`);
   324	          } else {
   325	            console.log(`    ${f}: difference exceeds the +/-100,000 search range`);
   326	          }
   327	        } catch { /* unparseable point; skip rather than guess */ }
   328	      }
   329	    }
   330	    console.log(`\n  What this does and does not show: it recovers the DIFFERENCE between two`);
   331	    console.log(`  amounts, not either amount, and it requires the signer to have reused r.`);
   332	    console.log(`  It is not a break of XLS-96 as specified. rippled accepts reused r — there is`);
   333	    console.log(`  no ledger-side defence — so this is entirely the signer's responsibility.`);
   334	    console.log(`  See CLAIMS.md E-63 and issue #3.`);
   335	  }
   336	
   337	  // --- what confidentiality does not cover ---------------------------------
   338	  const txs = await rpc({ command: "account_tx", account, limit: 200, ledger_index_min: -1, ledger_index_max: -1 });
   339	  const conf = (txs.result?.transactions ?? []).filter((t: any) => String((t.tx_json ?? t.tx)?.TransactionType ?? "").startsWith("ConfidentialMPT"));
   340	  console.log(`\n## What confidentiality does NOT cover — ${conf.length} confidential transactions visible`);

   345	    console.log(`  ${when.padEnd(22)} ${String(tx.TransactionType).padEnd(28)} ${String(tx.Destination ?? "—").padEnd(36)} ${tx.DestinationTag ?? ""}`);
   346	  }
   347	  console.log(`\n  Every row above is public: who transacted, with whom, when, how often, and any`);
     1	/**
     2	 * The custody demo mode — architecture §6 step 8, decision D4.
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠⚠ **THIS IS A SIMULATION AND EVERY VALUE IT RETURNS SAYS SO.** There is no
     5	 * custody-platform integration behind it, no MPC and no HSM. It models a
     6	 * **key-source shape** — a standalone ElGamal key plus an external signer — and
     7	 * walks each flow to report which survive and which break, and why.
     8	 *
     9	 * ⚠ `CLAIMS.md` **P-5** forbids claiming more than is delivered, and
    10	 * *"custody-compatible"* over a stub is exactly that kind of claim.
    11	 * `disclosure.test.ts` fails if any surface stops saying this, and that test is
    12	 * verified by breaking it.
    13	 *
    14	 * **What makes it more than a mock:** the verdicts are not hand-written per
    15	 * flow. Each flow declares which *capabilities* it needs, and the verdict is
    16	 * computed from what a custody-shaped key source actually has — so a flow added
    17	 * later cannot be quietly marked "works".
    18	 */
    19	
    20	/** What a step of a flow requires. The only inputs a verdict is computed from. */
    21	export type Requirement =
    22	  | "xrpl-signature"        // the account key signs. A custody platform does this.
    23	  | "elgamal-decrypt"       // read own confidential balance.
    24	  | "elgamal-prove"         // produce the ZK proof over hidden values.
    25	  | "policy-over-amount"    // an approval rule that reads the transferred value.
    26	  | "policy-over-identity"  // an approval rule over destination, issuance or time.
    27	  | "seed-material";        // the raw master seed must be available.
    28	
    29	/**
    30	 * What a **custody-shaped** key source has, and what it structurally cannot.
    31	 *
    32	 * ⚠ Derived from the two findings in `docs/research/CUSTODY-2026-08-28.md`, not
    33	 * from a wish-list: seed material never leaves the platform (that is the
    34	 * product), and an approval engine sees a proof rather than a value.
    35	 */
    36	export const CUSTODY_SHAPE: Record<Requirement, { available: boolean; because: string }> = {
    37	  "xrpl-signature": {
    38	    available: true,
    39	    because: "Signing is what a custody platform is for. The account key never leaves its boundary.",
    40	  },
    41	  "elgamal-decrypt": {
    42	    available: true,
    43	    because:
    44	      "⚠ Only via a STANDALONE ElGamal key, which relocates the problem rather than solving it — " +
    45	      "something outside the custody boundary now holds a permanent decryption credential for every " +
    46	      "balance this account has ever held. Who that is has had no review (CLAIMS.md E-117).",
    47	  },
    48	  "elgamal-prove": {
    49	    available: true,
    50	    because: "Same standalone key, same relocated problem. Proving needs the same secret decrypting does.",
    51	  },
    52	  "policy-over-amount": {
    53	    available: false,
    54	    because:
    55	      "⚠ STRUCTURAL. A confidential transaction carries a proof, not a value. 'Require two approvers " +
    56	      "above $1M' has no input to read — the amount is exactly what has been hidden, and it is hidden " +
    57	      "from the policy engine too.",
    58	  },
    59	  "policy-over-identity": {
    60	    available: true,
    61	    because:
    62	      "Destination, issuance and timing are public (CLAIMS.md P-4, P-11). Every policy over an " +
    63	      "identity, a time or a count survives; every policy over a value does not.",
    64	  },
    65	  "seed-material": {
    66	    available: false,
    67	    because:
    68	      "⚠ STRUCTURAL. Never releasing seed material is the entire purpose of a custody platform. So a " +
    69	      "custodied account cannot perform the ruled key derivation and cannot derive its own viewing key.",
    70	  },
    71	};
    72	
    73	export interface Flow {
    74	  id: string;
    75	  label: string;
    76	  requires: Requirement[];
    77	}
    78	
    79	export const FLOWS: Flow[] = [
    80	  { id: "public-payment", label: "Send a public XRPL payment", requires: ["xrpl-signature"] },
    81	  { id: "onboard-derived", label: "Onboard with the ruled derived key", requires: ["seed-material", "xrpl-signature"] },
    82	  { id: "onboard-standalone", label: "Onboard with a standalone ElGamal key", requires: ["xrpl-signature", "elgamal-decrypt"] },
    83	  { id: "read-own-balance", label: "Read own confidential balance", requires: ["elgamal-decrypt"] },
    84	  { id: "confidential-send", label: "Send a confidential amount", requires: ["elgamal-decrypt", "elgamal-prove", "xrpl-signature"] },
    85	  { id: "confidential-send-capped", label: "Send confidentially, under an amount-capped approval policy", requires: ["elgamal-decrypt", "elgamal-prove", "xrpl-signature", "policy-over-amount"] },
    86	  { id: "confidential-send-allowlist", label: "Send confidentially, under a destination-allowlist policy", requires: ["elgamal-decrypt", "elgamal-prove", "xrpl-signature", "policy-over-identity"] },
    87	  { id: "retire-tranches", label: "Retire a position in tranches", requires: ["elgamal-decrypt", "elgamal-prove", "xrpl-signature"] },
    88	];
    89	
    90	export interface FlowVerdict {
    91	  id: string;
    92	  label: string;
    93	  survives: boolean;
    94	  blockedBy: { requirement: Requirement; because: string }[];
    95	  caveats: string[];
    96	}
    97	
    98	/** ⚠ Computed, never hand-written per flow. */
    99	export function walkFlows(): { simulation: true; disclaimer: string; flows: FlowVerdict[] } {
   100	  const flows = FLOWS.map((f) => {
   101	    const blockedBy = f.requires
   102	      .filter((r) => !CUSTODY_SHAPE[r].available)
   103	      .map((r) => ({ requirement: r, because: CUSTODY_SHAPE[r].because }));
   104	    const caveats = f.requires
   105	      .filter((r) => CUSTODY_SHAPE[r].available && CUSTODY_SHAPE[r].because.includes("⚠"))
   106	      .map((r) => CUSTODY_SHAPE[r].because);
   107	    if (f.id === "retire-tranches") {
   108	      caveats.push(
   109	        "⚠ Every tranche size is published. MPTAmount is plaintext on each ConvertBack, so retiring " +
   110	        "in tranches discloses each amount and its timing even though the balance was hidden (CLAIMS.md F-24).",
   111	      );
   112	    }
   113	    return { id: f.id, label: f.label, survives: blockedBy.length === 0, blockedBy, caveats };
   114	  });
   115	
   116	  return {
   117	    simulation: true,
   118	    disclaimer:
   119	      "⚠ SIMULATION. There is no custody-platform integration behind this, no MPC and no HSM. What is " +
   120	      "modelled is a key-source shape — a standalone ElGamal key plus an external signer — so that the " +
   121	      "flows which survive and the flows which break can be shown rather than described. " +
   122	      "See docs/research/CUSTODY-2026-08-28.md.",
   123	    flows,
   124	  };
   125	}

    56	          {view?.keyPolicy ??

    12	  to: "/observer" | "/issuer";

    14	  current: { account: string; issuanceId: string };
    15	}) {
    16	  const navigate = useNavigate();
    17

    19	    <div className="mb-4">
    20	      <div className="text-xs uppercase tracking-wide opacity-60 mb-2">Known Devnet accounts</div>

    22	        {accounts.map((a) => {
    23	          const active = a.address === current.account;
    24	          return (
    25	            <button
    26	              key={a.address}
    27	              onClick={() => navigate({ to, search: { account: a.address, issuanceId: a.issuanceId } })}
    28	              className={`text-left border rounded p-3 text-sm transition ${
    29	                active
    30	                  ? "border-[var(--color-hidden)] bg-black/[0.03] dark:bg-white/[0.06]"

    32	              }`}
    33	            >
    34	              <div className="font-medium">{a.label}</div>
    35	              <code className="text-[10px] opacity-60 break-all">{a.address}</code>
    36	              <p className="text-xs opacity-75 mt-1">{a.note}</p>
    37	            </button>
    38	          );
    39	        })}

     3	const STYLE: Record<string, string> = {
     4	  yes: "bg-emerald-100 text-emerald-900 dark:bg-emerald-950 dark:text-emerald-200",
     5	  no: "bg-red-100 text-red-900 dark:bg-red-950 dark:text-red-200",
     6	  conditional: "bg-amber-100 text-amber-900 dark:bg-amber-950 dark:text-amber-200",
     7	  unverified: "bg-slate-200 text-slate-800 dark:bg-slate-800 dark:text-slate-200",
     8	};
     9	
    10	const LABEL: Record<string, string> = {
    11	  yes: "yes",
    12	  no: "no",
    13	  conditional: "conditional",
    14	  unverified: "⚠ unverified",
    15	};
    16	
    17	export function Verdict({ v }: { v: string }) {
    18	  return (
    19	    <span className={`inline-block rounded px-2 py-0.5 text-xs font-semibold ${STYLE[v] ?? STYLE.unverified}`}>
    20	      {LABEL[v] ?? v}
    21	    </span>
    22	  );
    23	}
    24	
    25	export function Panel({ title, tone = "plain", children }: { title: string; tone?: "plain" | "warn"; children: ReactNode }) {
    26	  const border = tone === "warn" ? "border-[var(--color-warn)]" : "border-black/10 dark:border-white/15";
    27	  return (
    28	    <section className={`border ${border} rounded-lg p-5 mb-6`}>
    29	      <h2 className="font-semibold mb-3">{title}</h2>
    30	      {children}
    31	    </section>
    32	  );
    33	}

     1	/**
     2	 * The capability matrix — architecture §6 step 8's spine.
     3	 *
     4	 * **What each key source can and cannot do, and why.** Every cell carries a
     5	 * citation, and `web/api/capability.test.ts` checks that each citation
     6	 * RESOLVES AND EXISTS — that the named file, register row, ADR or ruling is
     7	 * there.
     8	 *
     9	 * ⚠⚠ **IT DOES NOT CHECK SEMANTIC SUPPORT, AND THIS COMMENT SAID IT DID UNTIL
    10	 * 2026-09-04** (*"says what the cell claims"*, `CLAIMS.md` E-239). **A citation
    11	 * to an existing but irrelevant file passes.** That is the exact shape this
    12	 * project claims discipline against — a gate asserting more than it checks —
    13	 * and it was found by an adversarial leg asked for the worst overclaim in the
    14	 * repository.
    15	 *
    16	 * ⚠ **This is DERIVED from the signer's own types, not restated from a
    17	 * handover table.** `KeySource` in `signer/src/onboarding.rs:274` has exactly
    18	 * two variants — `DerivedFromMasterSeed` and `Standalone` — and an absent
    19	 * `elgamal_key_source` reads as `standalone` rather than being upgraded
    20	 * (`holder.rs:53`). The browser-wallet row is the one that does **not** exist
    21	 * in the code, and saying so is the point of the row.
    22	 *
    23	 * ⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` P-4, P-9 and P-11 govern every sentence here.** This system
    24	 * hides amounts. It does not hide who transacts, with whom, when or how often,
    25	 * and no configuration makes a holder private from the issuer.
    26	 */
    27	
    28	export type Verdict = "yes" | "no" | "conditional" | "unverified";
    29	
    30	export interface Capability {
    31	  verdict: Verdict;
    32	  /** One sentence. Rendered as-is; it is a disclosure surface. */
    33	  detail: string;
    34	  /** Where the claim comes from. A file, a ruling, or a `CLAIMS.md` row. */
    35	  citation: string;
    36	}

    73	 *
    74	 * ⚠ **It is excluded from the reasoning on purpose.** It is a version fact an

     5	export const Route = createFileRoute("/")({
     6	  loader: async () => {
     7	    // ⚠ Loaders read. There is no server function here and none may be added
     8	    // that reaches a signing operation — ADR-0003 D3 names that as exactly the
     9	    // one-line mechanism to watch for.
    10	    // ⚠ Identity is asserted in the ROOT route's beforeLoad, so it covers every
    11	    // route rather than this one.
    12	    const [meta, signer] = await Promise.all([
    13	      api.meta().catch((e) => ({ error: String(e) })),
    14	      api.signer().catch((e) => ({ error: String(e) })),
    15	    ]);
    16	    return { meta, signer };
    17	  },
    18	  component: Overview,
    19	});
    20	
    21	function Overview() {
    22	  const { meta, signer } = Route.useLoaderData();
    23	
    24	  return (
    25	    <>
    26	      <Panel title="What this is">
    27	        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed mb-3">
    28	          A read-only view of confidential positions on the XRPL Devnet. It adds no{" "}
    29	          <strong>signing</strong> authority: it never signs, never holds a key, and cannot reach
    30	          the two signer requests that carry authority. It does not follow that it moves no
    31	          boundary — it used to serve a decrypted issuer view and signer-local status to any
    32	          local caller. ADR-0003 named that disclosure, and it was removed rather than guarded:
    33	          the issuer page says whether a balance decrypted and never shows the figure.
    34	        </p>
    35	        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed">
    36	          Signing lives in a separate binary — <code>wconf-signer</code> — where the holder confirms
    37	          each transaction against parsed fields in the signer’s own interface. That split is the
    38	          trust boundary, and this tier is on the readable side of it.
    39	        </p>
    40	      </Panel>
    41

    46	          limitations note beside it is what this demonstration does <em>not</em> establish.
    47	        </p>

    49	          <li><strong>Capability matrix</strong> — what each key source can and cannot do.</li>
    50	          <li><strong>Observer view</strong> — what anyone can read, including which zero balances are visible.</li>
    51	          <li><strong>Issuer view</strong> — the same holder, decrypted. This is the point.</li>
    52	          <li><strong>Wallet</strong> — three structural reasons a wallet cannot hold a position.</li>
    53	          <li><strong>Custody</strong> — where confidentiality and policy controls collide.</li>
    54	        </ol>

    15	function ApiDoc() {

    19	        <p className="text-sm mb-4">
    20	          There is no write path in this tier — not a disabled one, an absent one. The API cannot
    21	          pair with the signer, cannot prepare a transaction, and holds no key.
    22	        </p>
    23	        <p className="text-sm mb-4">
    24	          ⚠ It also performs no decryption. The issuer view reads results written by a command-line
    25	          tool that holds the key; an earlier version decrypted on request, which made this an
    26	          unauthenticated decryption oracle.
    27	        </p>

    30	            {ENDPOINTS.map(([e, d]) => (

    33	                <td className="py-2 opacity-80">{d}</td>
    34	              </tr>
    35	            ))}
    36	          </tbody>
    37	        </table>
    38	      </Panel>
    39	
    40	      <Panel title="How the boundary is enforced">
    41	        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed">
    42	          The signer speaks a Unix domain socket, which no browser API can open. Only the API
    43	          process may connect to it, and it may send one request type. A gate —
    44	          <code className="mx-1">tools/check-web-readonly.ts</code>— asserts that nothing in the
    45	          browser application names the socket, that the API constructs no request carrying
    46	          authority, and that no route or loader reaches a signing operation.
    47	        </p>
    48	        <p className="text-xs opacity-60 mt-3">
    49	          The gate is lexical and says so in its own output. It is a check over a named set, not a
    50	          proof.
    51	        </p>
    52	      </Panel>

     1	import { createRouter } from "@tanstack/react-router";

     4	/**
     5	 * ⚠ Named `getRouter` because `@tanstack/react-start` imports that symbol from

     9	export function getRouter() {

for (const origin of ['https://wconf-demo.vercel.app', 'null', 'https://example.com', 'https://wconf-demo.vercel.app']) {

     9	
    10	function Custody() {
    11	  const { caps, sim } = Route.useLoaderData();
    12	  const { rows } = caps;
    13	  const row = rows.find((r: any) => r.id === "custody-simulated");
    14	
    15	  return (
    16	    <>
    17	      <Panel title="⚠ This is a simulation" tone="warn">
    18	        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed">
    19	          There is no integration with any custody platform behind this page. No MPC, no HSM, no
    20	          Harmonize. What is modelled is a <strong>key-source shape</strong> — a standalone ElGamal
    21	          key plus an external signer — so that the flows which survive and the flows which break
    22	          can be shown rather than described.
    23	        </p>
    24	        <p className="text-xs opacity-60 mt-3">
    25	          CLAIMS.md P-5 forbids claiming more than is delivered. “Custody-compatible” over a stub is
    26	          exactly that kind of claim.
    27	        </p>
    28	      </Panel>
    29	
    30	      <Panel title="The two findings that matter for capital markets">
    31	        <div className="text-sm space-y-5">
    32	          <div>
    33	            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">1. The ruled key derivation is incompatible with custodial key management</h3>
    34	            <p className="opacity-85 leading-relaxed">
    35	              The ElGamal secret is derived from the master seed. A custody platform’s entire purpose
    36	              is never to release seed material. So a custodied account <strong>cannot derive its own
    37	              viewing key</strong> — it cannot read its own confidential balance.
    38	            </p>
    39	            <p className="opacity-85 leading-relaxed mt-2">
    40	              The escape is a standalone ElGamal key, which moves the question rather than answering
    41	              it: something now holds a permanent decryption credential outside the custody boundary.
    42	              That is an open, unreviewed item in this project.
    43	            </p>
    44	          </div>
    45	          <div>
    46	            <h3 className="font-semibold mb-1">2. A policy engine cannot enforce an amount limit on a confidential amount by reading it</h3>
    47	            <p className="opacity-85 leading-relaxed">
    48	              Approval workflows see a proof, not a value. <em>“Require two approvers above $1M”</em>
    49	              is unevaluable on a confidential send — the amount is exactly what has been hidden.
    50	            </p>
    51	            <p className="opacity-85 leading-relaxed mt-2">
    52	              This is structural, not an integration detail: confidentiality and policy-based custody
    53	              controls are in direct tension, and choosing one constrains the other.
    54	            </p>
    55	          </div>
    56	        </div>
    57	      </Panel>
    58	
    59	      <Panel title="What a custody-shaped key source can and cannot do">
    60	        <div className="text-sm space-y-3">
    61	          {[
    62	            ["Decrypt own balance", row.decryptOwnBalance],
    63	            ["Prove + sign confidential", row.proveAndSign],
    64	            ["Public transactions", row.publicTransactions],
    65	          ].map(([label, cap]: any) => (
    66	            <div key={label} className="flex gap-3 items-start">
    67	              <Verdict v={cap.verdict} />
    68	              <div>
    69	                <div className="font-medium">{label}</div>
    70	                <p className="opacity-80">{cap.detail}</p>
    71	              </div>
    72	            </div>
    73	          ))}
    74	        </div>
    75	      </Panel>
    76	
    77	      <Panel title="Which flows survive, and which break">
    78	        <p className="text-sm mb-4 opacity-80">
    79	          Each flow declares the capabilities it needs; the verdict is computed from what a
    80	          custody-shaped key source actually has. A flow added later cannot be quietly marked as
    81	          working.
    82	        </p>
    83	        <div className="space-y-3">
    84	          {sim.flows.map((f: any) => (
    85	            <div key={f.id} className="border-l-2 pl-3"
    86	                 style={{ borderColor: f.survives ? "var(--color-public)" : "var(--color-warn)" }}>

    96	                <p key={c} className="text-xs opacity-70 mt-1">{c}</p>
    97	              ))}
    98	            </div>
    99	          ))}
   100	        </div>
   101	      </Panel>
   102	
   103	      <Panel title="One more thing a custody client would reasonably assume, and should not">
   104	        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed">
   105	          Retiring a position in tranches publishes <strong>every tranche size</strong>. The amount
   106	          field on each exit transaction is plaintext, so the sizes and their timing are public even
   107	          though the balance was not.

   109	        <p className="text-xs opacity-60 mt-3">CLAIMS.md F-24.</p>

     5	export const Route = createFileRoute("/wallet")({
     6	  loader: () => api.capabilities(),
     7	  component: Wallet,

    10	/**
    11	 * Prove-and-contrast. ⚠ The contrast is the lesson: a browser wallet cannot
    12	 * hold a confidential position, and the reasons are structural rather than
    13	 * missing features.
    14	 */
    15	function Wallet() {
    16	  const { rows, namedGaps } = Route.useLoaderData();
    17	  const wallet = rows.find((r: any) => r.id === "browser-wallet");
    18	
    19	  return (
    20	    <>
    21	      <Panel title="A browser wallet cannot hold a confidential position">
    22	        <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed mb-4">
    23	          This is not a feature gap in the three wallet APIs examined (Xaman, GemWallet,
    24	          Crossmark, checked 2026-08-28). ⚠ It is <em>not</em> a claim that no wallet API
    25	          could ever close it — an extension that derived or held an ElGamal key internally,
    26	          and exposed a prove-and-sign operation without exporting seed material, would.
    27	          Nothing observed does. Three reasons, each structural to the design as it stands:
    28	        </p>
    29	        <ol className="text-sm space-y-3 list-decimal pl-5">
    30	          <li>
    31	            A confidential operation needs <strong>two</strong> secrets — the ElGamal secret, to
    32	            decrypt the balance and produce the proof, and the XRPL account key, to sign. A wallet
    33	            supplies the second.
    34	          </li>
    35	          <li>
    36	            Under the ruled key generation, the ElGamal secret is <strong>derived from the master
    37	            seed</strong>. There is no other way to obtain it for a derived-key account.
    38	          </li>
    39	          <li>
    40	            A wallet extension exists <strong>in order not to release seed material</strong>. That is
    41	            the product, not a limitation of its API.
    42	          </li>
    43	        </ol>
    44	        <p className="text-sm mt-4">
    45	          So the operation cannot be assembled at all. The wallet is not being denied permission —
    46	          there is nothing to grant.
    47	        </p>
    48	      </Panel>
    49	
    50	      <Panel title="What a wallet CAN do">
    51	        <div className="text-sm space-y-3">
    52	          <div className="flex gap-3 items-start">
    53	            <Verdict v={wallet.publicTransactions.verdict} />
    54	            <div>
    55	              <div className="font-medium">Ordinary XRPL transactions</div>
    56	              <p className="opacity-80">{wallet.publicTransactions.detail}</p>
    57	              <p className="text-xs opacity-50 mt-1">{wallet.publicTransactions.citation}</p>
    58	            </div>
    59	          </div>
    60	          <div className="flex gap-3 items-start">
    61	            <Verdict v="yes" />
    62	            <div>
    63	              <div className="font-medium">Prove control of an account</div>
    64	              <p className="opacity-80">
    65	                Which is enough to show that account’s observer view — the same view anyone with the
    66	                address can read.
    67	              </p>
    68	            </div>
    69	          </div>
    70	        </div>
    71	      </Panel>
    72	
    73	      <Panel title="What this page does not claim" tone="warn">
    74	        <p className="text-sm">{namedGaps.walletCodec}</p>
    75	        <p className="text-xs opacity-60 mt-3">
    76	          CLAIMS.md E-206. Recorded because an earlier draft of this project’s reasoning led with
    77	          exactly this point, and it is a version fact an upstream release could close at any time.
    78	          The three reasons above cannot be closed by any release.
    79	        </p>
    80	      </Panel>
    81	    </>
    82	  );
    83	}

     7	 * ⚠ **It asserts SIX things, and this header said *"five"* while listing FOUR

     9	 * and part 6 was added by `REVIEW-036`. **A gate's own description going stale

    11	 *
    12	 *   1. **Nothing under `web/app/` reaches the signer's Unix socket** — not by

    14	 *      socket itself.
    15	 *   2. **The Elysia process builds `Request::Status` and nothing else.** `Pair`
    16	 *      and `Prepare` are the requests that carry authority.

    19	 *      added after E-212, because parts 1-3 ask what the tier can REACH and an
    20	 *      issuer key held INSIDE the API process is a different property.
    21	 *   5. **There is exactly ONE socket client in the whole tier**, so the surface
    22	 *      the other parts reason about is one file rather than a set.
    23	 *   6. **Every bind resolves to a loopback literal** — the API's `.listen(`/
