    29	 * loopback is **not uid-scoped**, and that is E-228's open finding.
    30	 *
    31	 * ⚠ **Gates in this repository have been built to catch false passes and have
    32	 * false-passed** (`CLAIMS.md` E-165, E-168, E-182, E-198, E-203). This one is
    33	 * therefore built to the standard those rounds produced: every control runs
    34	 * through **the same function the real check calls**, every count is
    35	 * **incremented by the helper that runs it** rather than typed, and the
    36	 * residual it cannot close is printed in its own output by
    37	 * `whatThisDoesNotSay()` — as **asserted-uncaught controls**, so a gap that
    38	 * silently closes makes the gate FAIL rather than leaving a stale disclosure.
    39	 *
    40	 * # Why this does not share `check-zeroize-bounds.py`'s stripper
    41	 *
    42	 * ⚠ **Two measured reasons, because "write it again" is normally the wrong
    43	 * answer** (`CLAIMS.md` E-141: a second implementation of one obligation is a
    44	 * second thing to drift).
    45	 *
    46	 *   1. **Rust block comments nest and TypeScript's do not, and that stripper
    47	 *      counts depth.** Measured 2026-08-28: `console.log("A"); /* outer /*
    48	 *      inner *\/ console.log("B");` prints **both** under `bun run`, so TS ends
    49	 *      the comment at the first `*\/`. A depth-counting stripper keeps
    50	 *      consuming and swallows real code. ⚠ **Over-stripping is THIS gate's
    51	 *      unsafe direction** — stripped code cannot be found, so a violation
    52	 *      hides. That is E-203's "no safe direction" lesson landing on a third
    53	 *      consumer.
    54	 *   2. **That stripper BLANKS string literals; this one must PRESERVE them.**
    55	 *      The socket path and `type: "status"` *are* string literals. Blanking
    56	 *      them would blind this gate to the exact thing it exists to find.
    57	 *
    58	 * **What is shared is the discipline, not the code**: parse string literals so
    59	 * a `//` inside one does not open a comment, and keep their contents.

    67	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    68	// The stripper. Comments out, string literals IN.
    69	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    70	
    71	/**
    72	 * Blank `//` tails and `/* ... *\/` blocks, **preserving string literals**.
    73	 *
    74	 * ⚠ **A commented-out violation is not a violation** — that is why comments go
    75	 * (E-182: a gate that counts a needle anywhere false-passes on a commented-out
    76	 * line). ⚠ **A violation written as a string literal IS one** — that is why
    77	 * literals stay.
    78	 *
    79	 * Handles `'...'`, `"..."` with backslash escapes, and `` `...` `` template
    80	 * literals **including `${...}` interpolation**, whose contents are code and
    81	 * are scanned as code. Block comments do **not** nest, matching TypeScript.
    82	 */
    83	export function stripComments(text: string): string {
    84	  const out: string[] = [];
    85	  let i = 0;
    86	  while (i < text.length) {
    87	    const c = text[i];
    88	    const next = text[i + 1];
    89	
    90	    if (c === "/" && next === "/") {
    91	      // Blank to end of line, keeping the newline so line numbers survive.
    92	      while (i < text.length && text[i] !== "\n") { out.push(" "); i++; }
    93	      continue;
    94	    }
    95	    if (c === "/" && next === "*") {
    96	      // ⚠ First `*/` closes it. TypeScript does not nest — measured, and it is
    97	      // the reason this is not the Rust stripper.
    98	      const end = text.indexOf("*/", i + 2);
    99	      const stop = end < 0 ? text.length : end + 2;
   100	      for (let k = i; k < stop; k++) out.push(text[k] === "\n" ? "\n" : " ");
   101	      i = stop;
   102	      continue;
   103	    }
   104	    if (c === '"' || c === "'") {
   105	      const end = endOfQuoted(text, i, c);
   106	      out.push(text.slice(i, end));
   107	      i = end;
   108	      continue;
   109	    }
   110	    if (c === "`") {
   111	      const end = endOfTemplate(text, i);
   112	      out.push(text.slice(i, end));
   113	      i = end;
   114	      continue;
   115	    }
   116	    out.push(c);
   117	    i++;
   118	  }
   119	  return out.join("");
   120	}
   121	
   122	function endOfQuoted(text: string, start: number, quote: string): number {
   123	  let k = start + 1;
   124	  while (k < text.length) {
   125	    if (text[k] === "\\") { k += 2; continue; }
   126	    if (text[k] === quote) return k + 1;
   127	    if (text[k] === "\n") return k; // unterminated; do not run away
   128	    k++;
   129	  }
   130	  return text.length;
   131	}
   132	
   133	/**
   134	 * End of a template literal. `${...}` is CODE and is kept as code — a nested
   135	 * template inside an interpolation is handled by recursing on depth.
   136	 */
   137	function endOfTemplate(text: string, start: number): number {
   138	  let k = start + 1;
   139	  let depth = 0;
   140	  while (k < text.length) {
   141	    if (text[k] === "\\") { k += 2; continue; }
   142	    if (text[k] === "$" && text[k + 1] === "{") { depth++; k += 2; continue; }
   143	    // ⚠ **An ORDINARY `{` inside an interpolation must be counted, or its `}`
   144	    // closes the interpolation early.** `${fn({a: 1}) + signAndSubmit(i)}` was
   145	    // truncated at the object literal's brace, and everything after it —
   146	    // including the signing call — was treated as template text and blanked
   147	    // (`CLAIMS.md` E-220).
   148	    // ⚠ **A brace inside a STRING within the interpolation is not syntax.**
   149	    // Round one counted it, so `${format("{")}` never returned depth to zero
   150	    // and the rest of the file was treated as template text — a false FAILURE
   151	    // rather than a false pass, but the same class (E-221).
   152	    if (depth > 0 && (text[k] === '"' || text[k] === "'")) { k = endOfQuoted(text, k, text[k]); continue; }
   153	    if (depth > 0 && text[k] === "{") { depth++; k++; continue; }
   154	    if (depth > 0 && text[k] === "}") { depth--; k++; continue; }
   155	    if (depth === 0 && text[k] === "`") return k + 1;
   156	    k++;
   157	  }
   158	  return text.length;
   159	}
   160	
   161	/**
   162	 * Blank string-literal **contents**, keeping the quotes and the line shape.
   163	 *
   164	 * ⚠⚠ **THIS EXISTS BECAUSE THE GATE'S FIRST RUN AGAINST THE REAL TREE CAUGHT
   165	 * PROSE, AND THE FIX IS A POLARITY DISTINCTION RATHER THAN AN EXEMPTION.**
   166	 * `capability.ts` describes a wallet's published API in a sentence that
   167	 * contains the word `signAndSubmit`. That is a **mention**, not a call — but
   168	 * `"/run/wconf/signer.sock"` in a string literal IS a socket path, and
   169	 * blanking it would be this gate's false-pass direction.
   170	 *
   171	 * ⚠ **So the two needle classes need OPPOSITE treatment, and each gets the one
   172	 * it needs instead of one being argued into a "safe direction":**
   173	 *
   174	 *   * `SOCKET_NEEDLES` are **values** — paths, env names. Scanned with string
   175	 *     literals **PRESERVED**.
   176	 *   * `SIGNING_NEEDLES` are **identifiers** — function and property names.
   177	 *     Scanned with string literals **BLANKED**, so prose about someone else's
   178	 *     API is not a violation of ours.
   179	 *   * `AUTHORITY_NEEDLES` are the wire tags `"pair"` / `"prepare"` **including
   180	 *     their quotes**, so they are literal-shaped by construction and are
   181	 *     scanned with literals preserved.
   182	 *
   183	 * ⚠ **This is `CLAIMS.md` E-203 landing on a fourth consumer.** That row's
   184	 * lesson was that a "safe direction" argument is only as good as the list of
   185	 * consumers, and the list grew after the argument was written. Here the list
   186	 * was known in advance and the consumers were given different instruments.
   187	 * **The residual is named**: a signing call assembled from strings at runtime
   188	 * is invisible to the identifier pass, and that is `whatThisDoesNotSay()` item 1.
   189	 */
   190	export function stripStringLiterals(text: string): string {
   191	  const out: string[] = [];
   192	  let i = 0;
   193	  while (i < text.length) {
   194	    const c = text[i];
   195	    if (c === '"' || c === "'") {
   196	      const end = endOfQuoted(text, i, c);
   197	      out.push(c);
   198	      for (let k = i + 1; k < end - 1; k++) out.push(text[k] === "\n" ? "\n" : " ");
   199	      out.push(text[end - 1] === c ? c : " ");
   200	      i = end;
   201	      continue;
   202	    }
   203	    if (c === "`") {
   204	      const end = endOfTemplate(text, i);
   205	      // ⚠ `${...}` inside a template is CODE. Blanking the whole template would
   206	      // hide a real call written as `${signAndSubmit(x)}`, so the interpolations
   207	      // are kept and only the literal text between them is blanked.
   208	      out.push(blankTemplateText(text.slice(i, end)));
   209	      i = end;
   210	      continue;
   211	    }
   212	    out.push(c);
   213	    i++;
   214	  }
   215	  return out.join("");
   216	}
   217	
   218	function blankTemplateText(t: string): string {
   219	  const out: string[] = [];
   220	  let depth = 0;
   221	  for (let k = 0; k < t.length; k++) {
   222	    const c = t[k];
   223	    if (c === "$" && t[k + 1] === "{") { depth++; out.push("$", "{"); k++; continue; }
   224	    // ⚠ Same nested-brace correction as `endOfTemplate`. Without it an object
   225	    // literal inside an interpolation ends the interpolation early and the real
   226	    // code after it is blanked (E-220).
   227	    // ⚠ Same string-awareness as `endOfTemplate`; the two must agree or one
   228	    // will blank what the other kept.
   229	    if (depth > 0 && (c === '"' || c === "'")) {
   230	      const e = endOfQuoted(t, k, c);
   231	      out.push(t.slice(k, e));
   232	      k = e - 1;
   233	      continue;
   234	    }
   235	    if (depth > 0 && c === "{") { depth++; out.push("{"); continue; }
   236	    if (depth > 0 && c === "}") { depth--; out.push("}"); continue; }
   237	    if (depth > 0) { out.push(c); continue; }
   238	    out.push(c === "\n" ? "\n" : c === "`" ? "`" : " ");
   239	  }
   240	  return out.join("");
   241	}

   249	function walk(dir: string): string[] {
   250	  let found: string[] = [];
   251	  let entries: string[];
   252	  try { entries = readdirSync(dir); } catch { return []; }
   253	  for (const e of entries) {
   254	    if (e === "node_modules" || e === "dist" || e === ".output" || e === ".tanstack") continue;
   255	    const p = join(dir, e);
     1	/* eslint-disable max-params -- context-hash builders mirror the C ABI argument lists */
     2	import {
     3	  ACCOUNT_ID_SIZE,
     4	  CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE,
     5	  ISSUANCE_ID_SIZE,
     6	} from './constants'
     7	import { bytesToHex, hexToBytes } from './hex'
     8	import { withModule } from './runtime'
     9	
    10	/**
    11	 * Context hash bound to a ConfidentialMPTConvert transaction.
    12	 *
    13	 * @param account - The 20-byte hex AccountID of the converting holder.
    14	 * @param issuance - The 24-byte hex MPTokenIssuanceID.
    15	 * @param sequence - The transaction sequence number.
    16	 * @returns The 32-byte hex context hash.
    17	 * @throws If inputs are malformed or the WASM call fails.
    18	 */
    19	export async function getConvertContextHash(
    20	  account: string,
    21	  issuance: string,
    22	  sequence: number,
    23	): Promise<string> {
    24	  const acc = hexToBytes(account, 'account', ACCOUNT_ID_SIZE)
    25	  const iss = hexToBytes(issuance, 'issuance', ISSUANCE_ID_SIZE)
    26	  return withModule((mod, marshaller) => {
    27	    const accPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(acc)
    28	    const issPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(iss)
    29	    const outPtr = marshaller.alloc(CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE)
    30	    if (
    31	      mod._mpt_get_convert_context_hash(accPtr, issPtr, sequence, outPtr) !== 0
    32	    ) {
    33	      throw new Error('mpt_get_convert_context_hash failed')
    34	    }
    35	    return bytesToHex(marshaller.readBytes(outPtr, CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE))
    36	  })
    37	}

   118	/**
   119	 * Context hash bound to a ConfidentialMPTClawback transaction.
   120	 *

   122	 * @param issuance - The 24-byte hex MPTokenIssuanceID.
   123	 * @param sequence - The transaction sequence number.

   125	 * @returns The 32-byte hex context hash.

   128	export async function getClawbackContextHash(
   129	  account: string,
   130	  issuance: string,
   131	  sequence: number,
   132	  holder: string,
   133	): Promise<string> {
   134	  const acc = hexToBytes(account, 'account', ACCOUNT_ID_SIZE)
   135	  const iss = hexToBytes(issuance, 'issuance', ISSUANCE_ID_SIZE)
   136	  const hold = hexToBytes(holder, 'holder', ACCOUNT_ID_SIZE)
   137	  return withModule((mod, marshaller) => {
   138	    const accPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(acc)
   139	    const issPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(iss)
   140	    const holdPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(hold)
   141	    const outPtr = marshaller.alloc(CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE)
   142	    if (
   143	      mod._mpt_get_clawback_context_hash(
   144	        accPtr,
   145	        issPtr,
   146	        sequence,
   147	        holdPtr,
   148	        outPtr,
   149	      ) !== 0
   150	    ) {
   151	      throw new Error('mpt_get_clawback_context_hash failed')
   152	    }
   153	    return bytesToHex(marshaller.readBytes(outPtr, CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE))
   154	  })
   155	}
     1	/* eslint-disable max-params, max-lines-per-function -- proof builders mirror the C ABI */
     2	import {
     3	  BLINDING_FACTOR_SIZE,
     4	  CLAWBACK_PROOF_SIZE,
     5	  CONVERT_BACK_PROOF_SIZE,
     6	  CONVERT_PROOF_SIZE,
     7	  CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE,
     8	  ELGAMAL_TOTAL_SIZE,
     9	  PEDERSEN_COMMIT_SIZE,
    10	  PRIVKEY_SIZE,
    11	  PUBKEY_SIZE,
    12	  SEND_PROOF_SIZE,
    13	} from './constants'
    14	import { bytesToHex, hexToBytes } from './hex'

    19	const SIZE_T_BYTES = 4

    21	/**
    22	 * Generate the 64-byte Schnorr proof for a ConfidentialMPTConvert transaction.
    23	 *
    24	 * @param publicKey - The 33-byte hex public key.
    25	 * @param privateKey - The 32-byte hex private key.
    26	 * @param contextHash - The 32-byte hex transaction context hash.
    27	 * @returns The 64-byte hex proof.
    28	 * @throws If inputs are malformed or the WASM call fails.
    29	 */
    30	export async function getConvertProof(
    31	  publicKey: string,
    32	  privateKey: string,
    33	  contextHash: string,
    34	): Promise<string> {
    35	  const pub = hexToBytes(publicKey, 'publicKey', PUBKEY_SIZE)
    36	  const priv = hexToBytes(privateKey, 'privateKey', PRIVKEY_SIZE)
    37	  const ctx = hexToBytes(contextHash, 'contextHash', CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE)
    38	  return withModule((mod, marshaller) => {
    39	    const pubPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(pub)
    40	    const privPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(priv)
    41	    const ctxPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(ctx)
    42	    const outPtr = marshaller.alloc(CONVERT_PROOF_SIZE)
    43	    if (mod._mpt_get_convert_proof(pubPtr, privPtr, ctxPtr, outPtr) !== 0) {
    44	      throw new Error('mpt_get_convert_proof failed')
    45	    }
    46	    return bytesToHex(marshaller.readBytes(outPtr, CONVERT_PROOF_SIZE))
    47	  })
    48	}
    49	
    50	/**
    51	 * Generate the 64-byte sigma proof for a ConfidentialMPTClawback transaction.
    52	 *
    53	 * @param privateKey - The issuer's 32-byte hex private key.
    54	 * @param publicKey - The issuer's 33-byte hex public key.
    55	 * @param contextHash - The 32-byte hex transaction context hash.
    56	 * @param amount - The publicly known amount being clawed back.
    57	 * @param ciphertext - The holder's 66-byte hex balance ciphertext.
    58	 * @returns The 64-byte hex proof.
    59	 * @throws If inputs are malformed or the WASM call fails.
    60	 */
    61	export async function getClawbackProof(
    62	  privateKey: string,
    63	  publicKey: string,
    64	  contextHash: string,
    65	  amount: bigint,
    66	  ciphertext: string,
    67	): Promise<string> {
    68	  assertUint64(amount, 'amount')
    69	  const priv = hexToBytes(privateKey, 'privateKey', PRIVKEY_SIZE)
    70	  const pub = hexToBytes(publicKey, 'publicKey', PUBKEY_SIZE)
    71	  const ctx = hexToBytes(contextHash, 'contextHash', CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE)
    72	  const ct = hexToBytes(ciphertext, 'ciphertext', ELGAMAL_TOTAL_SIZE)
    73	  return withModule((mod, marshaller) => {
    74	    const privPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(priv)
    75	    const pubPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(pub)
    76	    const ctxPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(ctx)
    77	    const ctPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(ct)
    78	    const outPtr = marshaller.alloc(CLAWBACK_PROOF_SIZE)
    79	    if (
    80	      mod._mpt_get_clawback_proof(
    81	        privPtr,
    82	        pubPtr,
    83	        ctxPtr,
    84	        amount,
    85	        ctPtr,
    86	        outPtr,
    87	      ) !== 0
    88	    ) {
    89	      throw new Error('mpt_get_clawback_proof failed')
    90	    }
    91	    return bytesToHex(marshaller.readBytes(outPtr, CLAWBACK_PROOF_SIZE))
    92	  })
    93	}

    95	/**
    96	 * Generate the 816-byte proof for a ConfidentialMPTConvertBack transaction.
    97	 *
    98	 * @param privateKey - The holder's 32-byte hex private key.
    99	 * @param publicKey - The holder's 33-byte hex public key.
   100	 * @param contextHash - The 32-byte hex transaction context hash.
   101	 * @param amount - The publicly revealed conversion amount.
   102	 * @param params - The holder's balance Pedersen witness.
   103	 * @returns The 816-byte hex proof.
   104	 * @throws If inputs are malformed or the WASM call fails.
   105	 */
   106	export async function getConvertBackProof(
   107	  privateKey: string,
   108	  publicKey: string,
   109	  contextHash: string,
   110	  amount: bigint,
   111	  params: PedersenParams,
   112	): Promise<string> {
   113	  assertUint64(amount, 'amount')
   114	  const priv = hexToBytes(privateKey, 'privateKey', PRIVKEY_SIZE)
   115	  const pub = hexToBytes(publicKey, 'publicKey', PUBKEY_SIZE)
   116	  const ctx = hexToBytes(contextHash, 'contextHash', CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE)
   117	  const rawParams = rawPedersenParams(params, 'params')
   118	  return withModule((mod, marshaller) => {
   119	    const privPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(priv)
   120	    const pubPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(pub)
   121	    const ctxPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(ctx)
   122	    const paramsPtr = marshaller.allocPedersenParams(rawParams)
   123	    const outPtr = marshaller.alloc(CONVERT_BACK_PROOF_SIZE)
   124	    if (
   125	      mod._mpt_get_convert_back_proof(
   126	        privPtr,
   127	        pubPtr,
   128	        ctxPtr,
   129	        amount,
   130	        paramsPtr,
   131	        outPtr,
   132	      ) !== 0
   133	    ) {
   134	      throw new Error('mpt_get_convert_back_proof failed')
   135	    }
   136	    return bytesToHex(marshaller.readBytes(outPtr, CONVERT_BACK_PROOF_SIZE))
   137	  })
   138	}

   140	/**
   141	 * Generate the 946-byte proof for a ConfidentialMPTSend transaction.
   142	 *
   143	 * @param params - The send-proof inputs (sender keys, participants, witnesses).
   144	 * @returns The 946-byte hex proof.
   145	 * @throws If inputs are malformed or the WASM call fails.
   146	 */
   147	export async function getConfidentialSendProof(
   148	  params: SendProofParams,
   149	): Promise<string> {
   150	  assertUint64(params.amount, 'amount')
   151	  const priv = hexToBytes(params.privateKey, 'privateKey', PRIVKEY_SIZE)
   152	  const pub = hexToBytes(params.publicKey, 'publicKey', PUBKEY_SIZE)
   153	  const txBlinding = hexToBytes(
   154	    params.txBlindingFactor,
   155	    'txBlindingFactor',
   156	    BLINDING_FACTOR_SIZE,
   157	  )
   158	  const ctx = hexToBytes(params.contextHash, 'contextHash', CONTEXT_HASH_SIZE)
   159	  const amountCommitment = hexToBytes(
   160	    params.amountCommitment,
   161	    'amountCommitment',
   162	    PEDERSEN_COMMIT_SIZE,
   163	  )
   164	  const participants = params.participants.map((participant, index) =>
   165	    rawParticipant(participant, `participants[${index}]`),
   166	  )
   167	  const balanceParams = rawPedersenParams(params.balanceParams, 'balanceParams')
   168	  return withModule((mod, marshaller) => {
   169	    const privPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(priv)
   170	    const pubPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(pub)
   171	    const participantsPtr = marshaller.allocParticipants(participants)
   172	    const txBlindingPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(txBlinding)
   173	    const ctxPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(ctx)
   174	    const amountCommitmentPtr = marshaller.allocBytes(amountCommitment)
   175	    const balancePtr = marshaller.allocPedersenParams(balanceParams)
   176	    const outPtr = marshaller.alloc(SEND_PROOF_SIZE)
   177	    const outLenPtr = marshaller.alloc(SIZE_T_BYTES)
   178	    marshaller.writeU32(outLenPtr, SEND_PROOF_SIZE)
   179	    if (
   180	      mod._mpt_get_confidential_send_proof(
   181	        privPtr,
   182	        pubPtr,
   183	        params.amount,
   184	        participantsPtr,
   185	        participants.length,
   186	        txBlindingPtr,
   187	        ctxPtr,
   188	        amountCommitmentPtr,
   189	        balancePtr,
   190	        outPtr,

   262	interface Source {
   263	  path: string;
   264	  rel: string;
   265	  /** Comments blanked, string literals PRESERVED. For value-shaped needles. */
   266	  code: string;
   267	  /** Comments AND string-literal contents blanked. For identifier needles. */
   268	  ident: string;
   269	}
   270	
   271	function load(dir: string): Source[] {
   272	  return walk(join(REPO, dir)).map((p) => make(relative(REPO, p), readFileSync(p, "utf8"), p));
   273	}
   274	
   275	function make(rel: string, raw: string, path = rel): Source {
   276	  const code = stripComments(raw);
   277	  return { path, rel, code, ident: stripStringLiterals(code) };
   278	}

   305	/**
   306	 * ⚠⚠ **SECRETS. Added after the tier SHIPPED A DECRYPTION ORACLE.**
   307	 *
   308	 * `web/api/issuer.ts` read `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK` and decrypted per request over an
   309	 * unauthenticated `GET` — violating architecture §6 step 8's own *"may never
   310	 * touch any private key"*, written by the session that then broke it
   311	 * (`CLAIMS.md` E-212). ⚠ **The gate did not catch it because the gate was about
   312	 * REACHABILITY, not about what the tier HOLDS.** Those are different properties
   313	 * and this part is the second one.
   314	 *
   315	 * ⚠ `decryptInRange` is named because it spawns `mptool decrypt <ct> <sk>` and
   316	 * **argv is world-readable via `ps`** — the exposure the issuer CLI refuses a
   317	 * `--key` flag to avoid.
   318	 */
   319	/**
   320	 * Environment variables that must never be READ by this tier.
   321	 *
   322	 * ⚠⚠ **Detected STRUCTURALLY, not by name in a needle list, because the name is
   323	 * a string and the identifier pass blanks strings.** `process.env["WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK"]`
   324	 * passed part 4 cleanly: the ident pass erased the literal before the search ran
   325	 * (`CLAIMS.md` E-220). ⚠ **And the name cannot simply be moved to the
   326	 * literal-preserving pass either** — `issuer.ts` legitimately declares
   327	 * `FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV = "WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK"` in order to forbid it, so a
   328	 * name-only match would flag the guard as the violation. **What is forbidden is
   329	 * the ACCESS, so the access is what is matched.**
   330	 */
   331	export const SECRET_ENV_NAMES = ["WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK", "WCONF_SIGNER_PROFILE"];
   332	
   333	/** `process.env.NAME`, `process.env["NAME"]`, `process.env['NAME']`, backticks. */
   334	export function secretEnvReads(sources: Source[]): { rel: string; needle: string; line: number }[] {
   335	  const hits: { rel: string; needle: string; line: number }[] = [];
   336	  for (const s of sources) {
   337	    // ⚠⚠ **MATCHED OVER THE WHOLE SOURCE, NOT LINE BY LINE.** The first version
   338	    // split on newlines and then applied a regex that allows whitespace — so a
   339	    // read wrapped by a formatter, `process.env[` on one line and the key on the
   340	    // next, produced no hit and part 4 reported PASS (`CLAIMS.md` E-221).
   341	    // **A line-based scan cannot see an expression that spans lines**, and
   342	    // formatters wrap expressions for a living.
   343	    for (const name of SECRET_ENV_NAMES) {
   344	      const re = new RegExp(
   345	        `process\\s*\\.\\s*env\\s*(?:\\.\\s*${name}\\b|\\[\\s*["'\`]${name}["'\`]\\s*\\])`,
   346	        "g",
   347	      );
   348	      for (const m of s.code.matchAll(re)) {
   349	        // Line number derived from the match offset, so it still points at code.
   350	        const line = s.code.slice(0, m.index).split("\n").length;
   351	        hits.push({ rel: s.rel, needle: `read of ${name}`, line });
   352	      }
   353	    }
   354	  }
   355	  return hits;
   356	}
   357	
   358	export const SECRET_NEEDLES = [
   359	  // ⚠ Env names are handled by `secretEnvReads` (structural). Keeping them here
   360	  // as well is harmless for the ident pass and catches a bare identifier use.
   361	  "WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK",
   362	  "WCONF_SIGNER_PROFILE",
   363	  "decryptInRange",
   364	  "decryptAmount",
   365	  "elgamal_sk",
   366	  "account_seed",
   367	  "Bun.spawn",
   368	  "child_process",
   369	];
   370	
   371	/** Every needle in `needles` that occurs in `sources`, with where. */
   372	export function findNeedles(
   373	  sources: Source[],
   374	  needles: string[],
   375	  field: "code" | "ident" = "code",
   376	): { needle: string; rel: string; line: number }[] {
   377	  const hits: { needle: string; rel: string; line: number }[] = [];
   378	  for (const s of sources) {
   379	    const lines = s[field].split("\n");
   380	    for (const needle of needles) {
   381	      lines.forEach((l, idx) => { if (l.includes(needle)) hits.push({ needle, rel: s.rel, line: idx + 1 }); });
   382	    }
   383	  }
   384	  return hits;
   385	}

   387	/**
   388	 * Files that reference the socket at all. Part 5 requires exactly one.
   389	 *
   390	 * ⚠⚠ **THIS LIST DISAGREED WITH `SOCKET_NEEDLES` AND THE NARROWER ONE WAS THE
   391	 * GATE — `CLAIMS.md` E-226, the SIXTH instance in this repository of a gate
   392	 * built to catch false passes false-passing** (E-165, E-168, E-182, E-198,
   393	 * E-203). `SOCKET_NEEDLES` carries **eight** needles including `node:net` and
   394	 * `createConnection`; this list carried **four** and omitted both. Part 1
   395	 * applies the eight to `web/app` ONLY, so **a second UDS client in `web/api`
   396	 * written with `node:net`'s `createConnection` was invisible to the whole-tier
   397	 * count** — the gate would print *"exactly one socket client"* over two.
   398	 * Found by `REVIEW-036b` with repository access.
   399	 *
   400	 * ⚠ **It is deliberately NOT `SOCKET_NEEDLES` itself.** That set includes
   401	 * `signer-status` and `signerSocketPath`, which match an ordinary *import* of
   402	 * the one client — `server.ts` imports `readSignerStatus` from
   403	 * `./signer-status` — so using it here would count importers as clients and
   404	 * the check would fail on a correct tree. **This list is the constructs that
   405	 * OPEN a socket; that list is anything that names one.** Two consumers, two
   406	 * instruments (E-203's fourth lesson, and `CLAIMS.md` E-220).
   407	 */
   408	export const SOCKET_OPENERS = [
   409	  "signer.sock",
   410	  "WCONF_SIGNER_SOCKET",
   411	  "Bun.connect",
   412	  "unix:",
   413	  // ⚠ Added by E-226. Both are how Node opens a UDS, and both were missing.
   414	  "node:net",
   415	  "createConnection",
   416	  // ⚠⚠ Added by REVIEW-037 F3 (E-234). E-226's fix added the two spellings the
   417	  // FINDING named and stopped there — so `import net from "net"; net.connect(p)`
   418	  // still counted zero, and the leg demonstrated it against the real source
   419	  // set. **A fix written against a finding's list is a fix against that list**
   420	  // (E-168's rule, landing on the fix for E-226 one round later).
   421	  "net.connect",
   422	  'from "net"',
   423	  "from 'net'",
   424	  'require("net")',
   425	  "require('net')",
   426	];
   427	
   428	/**
   429	 * ⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` E-238.** Three corrections from the REVIEW-037 spot-check:
   430	 *
   431	 *  - **`from "net"` may span lines.** `import transport from\n  "net";` evaded
   432	 *    the substring needle, so the specifier is matched by regex, not `includes`.
   433	 *  - **`await import("net")` is a bare-specifier client** and matched nothing.
   434	 *  - ⚠ **A TYPE-ONLY import opens no socket** and was counted, which is a
   435	 *    FALSE-FAIL: `import type { Socket } from "node:net"` raised the client
   436	 *    count with no runtime effect. Type-only import lines are stripped first.
   437	 */
   438	const BARE_NET_SPECIFIER = /(?:\bfrom\s*|\bimport\s*\(\s*|\brequire\s*\(\s*)["'](?:node:)?net["']/;
   439	
   440	export function socketClients(sources: Source[]): string[] {
   441	  return [...new Set(
   442	    sources
   443	      .filter((s) => {
   444	        // A type-only import is erased at compile time and opens nothing.
   445	        const runtime = s.code.replace(/^\s*import\s+type\b[^;]*;?/gm, "");
   446	        return SOCKET_OPENERS.some((n) => runtime.includes(n)) || BARE_NET_SPECIFIER.test(runtime);
   447	      })
   448	      .map((s) => s.rel),
   449	  )];
   450	}

   452	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   453	// Controls — counted by the helper that runs them (E-203), never typed.
   454	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   455	
   456	let controlsRun = 0;
   457	let failures = 0;
   458	
   459	function control(label: string, passed: boolean, detail: string) {
   460	  controlsRun++;
   461	  if (!passed) { failures++; console.log(`  CONTROL ✗ ${label} — ${detail}`); }
   462	  else console.log(`  CONTROL ✓ ${label}`);
   463	}
   464	
   465	function check(label: string, passed: boolean, detail: string) {
   466	  if (!passed) { failures++; console.log(`  ✗ ${label}\n      ${detail}`); }
   467	  else console.log(`  ✓ ${label}`);
   468	}
   469	
   470	/** Build a synthetic source so a control runs through the REAL detector. */
   471	function synthetic(rel: string, body: string): Source {
   472	  return make(rel, body);
   473	}

   488	// nothing. Found by reading the gate's own output rather than by a control,
   489	// which is why it is a hard refusal now: **deleting the UI must not be a way to
   490	// pass.** `CLAIMS.md` E-142's narrowing, and E-190's precedent — a gate that
   491	// refuses its own extension loudly.

   498	              "      so the gate would certify a property it never checked. A gate over an\n" +
   499	              "      empty set proves nothing (CLAIMS.md E-142). Build the app, or remove\n" +

     1	/**

     5	 * exactly like a disclosure.** That row was written about a docstring citing a

     9	 */
    10	
    11	import { test, expect, describe } from "bun:test";
    12	import { existsSync, readFileSync, readdirSync } from "node:fs";
    13	import { join } from "node:path";
    14	import { CAPABILITY_MATRIX, INVARIANTS } from "./capability.ts";
    15	
    16	const REPO = new URL("../..", import.meta.url).pathname.replace(/\/$/, "");
    17	const CLAIMS = readFileSync(join(REPO, "CLAIMS.md"), "utf8");
    18	
    19	/** Does a `REVIEW-nnn` / `ADR-nnnn` name a file that exists? */
    20	function hasRef(ref: string): boolean {
    21	  const dirs = ["docs/decisions", "docs/reviews"];
    22	  for (const d of dirs) {
    23	    try {
    24	      if (readdirSync(join(REPO, d)).some((f) => f.startsWith(ref))) return true;
    25	    } catch { /* directory absent — falls through to false */ }
    26	  }
    27	  return false;
    28	}
    29	
    30	/** Every `path/like/this.ext` or `path/like/this.ext:123` in a citation. */
    31	function filePaths(citation: string): string[] {
    32	  return [...citation.matchAll(/\b((?:[\w.-]+\/)+[\w.-]+\.(?:rs|ts|tsx|md|py|json))(?::\d+)?/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
    33	}
    34	
    35	/**
    36	 * Named rulings, ADRs and issues — the third kind of citable target.
    37	 *
    38	 * ⚠ These are checked to EXIST as files or as text in the repository, not
    39	 * merely to look like citations, because "looks like a citation" is exactly the
    40	 * bar that let prose through.
    41	 */
    42	function rulingRefs(citation: string): string[] {
    43	  return [...citation.matchAll(/\b(REVIEW-\d+[a-z]?|ADR-\d{4}|issue #\d+)\b/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
    44	}
    45	
    46	/**
    47	 * External sources — a fourth citable kind, and the only one whose target this
    48	 * repository cannot hold.
    49	 *
    50	 * ⚠ **Validated on the thing that actually matters for an external source: a
    51	 * READ DATE.** `CLAIMS.md` P-3's habit generalises — an external corpus moves,
    52	 * so a citation to one without the date it was read is a claim with no
    53	 * falsifier. A bare domain does **not** count; `docs.crossmark.io (read
    54	 * 2026-08-28)` does.
    55	 */
    56	function externalRefs(citation: string): string[] {
    57	  if (!/\b(?:read|observed|checked)\s+\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}\b/i.test(citation)) return [];
    58	  return [...citation.matchAll(/\b([a-z0-9-]+\.(?:app|io|dev|com|org|net))\b/gi)].map((m) => m[1]);
    59	}
    60	
    61	/** Everything in a citation that this test can actually go and check. */
    62	export function resolvableTargets(citation: string): string[] {
    63	  return [...filePaths(citation), ...registerRows(citation), ...rulingRefs(citation), ...externalRefs(citation)];
    64	}
    65	
    66	/** Every `E-nnn`, `F-nnn`, `P-nn`, `B-nn` register row a citation names. */
    67	function registerRows(citation: string): string[] {
    68	  return [...citation.matchAll(/\b([EFPBRU]-\d+)\b/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
    69	}
    70	
    71	/**
    72	 * Every `KeySource::… => "token"` arm in the signer, **unfiltered**.
    73	 *
    74	 * ⚠ The match is anchored on `KeySource::` so it cannot pick up unrelated
    75	 * string-returning arms elsewhere in the file — narrowing by SHAPE, not by a
    76	 * whitelist of values already known.
    77	 */
    78	export function parseKeySourceTokens(src: string): string[] {
    79	  return [...src.matchAll(/KeySource::\w+\s*=>\s*"([^"]+)"/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
    80	}
    81	
    82	const KEYGEN_SRC = readFileSync(join(REPO, "signer/src/onboarding.rs"), "utf8");
    83	const keySourceTokens = () => [...new Set(parseKeySourceTokens(KEYGEN_SRC))];
    84	
    85	const ALL_CAPS = CAPABILITY_MATRIX.flatMap((r) => [
    86	  [r.id, "decryptOwnBalance", r.decryptOwnBalance] as const,
    87	  [r.id, "proveAndSign", r.proveAndSign] as const,
    88	  [r.id, "publicTransactions", r.publicTransactions] as const,
    89	]);
    90	
    91	describe("the matrix is not empty and covers what the signer supports", () => {
    92	  // ⚠⚠ **THE FIRST VERSION FILTERED TO THE TWO TOKENS IT ALREADY KNEW, WHICH
    93	  // MADE IT BLIND TO EXACTLY THE CHANGE IT EXISTS TO CATCH.** A third
    94	  // `KeySource` arm would have been stripped before both the size assertion and
    95	  // the coverage check, so the test stayed green while the matrix silently
    96	  // omitted the new source — a regression test that cannot regress
    97	  // (`CLAIMS.md` E-214). Every arm is now derived, unfiltered.
    98	  test("every KeySource variant in the signer has a row — ALL arms, unfiltered", () => {
    99	    const tokens = keySourceTokens();
   100	    // A parse that finds nothing must fail loudly rather than pass vacuously.
   101	    expect(tokens.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   102	    const covered = new Set(CAPABILITY_MATRIX.map((r) => r.profileToken).filter(Boolean));
   103	    for (const t of tokens) expect(covered).toContain(t);
   104	  });
   105	
   106	  test("...and the parser sees exactly the arms the signer defines today", () => {
   107	    // ⚠ Stated as a fact about the tree, so adding an arm without a matrix row
   108	    // fails the test above rather than drifting past it.
   109	    expect(keySourceTokens().sort()).toEqual(["derived-from-master-seed", "standalone"]);
   110	  });
   111	
   112	  test("the parser CATCHES an added arm (verified by breaking it)", () => {
   113	    const withThird = KEYGEN_SRC.replace(
   114	      'KeySource::Standalone => "standalone",',
   115	      'KeySource::Standalone => "standalone",\n            KeySource::Custodial => "custodial",',
   116	    );
   117	    expect(parseKeySourceTokens(withThird)).toContain("custodial");
   118	  });
   119	
   120	  test("there are rows the code does NOT support, and they are marked not built", () => {
   121	    const notBuilt = CAPABILITY_MATRIX.filter((r) => !r.existsToday.built);
   122	    expect(notBuilt.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   123	    for (const r of notBuilt) expect(r.existsToday.where).toBeTruthy();
   124	  });
   125	});
   126	
   127	describe("every citation resolves — E-203's shape", () => {
   128	  for (const [rowId, field, cap] of ALL_CAPS) {
   129	    // ⚠⚠ **THIS ASSERTED ONLY THAT THE STRING WAS LONGER THAN EIGHT
   130	    // CHARACTERS.** When both parsers returned empty, the existence checks below
   131	    // passed over nothing — so arbitrary prose satisfied a "citation" test, and
   132	    // two live cells did exactly that (`CLAIMS.md` E-214). A citation must now
   133	    // resolve to a FILE, a register row, or a named ruling/ADR.
   134	    test(`${rowId}.${field} — its citation resolves to something checkable`, () => {
   135	      expect(resolvableTargets(cap.citation)).not.toEqual([]);
   136	    });
   137	
   138	    test(`${rowId}.${field} — every FILE it names exists`, () => {
   139	      const missing = filePaths(cap.citation).filter((f) => !existsSync(join(REPO, f)));
   140	      expect(missing).toEqual([]);
   141	    });
   142	
   143	    test(`${rowId}.${field} — every RULING or ADR it names exists in the tree`, () => {
   144	      const missing = rulingRefs(cap.citation).filter((r) => {
   145	        if (r.startsWith("issue #")) return false; // GitHub issues are outside the tree, by nature.
   146	        return !existsSync(join(REPO, "docs/decisions")) ? true : !hasRef(r);
   147	      });
   148	      expect(missing).toEqual([]);
   149	    });
   150	
   151	    test(`${rowId}.${field} — every REGISTER ROW it names exists in CLAIMS.md`, () => {
   152	      const missing = registerRows(cap.citation).filter((r) => !new RegExp(`\\|\\s*${r}\\s*\\|`).test(CLAIMS));
   153	      expect(missing).toEqual([]);
   154	    });
   155	  }
   156	
   157	  test("PROSE with no target is NOT a citation (verified by breaking it)", () => {
   158	    // ⚠ The exact shape that passed before: long enough, resolves to nothing.
   159	    expect(resolvableTargets("a sound and well-reasoned argument, honestly")).toEqual([]);
   160	    // ⚠ An external source with NO read date is not a citation either — the
   161	    // corpus moves, so an undated reference has no falsifier (P-3's habit).
   162	    expect(resolvableTargets("gemwallet.app API reference; docs.crossmark.io")).toEqual([]);
   163	    expect(resolvableTargets("gemwallet.app API reference (read 2026-08-28)")).not.toEqual([]);
   164	    expect(resolvableTargets("REVIEW-012 (derivation from master seed); ADR-0003 D2")).not.toEqual([]);
   165	    expect(resolvableTargets("signer/src/keygen.rs")).not.toEqual([]);
   166	    expect(resolvableTargets("CLAIMS.md E-117")).not.toEqual([]);
   167	  });
   168	
   169	  test("the detectors CATCH a bad citation (verified by breaking them)", () => {
   170	    expect(filePaths("signer/src/nonexistent-file.rs").filter((f) => !existsSync(join(REPO, f)))).toHaveLength(1);
   171	    expect(registerRows("CLAIMS.md E-99999").filter((r) => !new RegExp(`\\|\\s*${r}\\s*\\|`).test(CLAIMS))).toHaveLength(1);
   172	  });
   173	
   174	  test("...and do NOT fire on citations that resolve", () => {
   175	    expect(filePaths("signer/src/keygen.rs").filter((f) => !existsSync(join(REPO, f)))).toEqual([]);
   176	    expect(registerRows("CLAIMS.md E-117").filter((r) => !new RegExp(`\\|\\s*${r}\\s*\\|`).test(CLAIMS))).toEqual([]);
   177	  });
   178	});
   179	
   180	describe("every invariant names a real standing prohibition or finding", () => {
   181	  for (const inv of INVARIANTS) {
   182	    test(`${inv.rule} exists in CLAIMS.md`, () => {
   183	      expect(new RegExp(`\\|\\s*${inv.rule}\\s*\\|`).test(CLAIMS)).toBe(true);
   184	    });
   185	  }
   186	
   187	  test("the four standing prohibitions this tier most risks are all present", () => {
   188	    const rules = INVARIANTS.map((i) => i.rule);
   189	    for (const r of ["P-4", "P-9", "P-10", "P-11"]) expect(rules).toContain(r);
   190	  });
   191	});

   680	
   681	// --- Part 6 -----------------------------------------------------------------
   682	// ⚠⚠ **A FALSIFIER CONDITION NAMED IN THE ARCHITECTURE HAD NO FALSIFIER —
   683	// `CLAIMS.md` E-227.** Architecture §6 step 8's *Falsified if:* clause says the
   684	// step is falsified if *"the API binds to anything but loopback"*, and this
   685	// gate — **the artifact that clause names** — contained ZERO references to
   686	// `hostname`, `127.0.0.1`, `0.0.0.0` or `BIND_HOSTNAME` until this part was
   687	// written. **E-213 measured the `0.0.0.0` defect on the LAN; the fix went into
   688	// the code and the gate was never extended to the condition.** Found by
   689	// `REVIEW-036b` with repository access.
   690	//
   691	// ⚠ `Bun.serve` defaults to `0.0.0.0` when `listen` is handed a bare port
   692	// number, so "passes no hostname" is the defect, not merely a style.
   693	console.log("\nPart 6 — the API binds to loopback, and nothing else");
   694	
   695	const LOOPBACK_LITERALS = ['"127.0.0.1"', "'127.0.0.1'", '"localhost"', "'localhost'", '"::1"', "'::1'"];
   696	
   697	/**
   698	 * Every `.listen(` call must pass a `hostname`, and the bound value must be a
   699	 * loopback literal. ⚠ **LEXICAL, and it resolves ONE level of indirection** —
   700	 * a `const` naming a literal. A hostname computed at run time is invisible and
   701	 * is an asserted-uncaught control below.
   702	 */
   703	export function bindVerdict(sources: Source[]): { ok: boolean; why: string } {
   704	  const calls: { rel: string; call: string }[] = [];
   705	  for (const s of sources) {
   706	    // ⚠ `Bun.serve(` as well as `.listen(` — REVIEW-037 F2 showed a
   707	    // `Bun.serve({ hostname: "0.0.0.0" })` was invisible beside a correct
   708	    // `.listen(`, caught only by the vacuous-pass guard when it stood alone.
   709	    //
   710	    // ⚠⚠ **BALANCED, not `[^)]*` — `CLAIMS.md` E-237.** The first version
   711	    // stopped at the FIRST `)`, so a legitimate
   712	    // `.listen({ port: Number(p), hostname: BIND_HOSTNAME })` truncated to
   713	    // `.listen({ port: Number(` — the hostname fell outside the captured text
   714	    // and the call was reported as passing NO hostname. **A FALSE-FAIL, and a
   715	    // gate that cries wolf on correct code gets disabled**, which is the
   716	    // failure mode that ends a gate's life rather than merely blunting it.
   717	    for (const m of s.code.matchAll(/(?:\.listen|Bun\.serve)\s*\(/g)) {
   718	      let depth = 0, i = m.index! + m[0].length - 1;
   719	      for (; i < s.code.length; i++) {
   720	        if (s.code[i] === "(") depth++;
   721	        else if (s.code[i] === ")") { depth--; if (depth === 0) break; }
   722	      }
   723	      calls.push({ rel: s.rel, call: s.code.slice(m.index!, i + 1).replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim() });
   724	    }
   725	  }
   726	  if (calls.length === 0) return { ok: false, why: "no .listen( or Bun.serve( call found — this part would pass vacuously" };
   727	
   728	  for (const { rel, call } of calls) {
   729	    // ⚠⚠ **EVERY `hostname` KEY, NOT THE FIRST — `CLAIMS.md` E-238.** A
   730	    // duplicate key `{ hostname: "127.0.0.1", hostname: "0.0.0.0" }` passed,
   731	    // because the first match was inspected and **in JavaScript the LAST
   732	    // duplicate key wins** — so the check read the one value that does not take
   733	    // effect. Found by the REVIEW-037 detector spot-check.
   734	    const all = [...call.matchAll(/hostname\s*:\s*([^,}]+)/g)];
   735	    if (all.length === 0) return { ok: false, why: `${rel}: ${call} passes NO hostname — Bun.serve then defaults to 0.0.0.0 (E-213)` };
   736	    for (const m of all) {
   737	    const expr = m[1].trim();
   738	
   739	    // A quoted literal is checked directly.
   740	    if (/^["'`]/.test(expr)) {
   741	      if (!LOOPBACK_LITERALS.some((l) => expr === l)) return { ok: false, why: `${rel}: hostname ${expr} is not a loopback literal` };
   742	      continue;
   743	    }
   744	    // ⚠ A bare identifier is resolved to its declaration — ONE level, and only
   745	    // to a literal. REVIEW-037 F2: the first version checked that the call
   746	    // contained the WORD `hostname` and, separately, that SOME loopback
   747	    // constant existed somewhere — it never connected the two, so
   748	    // `.listen({ hostname: "10.13.156.66" })` passed while an unused
   749	    // `BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1"` sat in the same file.
   750	    if (/^[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*$/.test(expr)) {
   751	      const decls = sources.flatMap((s) => s.code.match(new RegExp(`\\b${expr}\\s*=\\s*("[^"]*"|'[^']*'|\`[^\`]*\`)`, "g")) ?? []);
   752	      if (decls.length === 0) return { ok: false, why: `${rel}: hostname \`${expr}\` resolves to no string literal in this source set` };
   753	      for (const d of decls) {
   754	        const lit = d.slice(d.indexOf("=") + 1).trim();
   755	        if (!LOOPBACK_LITERALS.some((l) => lit === l)) return { ok: false, why: `${rel}: hostname \`${expr}\` = ${lit}, which is not a loopback literal` };
   756	      }
   757	      continue;
   758	    }
   759	    // ⚠ Anything else — env read, ternary, concatenation, call — is NOT
   760	    // PROVABLY loopback, and this gate fails closed rather than assuming.
   761	    return { ok: false, why: `${rel}: hostname ${expr} is not a literal or a resolvable constant — not provably loopback` };
   762	    }
   763	  }
   764	  return { ok: true, why: `${calls.length} bind call(s), every hostname resolved to a loopback literal` };
   765	}
   766	
   767	// ⚠ Test files are excluded from the BIND scan — E-238. `probe.listen()` in a
   768	// test helper is not a network bind, and a gate that false-fails on correct
   769	// test code gets disabled. They remain in every other part.
   770	const bind = bindVerdict(apiSrc.filter((s) => !/\.test\.tsx?$/.test(s.rel)));
   771	check("every .listen( passes an explicit loopback hostname", bind.ok, bind.why);
   772	
   773	/**
   774	 * ⚠⚠ **THE DEV SERVER'S BIND IS PART OF THE SAME PROPERTY, AND THE FIRST
   775	 * VERSION OF PART 6 MISSED IT — found by this round's own self-attack, not by a
   776	 * leg** (`CLAIMS.md` E-231). `web/app/vite.config.ts` sets `host` **and**
   777	 * proxies `/api` to the API process. **A dev server on `0.0.0.0` therefore
   778	 * publishes the whole API — the issuer route included — to the LAN, bypassing
   779	 * the API's own loopback bind entirely.**
   780	 *
     1	/**
     2	 * Wording tests over every sentence this tier renders.
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠⚠ **`claims-check` is necessary and it is NOT sufficient, and this project
     5	 * has the receipts.** A false disclosure sentence survived that gate once
     6	 * already (`CLAIMS.md` F-24 — *"Public: the aggregate change"*, when
     7	 * `MPTAmount` is plaintext on **every individual** ConvertBack). **The surface
     8	 * without a wording test is where the false sentence always lives**, and a UI
     9	 * is the largest disclosure surface this project has built.
    10	 *
    11	 * These tests fail if the interface claims something `CLAIMS.md` forbids, or
    12	 * stops saying something a ruling obliges.
    13	 */

    23	const REPO = new URL("../..", import.meta.url).pathname.replace(/\/$/, "");
    24	
    25	function sources(dir: string): { rel: string; text: string }[] {
    26	  const out: { rel: string; text: string }[] = [];
    27	  const walk = (d: string) => {
    28	    for (const e of readdirSync(d)) {
    29	      if (e === "node_modules" || e === "dist" || e === ".output") continue;
    30	      const p = join(d, e);
    31	      if (statSync(p).isDirectory()) walk(p);
    32	      else if (/\.(ts|tsx)$/.test(e) && !e.endsWith(".test.ts")) {
    33	        out.push({ rel: p.slice(REPO.length + 1), text: readFileSync(p, "utf8") });
    34	      }
    35	    }
    36	  };
    37	  walk(join(REPO, dir));
    38	  return out;
    39	}

    41	const TIER = [...sources("web/api"), ...sources("web/app/src")];

    43	/** Stable per-body temp filename, so parallel tests do not collide. */
    44	function hashOf(s: string): number {
    45	  let h = 0;
    46	  for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) h = (Math.imul(31, h) + s.charCodeAt(i)) | 0;
    47	  return h;
    48	}
    49	
    50	/**
    51	 * Does this text ASSERT that a balance is non-zero?
    52	 *
    53	 * ⚠ The hedged forms — *"not the same as saying it is non-zero"*, *"some
    54	 * non-zero value, or never reset"* — are the CORRECT copy and must not match.
    55	 */
    56	export function assertsNonZero(text: string): boolean {
    57	  for (const m of text.matchAll(/non-zero/gi)) {
    58	    const before = text.slice(Math.max(0, m.index - 60), m.index);
    59	    if (/\b(?:not|never|neither|nothing|isn't|is not)\b[^.]{0,60}$/i.test(before)) continue;
    60	    if (/\bsome\s*$/i.test(before)) continue;   // "some non-zero value, or never reset"
    61	    if (/\bof a\s*$|\bthe value of\s*$/i.test(before)) continue;
    62	    return true;
    63	  }
    64	  return false;
    65	}
    66	
    67	/** Does this line ASSERT that a holder is private from the issuer? P-9 says no
    68	 * configuration makes that true, so every occurrence in this tier is a negation
    69	 * and the detector must distinguish the two.
    70	 */
    71	export function claimsPrivateFromIssuer(line: string): boolean {
    72	  const claim = /private\s+from\s+the\s+issuer/i;
    73	  if (!claim.test(line)) return false;
    74	  const before = line.slice(0, line.search(claim));
    75	  return !/\b(?:no|not|never|neither|nothing|cannot|does\s+not|is\s+not)\b/i.test(before);
    76	}
    77	
    78	/** Does this line ASSERT that identities are hidden?
    79	 *
    80	 * ⚠ The guard is the negation FORM. `No key source hides identities` and
    81	 * `It does not hide who transacts` are the tier's own correct sentences and
    82	 * must not match; `hides identities` standing alone must.
    83	 */
    84	export function claimsIdentitiesHidden(line: string): boolean {
    85	  const claim = /\bhide[sn]?\b[^.]{0,40}?\b(?:identit|who\s+transacts|sender|receiver)/i;
    86	  if (!claim.test(line)) return false;
    87	  // A negator anywhere earlier in the same sentence inverts it.
    88	  const upToClaim = line.slice(0, line.search(claim));
    89	  return !/\b(?:no|not|never|neither|nothing|cannot|does\s+not|is\s+not)\b/i.test(upToClaim);
    90	}
    91	
    92	/** Does this text ASSERT that something is hidden or confidential? */
    93	export function assertsHidden(text: string): boolean {
    94	  for (const m of text.matchAll(/\b(?:hidden|confidential|private)\b/gi)) {
    95	    const before = text.slice(Math.max(0, m.index - 40), m.index);
    96	    if (/\b(?:not|never|no|nothing|neither|publicly)\b\s*$|\b(?:not|never)\b[^.]{0,20}$/i.test(before)) continue;
    97	    // `confidential balance` / `ConfidentialMPT` are NAMES, not claims.
    98	    const after = text.slice(m.index, m.index + 40);
    99	    if (/^confidential\s+(?:balance|transaction|position|MPT|send)/i.test(after)) continue;
   100	    return true;
   101	  }
   102	  return false;
   103	}

   111	describe("P-5 — never trustless, non-custodial, audited, production-ready or secure", () => {
   112	  // ⚠ The needle is the CLAIM, not the word. "not secure" and "is secure" both
   113	  // contain "secure", and a test that banned the word would fail on its own
   114	  // disclaimer — which is how a wording test gets deleted rather than fixed.
   115	  const FORBIDDEN = [
   116	    /\bis\s+(?:fully\s+)?secure\b/i,
   117	    /\bproduction[- ]ready\b(?!\s*\()/i,
   118	    /\bnon-custodial\b/i,
   119	    /\btrustless\b/i,
   120	    /\bfully\s+audited\b/i,
   121	  ];
   122	
   123	  for (const re of FORBIDDEN) {
   124	    test(`no rendered sentence asserts ${re}`, () => {
   125	      const hits: string[] = [];
   126	      for (const s of TIER) {
   127	        for (const line of s.text.split("\n")) {
   128	          // A line that cites the prohibition is not making the claim.
   129	          if (/P-5|CLAIMS\.md|forbid|never claim|prohibition/i.test(line)) continue;
   130	          if (re.test(line)) hits.push(`${s.rel}: ${line.trim().slice(0, 120)}`);
   131	        }
   132	      }
   133	      expect(hits).toEqual([]);
   134	    });
   135	  }

   782	 * in `vite.config.ts` and the API got a bare port; part 6 was written to gate

   784	 * defect gates the last defect.**

   786	export function hostKeyVerdict(sources: Source[]): { ok: boolean; why: string } {
   787	  // ⚠⚠ **EVERY `host:` IS COLLECTED, NOT ONLY THE QUOTED-LITERAL ONES —
   788	  // `CLAIMS.md` E-237, THE NINTH FALSE-PASSING GATE IN THIS REPOSITORY.** The
   789	  // first version's regex matched only `host:` followed by a quoted literal, so
   790	  // a `host: process.env.H` was **not collected at all** and passed silently
   791	  // whenever one legitimate loopback literal existed elsewhere to satisfy the
   792	  // vacuous-pass guard.
   793	  //
   794	  // ⚠ **That is E-235's defect — the one `bindVerdict` was rewritten to fix —
   795	  // reproduced in its SIBLING, twenty lines away, IN THE SAME COMMIT.** The fix
   796	  // was applied to the function the finding named rather than to the property
   797	  // both functions share. `CLAIMS.md` E-168, and the rule is still not
   798	  // self-executing.
   799	  const hits: { rel: string; decl: string }[] = [];
   800	  for (const s of sources) {
   801	    // ⚠ `host:`, `"host":`, `'host':` and `["host"]:` — E-238. A computed key
   802	    // bypassed collection entirely while a harmless sibling satisfied the
   803	    // vacuous-pass guard, and a legitimately quoted key FALSE-FAILED.
   804	    for (const d of s.code.match(/(?:\bhost|["']host["']|\[\s*["']host["']\s*\])\s*:\s*[^,}\n]+/g) ?? []) {
   805	      hits.push({ rel: s.rel, decl: d.trim() });
   806	    }
   807	  }
   808	  if (hits.length === 0) return { ok: false, why: "no `host:` key found under web/app — this check would pass vacuously" };
   809	  for (const { rel, decl } of hits) {
   810	    const val = decl.slice(decl.indexOf(":") + 1).trim();
   811	    if (/^["'`]/.test(val)) {
   812	      if (!LOOPBACK_LITERALS.some((l) => val === l)) return { ok: false, why: `${rel}: ${decl} is not a loopback literal` };
   813	      continue;
   814	    }
   815	    // Fail closed: not a literal, so not provably loopback.
   816	    return { ok: false, why: `${rel}: ${decl} is not a string literal — not provably loopback` };
   817	  }
   818	  return { ok: true, why: `${hits.length} host: key(s), all loopback` };
   819	}
   820	
   821	const hostBind = hostKeyVerdict(app);
   822	check("every `host:` under web/app is a loopback literal", hostBind.ok, hostBind.why);
   823	
   824	// ⚠⚠ E-237's controls. The first two are the ninth false-pass and the
   825	// false-fail that came with it, both found by a self-attack during the
   826	// REVIEW-037 detector spot-check.
   827	// ⚠⚠ E-238's controls — six evasions and false-fails found by the REVIEW-037
   828	// detector spot-check, each run through the REAL detector.
   829	control("a DUPLICATE hostname key is caught (the LAST one wins in JS)",
   830	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/s.ts",
   831	    'app.listen({ port: 3011, hostname: "127.0.0.1", hostname: "0.0.0.0" });')]).ok,
   832	  "only the first hostname was inspected — the check reads the value that does NOT take effect");
   833	
   834	control("a COMPUTED host key [\"host\"] is caught",
   835	  !hostKeyVerdict([synthetic("web/app/v.ts",
   836	    'export default { server: { ["host"]: "0.0.0.0" }, preview: { host: "127.0.0.1" } };')]).ok,
   837	  "a computed host key bypassed collection while a sibling satisfied the vacuous guard");
   838	
   839	control("a QUOTED host key is NOT a false-FAIL",
   840	  hostKeyVerdict([synthetic("web/app/v.ts", 'export default { server: { "host": "127.0.0.1" } };')]).ok,
   841	  "a legitimately quoted host key was not collected and failed vacuously");
   842	
   843	control("a dynamic `await import(\"net\")` client IS counted",
   844	  socketClients([
   845	    synthetic("web/api/one.ts", 'Bun.connect({ unix: "/x/signer.sock" })'),
   846	    synthetic("web/api/two.ts", 'const transport = await import("net"); transport.connect({ path: p });'),
   847	  ]).length === 2,
   848	  "a dynamic bare-specifier net client was invisible");
   849	
   850	control("a MULTI-LINE `from \"net\"` import IS counted",
   851	  socketClients([
   852	    synthetic("web/api/one.ts", 'Bun.connect({ unix: "/x/signer.sock" })'),
   853	    synthetic("web/api/three.ts", 'import transport from\n  "net";\ntransport.connect({ path: "/x" });'),
   854	  ]).length === 2,
   855	  "the specifier needle broke across a newline");
   856	
   857	control("a TYPE-ONLY socket import is NOT counted as a client",
   858	  socketClients([
   859	    synthetic("web/api/one.ts", 'Bun.connect({ unix: "/x/signer.sock" })'),
   860	    synthetic("web/api/types.ts", 'import type { Socket } from "node:net";\nexport type C = Socket;'),
   861	  ]).length === 1,
   862	  "a type-only import raised the client count — it opens nothing and is a false-FAIL");
   863	
   864	control("a NON-LITERAL host: beside a legitimate loopback host IS caught",
   865	  !hostKeyVerdict([
   866	    synthetic("web/app/vite.config.ts", 'export default { server: { host: "127.0.0.1" } };'),
   867	    synthetic("web/app/other.ts", 'export const s = { server: { host: process.env.H } };'),
   868	  ]).ok,
   869	  "a non-literal host was not collected — E-237 is open again, and it is E-235's defect in the sibling");
   870	
   871	control("a bind whose ARGUMENTS CONTAIN PARENTHESES is not a false-FAIL",
   872	  bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/server.ts",
   873	    'export const BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1";\napp.listen({ port: Number(p), hostname: BIND_HOSTNAME });')]).ok,
   874	  "a legitimate nested-call bind was rejected — the extractor is truncating at the first ) again");
   875	
   876	control("a 0.0.0.0 host STILL caught with the widened collector",
   877	  !hostKeyVerdict([synthetic("web/app/vite.config.ts", 'export default { server: { host: "0.0.0.0" } };')]).ok,
   878	  "widening the collector lost the original catch");
   879	
   880	control("a 0.0.0.0 dev-server host IS caught",
   881	  !hostKeyVerdict([synthetic("web/app/vite.config.ts", 'export default { server: { host: "0.0.0.0" } };')]).ok,
   882	  "a 0.0.0.0 dev-server host was not caught — it would proxy the API to the LAN");
   883	
   884	control("a LAN dev-server host IS caught",
   885	  !hostKeyVerdict([synthetic("web/app/vite.config.ts", 'export default { server: { host: "10.13.156.66" } };')]).ok,
   886	  "a LAN dev-server host was accepted as loopback");
   887	
   888	control("the REAL dev-server form is NOT caught",
   889	  hostKeyVerdict([synthetic("web/app/vite.config.ts", 'export default { server: { host: "127.0.0.1" } };')]).ok,
   890	  "the correct loopback dev-server host was rejected — the check would false-FAIL");
   891	
   892	control("a source set with NO host: key FAILS rather than passing vacuously",
   893	  !hostKeyVerdict([synthetic("web/app/plain.tsx", "export const x = 1;")]).ok,
   894	  "an empty host: set passed vacuously");
   895

   897	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts", 'const BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1"; app.listen(3011);')]).ok,
   898	  "a bare port bind was not caught — E-213's exact defect is invisible again");
   899	
   900	control("an explicit 0.0.0.0 bind IS caught",
   901	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts", 'const BIND_HOSTNAME = "0.0.0.0"; app.listen({ port, hostname: BIND_HOSTNAME });')]).ok,
   902	  "a 0.0.0.0 bind was not caught");
   903	
   904	control("a non-loopback hostname literal IS caught",
   905	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts", 'const BIND_HOSTNAME = "10.13.156.66"; app.listen({ port, hostname: BIND_HOSTNAME });')]).ok,
   906	  "a LAN address was accepted as loopback");
   907

   909	  bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts", 'const BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1"; app.listen({ port, hostname: BIND_HOSTNAME });')]).ok,
   910	  "the correct loopback form was rejected — the check would false-FAIL");
   911

   913	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/plain.ts", "export const x = 1;")]).ok,
   914	  "an empty listen set passed vacuously — parts 1 and 3's guard is missing here");
   915	
   916	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   917	// The residual, and the two shapes this gate CANNOT catch, asserted as
   918	// uncaught. ⚠ If either is ever caught, the gate FAILS — because the
   919	// disclosure below would then be stale (E-203's mechanism).
   920	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   921	console.log("\nAsserted-uncaught — the disclosure below fails if these ever start passing");
   922

   924	  findNeedles([synthetic("web/app/sneak.tsx", 'const p = ["signer", "sock"].join(".");')], SOCKET_NEEDLES).length === 0,
   925	  "⚠ this is now CAUGHT — good, but whatThisDoesNotSay() item 1 is STALE. Update it.");
   926	
   927	// ⚠ **This was an ASSERTED-UNCAUGHT control and is now a positive one.**
   928	// `REVIEW-037` F2 showed part 6's first version never connected the hostname
   929	// ARGUMENT to the constant it checked, so a run-time hostname passed. The
   930	// rewrite FAILS CLOSED on anything it cannot resolve to a literal — and the
   931	// asserted-uncaught control then failed, correctly, saying item 10 was stale.
   932	// **That is E-203's mechanism catching a disclosure that had gone false.**
   933	control("a hostname computed at run time IS caught — part 6 fails closed",
   934	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts",
   935	    'const BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1"; app.listen({ port, hostname: pick() });')]).ok,
   936	  "a computed hostname was accepted — part 6 is assuming rather than resolving");
   937	
   938	control("a hostname read from the environment IS caught",
   939	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts",
   940	    'const BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1"; app.listen({ port, hostname: process.env.H });')]).ok,
   941	  "an env-supplied hostname was accepted as loopback");
   942	
   943	control("a LAN hostname ARGUMENT is caught even when a loopback CONSTANT is present",
   944	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts",
   945	    'export const BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1";\napp.listen({ port, hostname: "10.13.156.66" });')]).ok,
   946	  "REVIEW-037 F2 is open again: the check is not connecting the argument to the declaration");
   947

   949	  !bindVerdict([synthetic("web/api/x.ts",
   950	    'export const BIND_HOSTNAME = "127.0.0.1";\napp.listen({ port, hostname: BIND_HOSTNAME });\nBun.serve({ port, hostname: "0.0.0.0" });')]).ok,
   951	  "a Bun.serve bind beside a correct .listen was invisible");
   952	
   953	control("ASSERTED UNCAUGHT: a socket reached through a dependency this gate does not read",
   954	  findNeedles([synthetic("web/app/dep.tsx", 'import { talk } from "some-pkg"; await talk();')], SOCKET_NEEDLES).length === 0,
   955	  "⚠ this is now CAUGHT — whatThisDoesNotSay() item 2 is STALE. Update it.");

   959	export function whatThisDoesNotSay(): string[] {
   960	  return [
   961	    "1. It is LEXICAL. A socket path assembled at runtime — string concatenation, an array join, " +
   962	      "a base64 blob — is invisible to it, and that shape is an asserted-uncaught control above.",
   963	    "2. It reads THIS REPOSITORY'S sources only. A dependency under node_modules that opens a " +
   964	      "socket is not read, and could not be gated this way; the property there is that nothing in " +
   965	      "web/app has a reason to call one.",
   966	    "3. The needle sets are NAMED. A signing helper spelled differently from every entry in " +
   967	      "SIGNING_NEEDLES is not counted, by name (E-142's narrowing, forced three times since).",
   968	    "4. It checks SOURCE, not behaviour. It cannot prove that the one socket client sends only what " +
   969	      "it constructs, and it is not a substitute for reading that file.",
   970	    "5. It says nothing about what the tier DISCLOSES. Whether a rendered sentence about privacy is " +
   971	      "true is `claims-check`'s question and the wording tests', not this gate's.",
   972	    "6. Part 4 is about what the tier HOLDS; parts 1-3 are about what it can REACH. Those are " +
   973	      "different properties and the gate had only the second when an issuer key was added to the " +
   974	      "API process (E-212). A secret obtained some way this list does not name is still invisible.",
   975	    "7. The signing pass BLANKS string literals, so a call assembled as a string and eval'd is " +
   976	      "invisible to it. That is the price of not treating prose about another project's API as a " +
   977	      "violation of ours, it is a deliberate trade, and the socket pass — where a literal IS the " +
   978	      "value — keeps literals for exactly the opposite reason (CLAIMS.md E-203, fourth consumer).",
   979	    // --- added by REVIEW-036 (E-226, E-227). Every one of these was a limit
   980	    // the gate ALREADY had and did NOT print, which is what made the
   981	    // overstatement a P1: ADR-0003 leans on this list as the gate's reach.
   982	    "8. It scans web/app and web/api ONLY. A signing helper, a socket client or a listener placed " +
   983	      "in any OTHER directory under web/ — a new web/bridge/, say — is not read at all, and the " +
   984	      "count in Part 5 is over the two directories it loads (REVIEW-036b).",
   985	    "9. It does not check for MUTATING routes. A .post, .put or .delete that writes is invisible " +
   986	      "to every part; 'read-only' is checked as 'reaches no signing operation and holds no " +
   987	      "secret', which is not the same property (REVIEW-036b).",
   988	    "10. Part 6's bind check is LEXICAL and FAILS CLOSED. It resolves a quoted literal, or ONE " +
   989	      "level of indirection to a constant declared in the same source set; anything else — an env " +
   990	      "read, a ternary, a concatenation, a call — is REFUSED rather than assumed loopback. ⚠ It " +
   991	      "reads `.listen(` and `Bun.serve(` by name: a listener started through some other API, or in " +
   992	      "a directory this gate does not load, is invisible (REVIEW-037 F2).",
   993	    "11. ⚠ Part 6's resolver IGNORES LEXICAL SCOPE. A parameter or inner binding that SHADOWS an " +
   994	      "outer loopback constant resolves to the outer one, so `function start(HOST){ listen({hostname: HOST}) }` " +
   995	      "called with a routable address passes. Full scope analysis needs a parser; this is a named " +
   996	      "limit, not an oversight (REVIEW-037 spot-check, E-238).",
   997	    "12. ⚠ Part 6 matches `.listen(` and `Bun.serve(` BY NAME. A parenthesised `(Bun.serve)(…)`, a " +
   998	      "bind through a framework helper, or an aliased reference is invisible. Chasing spellings is " +
   999	      "how a named set pretends to be a property; it is disclosed instead (E-238).",
  1000	    "13. ⚠ A socket reached through an ALIASED dynamic import — `const t = await import(x); t.connect(…)` " +
  1001	      "with the specifier assembled at run time — is invisible. The import spellings are matched; " +
  1002	      "the aliasing is not, and cannot be lexically (E-238).",
  1003	    "14. ⚠ Part 6 reads STRING LITERALS as values, so prose containing `host: \"0.0.0.0\"` inside a " +
  1004	      "quoted string FALSE-FAILS. That is the unsafe direction chosen deliberately: a false-fail is " +
  1005	      "loud and gets fixed, a false-pass is silent (CLAIMS.md E-203's two-consumer lesson).",
  1006	    "15. It says nothing about WHO MAY ASK. The tier serves every route unauthenticated on " +
  1007	      "loopback, and loopback is not uid-scoped — so a datum this gate certifies as reachable-by-" +
  1008	      "design is reachable by any local user. Egress is a third property beside REACH and HOLD, " +
  1009	      "and no part of this gate looks at it (CLAIMS.md E-228, REVIEW-036c U1).",
  1010	  ];

   267	
   268	describe("the issuer view holds NO key and performs NO decryption", () => {
   269	  // ⚠⚠ It DID hold one, and decrypted per request, which is a decryption
   270	  // oracle over an unauthenticated GET — the fourth rotation trigger ADR-0003
   271	  // names, written by the same session one file over (CLAIMS.md E-212).
   272	  // Architecture §6 step 8 lets this tier touch the TOOL'S OUTPUT and never a
   273	  // private key. These tests are what stop it drifting back.
   274	  // ⚠ **The gate's own instruments, imported rather than re-derived.** These
   275	  // needles are IDENTIFIERS, so string literals are blanked: declaring
   276	  // `FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV = "WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK"` is the name being forbidden,
   277	  // not a use of it, and a weaker stripper in the test would disagree with the
   278	  // gate about the same file. One instrument, two callers (CLAIMS.md E-207).
   279	  const src = readFileSync(join(REPO, "web/api/issuer.ts"), "utf8");
   280	  const code = stripStringLiterals(stripComments(src));
   281	
   282	  test("it never reads the issuer secret from the environment", () => {
   283	    expect(code).not.toContain(`process.env.${FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV}`);
   284	    expect(code).not.toMatch(/process\.env\[/);
   285	  });
   286	
   287	  test("no file under web/ reads the issuer secret", () => {
   288	    const hits = TIER.filter((f) =>
   289	      stripStringLiterals(stripComments(f.text)).includes(`process.env.${FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV}`),
   290	    ).map((f) => f.rel);
   291	    expect(hits).toEqual([]);
   292	  });
   293	
   294	  test("it spawns nothing — argv is world-readable via `ps`", () => {
   295	    // ⚠ The version that decrypted used `decryptInRange`, which spawns
   296	    // `mptool decrypt <ciphertext> <sk> …`. That is the exact exposure the
   297	    // issuer CLI refuses a `--key` flag to avoid.
   298	    // ⚠ Built from the gate's own needle list rather than re-spelled here.
   299	    // Two reasons: one source of truth, and a literal spelling in a test file
   300	    // is itself a secret reference the gate must then flag — which it did.
   301	    for (const needle of SECRET_NEEDLES) expect(code).not.toContain(needle);
   302	  });
   303	
   304	  test("it never accepts a key from the request", () => {
   305	    expect(code).not.toMatch(/params\.(key|sk|secret)/);
   306	    expect(code).not.toMatch(/query\.(key|sk|secret)/);
   307	    expect(code).not.toMatch(/headers\.get\(["'`](x-)?(key|sk|secret)/i);
   308	  });
   309	
   310	  // ⚠⚠ **THE FALLBACK COPY WAS STALE AND CONTRADICTED THE SAME SITE.** Before an
   311	  // account is picked, `view` is null and the issuer page rendered a default
   312	  // string saying the web process reads the key from its environment — false
   313	  // since the snapshot design, and contradicting `/api/meta`'s
   314	  // `holdsKeys: false` two clicks away. `/api-doc` carried the same sentence.
   315	  // ⚠ **A default rendered only in an empty state is exactly where a stale
   316	  // sentence survives**: nobody screenshots the empty state.
   317	  test("no rendered copy claims THIS process reads the issuer key", () => {
   318	    const hits: string[] = [];
   319	    for (const f of TIER) {
   320	      if (!f.rel.startsWith("web/app/")) continue;
   321	      for (const line of f.text.split("\n")) {
   322	        if (/^\s*(\/\/|\*|\{\/\*)/.test(line)) continue;          // a comment about the defect is not the defect
   323	        if (/(issuer )?key is read from|key from the environment|environment only/i.test(line)) {
   324	          hits.push(`${f.rel}: ${line.trim().slice(0, 110)}`);
   325	        }
   326	      }
   327	    }
   328	    expect(hits).toEqual([]);
   329	  });
   330

   333	    expect(/(issuer )?key is read from|key from the environment|environment only/i.test(bad)).toBe(true);
   334	  });
   335	
   336	  test("the key policy states the absence, not a guard around a presence", () => {
   337	    expect(ISSUER_KEY_POLICY).toMatch(/holds NO issuer key/i);
   338	    // ⚠ Was `/no oracle to authenticate/`. E-241 made the property STRONGER —

   340	    // follows the property rather than the old sentence. Retargeted, not
   341	    // deleted: it still fails if the policy starts describing a guard.
   342	    expect(ISSUER_KEY_POLICY).toMatch(/no request that reaches a decryption and none that returns one/i);
   343	    expect(ISSUER_KEY_POLICY).not.toMatch(/authenticat(e|ed|ion) (the|this) (request|caller|endpoint)/i);
   344	  });

   347	  // "no key here" may still assume the figure is served.
   348	  test("the key policy says the decrypted figure is NOT served", () => {

   350	    expect(ISSUER_KEY_POLICY).toMatch(/loopback is not uid-scoped/i);

   356	  });

   358	  // ⚠⚠ **THIS TEST FALSE-PASSED, AND IT WAS WRITTEN IN THE ROUND THAT FIXED A
   359	  // FALSE PASS.** `SNAPSHOT_PATH` was a module-level `const`, bound at import
   360	  // time, so setting the env var afterwards did nothing and `await import()`
   361	  // returned the cached module. It read the DEFAULT path — absent in a clean
   362	  // checkout — so `issuerView` said unavailable for that reason and the test
   363	  // passed with the refusal DELETED. **Measured: removing both refusal lines
   364	  // left all 46 tests green.** The path is read lazily now, and the control
   365	  // below is what proves the assertion can fail (`CLAIMS.md` E-215).
   366	  const withSnapshot = (body: unknown, fn: () => void) => {
   367	    const tmp = join(REPO, `web/api/.test-snapshot-${Math.abs(hashOf(JSON.stringify(body)))}.json`);
   368	    writeFileSync(tmp, JSON.stringify(body));
   369	    const prev = process.env.WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT;
   370	    process.env.WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT = tmp;
   371	    try { fn(); } finally {
   372	      if (prev === undefined) delete process.env.WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT;
   373	      else process.env.WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT = prev;
   374	      rmSync(tmp, { force: true });
   375	    }
   376	  };
   377	
   378	  test("CONTROL — a clean snapshot IS read, so the refusal test below can fail", () => {
   379	    // ⚠ Without this the refusal test proves nothing: an unavailable result is
   380	    // the default for a missing file, which is what made it false-pass.
   381	    withSnapshot({ producedAtUnix: 1, entries: { "a/b": { available: true, decrypted: "5" } } }, () => {
   382	      const v = issuerView("a", "b");
   383	      expect(v.available).toBe(true);
   384	      // ⚠ E-241: the snapshot HAS the value; the API reports only that it exists.
   385	      expect(v.decryptable).toBe(true);
   386	    });
   387	  });

   390	  // The decrypted figure must never cross this boundary. Loopback is not
