35:The design maintains the standard definition of OutstandingAmount (OA) as the sum of all non-issuer balances. A complementary value, ConfidentialOutstandingAmount (COA), tracks the confidential portion of circulation, ensuring that while individual balances are encrypted, the global supply remains auditable. MaximumAmount (MA) continues to cap the total supply, allowing validators to enforce consistency with the existing invariant OA ≤ MA.
43:- Enhanced Issuer Control: Provides optional Freeze and a Clawback transaction, giving issuers the tools needed to manage assets and enforce compliance. Clawback burns the holder’s confidential balance.
52:- **ConfidentialOutstandingAmount (COA):** The total amount of an MPT currently held in confidential balances by non-issuers, including any issuer-controlled dedicated account.
62:- **Clawback:** A privileged issuer-only operation performed via a `ConfidentialMPTClawback` transaction, which forcibly burns a holder’s entire confidential balance (decreasing both COA and OA) while preserving ledger accounting consistency through ZKPs. The burned funds are permanently removed from circulation.
74:- ConfidentialMPTClawback: An issuer-only transaction to forcibly burn a holder’s entire confidential balance, permanently removing those tokens from circulation.
106:- **Issuer encryption:** The same balance is also encrypted under the issuer’s public key (`IssuerEncryptedBalance`). This encrypted mirror supports supply consistency checks and issuer-level auditing without granting spending capability.
120:  - **Compact Clawback sigma proof (64 bytes)**: Used in `ConfidentialMPTClawback`. Proves the issuer's on-ledger encrypted balance mirror contains the plaintext amount being claimed.
128:The issuer may designate one or more **delegate accounts** to act on its behalf, for example, to submit `ConfidentialMPTClawback` transactions or to manage the dedicated account (Confidential Vault). Delegate accounts must be considered **fully trusted** by the issuer. A delegate account has complete operational authority within the scope delegated to it, and the protocol does not impose additional on-chain restrictions on delegate actions beyond those that apply to the issuer itself. Issuers should apply the same key-management standards to delegate accounts as to their primary issuer account.
130:`ConfidentialMPTSend`, `ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox`, `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack`, and `ConfidentialMPTClawback` are delegable. **`ConfidentialMPTConvert` is NOT delegable.** It is the transaction that registers an account's `HolderEncryptionKey`, and that key carries exclusive spending authority over the account's confidential balance — delegating it would let the delegate register a key whose secret it alone controls, and thereby take over the account's confidential funds.
142:| `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` | (default) | `number`  | `UINT64`      | The total amount of this token that is currently held in confidential balances. This value is adjusted with every `ConfidentialMPTConvert`, `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack`, and `ConfidentialMPTClawback` transaction. **Required** to use the confidential transfer feature. |
202:- The issuance can only be destroyed while `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is 0, but this requires no confidential-specific condition on `MPTokenIssuanceDestroy`. Because `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount <= OutstandingAmount` holds at all times (§6.6), the existing XLS-33 requirement already implies it.
209:In the rules below, `ΔCOA` and `ΔOA` denote a transaction's net change to this issuance's `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` and `OutstandingAmount`, and `ΔMPTAmount` denotes its net change to `MPTAmount` summed over _every_ `MPToken` of this issuance rather than that of any single holder.
211:- `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` <= `OutstandingAmount`. Since `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is an unsigned 64-bit field, this also catches any underflow: a subtraction below zero wraps to a value that necessarily exceeds `OutstandingAmount`.
224:  "ConfidentialOutstandingAmount": "500000",
245:| `IssuerEncryptedBalance`      |           | `string`  | `Blob`        | A 66-byte ElGamal ciphertext encrypting the holder's **total** confidential balance (`CB_S` + `CB_IN`) under the issuance's `IssuerEncryptionKey`. The issuer's mirror, used for supply reconciliation and as the basis for clawback (§12). |
250:**Deletion Transactions:** `MPTokenAuthorize` (with `tfMPTUnauthorize`), and implicitly by transactions that reclaim an empty holding on the holder's behalf — `VaultDelete`, `VaultWithdraw`, `VaultClawback`, and `LoanBrokerDelete`.
255:- **Holder-initiated deletion:** An `MPToken` that carries confidential balance fields cannot be deleted while the issuance's `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is non-zero, because the ledger cannot tell from the ciphertexts alone whether that particular holder's balance is zero. The attempt fails with `tecHAS_OBLIGATIONS`. Once `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` reaches 0, every holder's confidential balance is necessarily zero and the `MPToken` may be deleted normally, even if the confidential fields are still present on it.
256:- **Implicit deletion:** Deletion performed implicitly by one of the vault or loan-broker transactions above is blocked whenever the `MPToken` still carries any confidential balance field (`ConfidentialBalanceSpending`, `ConfidentialBalanceInbox`, `IssuerEncryptedBalance`, or `AuditorEncryptedBalance`), irrespective of `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount`, and fails with `tecHAS_OBLIGATIONS`. This is deliberately stricter than the holder-initiated rule, as defense in depth: objects reachable through those paths cannot acquire confidential fields in the first place — vault share issuances are created without `lsfMPTCanHoldConfidentialBalance`, and a vault or loan-broker pseudo-account cannot submit the `ConfidentialMPTConvert` that would register the fields — so the two rules never disagree on a state the protocol can actually produce.
262:- `ConfidentialBalanceSpending`, `ConfidentialBalanceInbox`, and `IssuerEncryptedBalance` are all present or all absent. A single `ConfidentialMPTConvert` creates all three at once.
264:- If a transaction creates or modifies any of `ConfidentialBalanceSpending`, `ConfidentialBalanceInbox`, `IssuerEncryptedBalance`, or `AuditorEncryptedBalance`, then the corresponding `MPTokenIssuance` MUST have the `lsfMPTCanHoldConfidentialBalance` flag enabled. The converse is deliberately not required: these fields may remain on an `MPToken` after all confidential balances have returned to zero, so merely carrying them is not constrained — only changing them is.
266:- While the issuance's `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is greater than 0, no `MPToken` may be removed from the ledger if, immediately before removal, it had a non-zero `MPTAmount` or carried any of `ConfidentialBalanceSpending`, `ConfidentialBalanceInbox`, `IssuerEncryptedBalance`, or `AuditorEncryptedBalance`. This holds regardless of which transaction attempts the removal (§7.2).

In all cases, `OutstandingAmount` (OA) and `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` (COA) are maintained in plaintext according to existing invariants.

679:## 12. Transaction: `ConfidentialMPTClawback`
681:Clawback involves the issuer forcibly reclaiming funds from a holder's account. This action is fundamentally incompatible with standard confidential transfers, as the issuer does not possess the holder's private ElGamal key and therefore cannot generate the required ZKPs for a normal ConfidentialMPTSend. To solve this, the protocol introduces a single and privileged transaction that allows an issuer to verifiably reclaim funds in one uninterruptible step.
683:This issuer-only transaction is designed to forcibly burn a holder's entire confidential balance, permanently removing those tokens from circulation. Both `OutstandingAmount` (OA) and `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` (COA) are reduced, effectively destroying the tokens rather than returning them to the issuer's reserve.
685:### 12.1 How the Clawback Process Works
687:1. Issuer Decrypts and Prepares: The issuer takes the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` ciphertext from the HolderToClawback's MPToken object and uses its own private key to decrypt it, revealing the holder's total confidential balance, m.
688:2. Issuer Submits Transaction: The issuer creates and signs a ConfidentialMPTClawback transaction, setting the `MPTAmount` field to m. It also generates and includes a compact Clawback sigma proof.
693:     2. The global `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` (COA) is decreased by `MPTAmount`.
702:| `TransactionType`   | Yes       | `string`  | `UINT16`      | N/A           | Identifies this as a `ConfidentialMPTClawback` transaction, which is 89.                                                              |
707:| `ZKProof`           | Yes       | `string`  | `BLOB`        | N/A           | A 64-byte **AND-composed compact Clawback sigma proof** proving that the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` encrypts the plaintext `MPTAmount`. |
731:3. The issuance does not have the `lsfMPTCanClawback` flag set. (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
734:6. The holder's `MPToken` is missing the `IssuerEncryptedBalance`. (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
736:8. The `MPTAmount` exceeds the global `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount`, or exceeds the global `OutstandingAmount`. (`tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`)
737:9. The ZKP fails to prove that the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` (the mirror balance) encrypts the plaintext `MPTAmount`. (`tecBAD_PROOF`)
746:  - `IssuerEncryptedBalance` is set to **Encrypted Zero**.
750:  - The global `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` (COA) is decreased by `MPTAmount`.
758:  "TransactionType": "ConfidentialMPTClawback",
822:4. The transaction attempts to upload keys or set `tfMPTSetCanHoldConfidentialBalance`, but `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` is greater than zero (tokens are already in confidential circulation). (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
864:`ConfidentialMPTConvert` and `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack` disclose their amounts in plaintext, unlike `ConfidentialMPTSend`. Hiding them would require proving in zero knowledge that the public balance change matches the confidential one, and would force `OutstandingAmount` and `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` to be tracked as ciphertexts. Keeping both totals in plaintext is what makes the supply invariants in §6.6 checkable at all. §11.8 works through the resulting leakage for a low-volume flow.
944:- Issuer Ciphertexts (`IssuerEncryptedBalance`): Every confidential balance is dually encrypted under the issuer's public key. This allows the issuer to monitor aggregate confidential circulation and reconcile it with public issuance.
1047:Compliance is supported by storing each confidential balance as parallel ciphertexts under the holder’s key (CB_S/CB_IN), the issuer’s key (`IssuerEncryptedBalance`), and an optional auditor’s key (`AuditorEncryptedBalance`) if enabled, with ZKPs ensuring all ciphertexts encrypt the same value, allowing the public to verify supply limits via issuer ciphertexts and auditors to decrypt balances if permitted.

   389	  // ⚠⚠ **THE POINT OF E-241, AND THE ONLY TEST THAT FAILS IF IT REGRESSES.**
   390	  // The decrypted figure must never cross this boundary. Loopback is not
   391	  // uid-scoped, so a served value reaches every local user out of a file the
   392	  // producing tool writes `mode 0600` (REVIEW-036 U1, E-228).
   393	  test("the decrypted balance is NEVER served, even when the snapshot has it", () => {
   394	    const secret = "123456789";
   395	    withSnapshot({ producedAtUnix: 1, entries: { "a/b": { available: true, decrypted: secret, publicAmount: "7" } } }, () => {
   396	      const v = issuerView("a", "b");
   397	      expect(v.available).toBe(true);
   398	      expect(v.decryptedWithheld).toBe(true);
   399	      // The figure appears NOWHERE in the serialised response.
   400	      expect(JSON.stringify(v)).not.toContain(secret);
   401	      // ...while the public plaintext amount, which IS public, still does.
   402	      expect(JSON.stringify(v)).toContain("7");
   403	    });
   404	  });

   426	  test("balances are DECIMAL STRINGS, so a UINT64 above 2^53 survives", () => {

    64	- **The observer view is computed in the visitor's browser**, from one WebSocket
    65	  connection, pinned to one validated ledger index that the page shows (M2, M3, M5).
    66	- **The capability matrix, the custody simulation and the wallet page are
    67	  static.** They are pure functions of this repository's own text today
    68	  (`web/api/capability.ts`, `web/api/custody-sim.ts`), so they are computed at
    69	  build time.
    70	- **The signer page is absent from the hosted build.** The launch plan already
    71	  says so, and E-241 had withdrawn its contents from the local tier.
    72	- **The issuer view is D-H3**, an owner decision.
    73	
    74	**Why this shape.** Under it, the hosted tier serves the static output of the
    75	public repository's own source, plus whatever D-H3 deliberately adds. Trigger 4,
    76	the decryption oracle, cannot fire: there is no request handler for a request to
    77	reach. Step 8's loopback falsifier has nothing to bind. Trigger 5 fires only for
    78	what is put into the bundle on purpose, and the gate can enumerate that.
    79	
    80	**What it costs.** The browser application's data layer moves from HTTP-to-Elysia
    81	to a WebSocket reader in the page, and `/api/*` stops existing in the hosted
    82	build. That is a second build target beside the local one, not a replacement:
    83	step 8's local tier stays as it is.
    84	
    85	⚠ **One obligation must not be reimplemented.** `web/api/ledger-view.ts` says
    86	why it composes `facility/src/loopback.ts` instead of reading the ledger itself:
    87	two readings of one obligation drift (E-141's shape). The hosted reader is the
    88	same `Snapshot` discipline with a second transport, WebSocket beside JSON-RPC,
    89	not a second reader. The network check (`network_id` 2, refuse anything else),
    90	the single pinned validated index, and the check that every answer came from
    91	that index stay in one implementation. ⚠ The observer harness's WebSocket client
    92	is not that implementation and should not be lifted into the page: it reads
    93	`validated` per request, so two reads on one view can come from two ledgers (M6).
    94	
    95	## What the hosted surface may touch, and may never touch
    96	
    97	**May touch:** public Devnet ledger state, read from the visitor's browser over
    98	the one named WebSocket endpoint; static content built from the public
    99	repository; and, only if D-H3 rules it, one published snapshot file.
   100	
   101	**May never touch:** any server-side request handler; any socket client or the
   102	signer's socket path; any secret, seed or private key, the demo issuer key
   103	included; any decryption routine in the shipped output; any network but Devnet;
   104	any second ledger endpoint, as a fallback or otherwise; and any analytics or
   105	third-party script.
   106	
   107	## The owner's decisions
   108	
   109	### D-H1. The hosting shape
   110	
   111	| option | cost | what it buys |
   112	|---|---|---|
   113	| **H1. Static site, browser reads Devnet directly** *(recommended)* | the page's data layer moves into the browser; a WebSocket transport under `Snapshot`; a second build target | no code of ours runs per request, so there is no rate limiting and no CORS change, and trigger 4 cannot fire. ⚠ Residual: the visitor's IP and every address they look up go to Ripple's node. Vercel sees each page load, and today's observer route carries the account in the URL (`web/app/src/routes/observer.tsx:7-8,51`), so a reload or a shared link sends the lookup to Vercel too unless the hosted build keeps it out of the URL |
   114	| H2. Vercel functions running the Elysia API against Devnet over JSON-RPC, with SSR | the API on the public internet: CORS for the deployed origin, rate limiting, caching, function logs holding visitors' IPs and queries, and billing exposure on the owner's account | the existing reader unchanged, and visitors' IPs hidden from Ripple's node. ⚠ Every route added later runs our code per request, which is trigger 4's shape |
   115	| H3. Prerendered pages only | the build reads Devnet once and bakes every view, with its ledger index and date | the cheapest and the most honest about time, since every value is a dated reading (P-3's habit). ⚠ A visitor cannot enter an account, so *"so people can try it"* reduces to *"so people can read it"* |
   116	
   117	**Why H1.** It is the only shape in which the answer to *"what does the hosted
   118	tier serve that a public node does not?"* is fixed at build time. H2 answers it
   119	with a promise about future routes. H3 answers it well and drops the live half
   120	of the goal.
   121	
   122	### D-H2. The ledger-source risk acceptance for a hosted page
   123	
   124	B-15's named risk acceptance was written for a local operator who chose their
   125	node (E-110, extended to the facility demo by E-151). A hosted page needs its
   126	own, and accepting a risk is the owner's act, not this document's. The proposed
   127	wording, from the launch plan:
   128	
   129	> *A lying or substituted node makes this page show wrong values. Nothing on this
   130	> page signs or submits, so no amount can be published because of it.*
   131	
   132	Under H1 it gains a second sentence, because the visitor's browser makes the
   133	connection:
   134	
   135	> *Your browser connects to the public Devnet node operated by Ripple. That
   136	> operator sees your IP address and every account you look up here.*
   137	
   138	| option | what it means |
   139	|---|---|
   140	| **Accept, by name, with both sentences on every view that shows a ledger value** *(recommended)* | the same shape as E-151: the risk is unmet by name, and the page says so where the value is |
   141	| Do not accept | H3 only, with every value a dated reading the page does not refresh |
   142	
   143	Corroboration across independently operated Devnet nodes is not offered as an
   144	option. Every Devnet host this repository names is under `rippletest.net`
   145	(`git grep -ohiE` for `devnet` hostnames, 2026-10-19), so every endpoint it knows
   146	has one operator. Whether an independently operated public Devnet node exists has
   147	**not been searched** outside this repository. That is a named gap, not a
   148	negative.
   149	
   150	### D-H3. The issuer view: what, if anything, the hosted page says the issuer reads
   178	### D-H4. What Vercel deploys, and from where
   179	
   180	Read on 2026-10-19 from Vercel's own pages: `vercel.com/docs/cli/deploy`, marked
   181	updated 2026-09-18, and `vercel.com/docs/git/vercel-for-github`, marked updated
   182	2026-09-17.
   183	
   184	| option | cost | what it buys |
   185	|---|---|---|
   186	| **A. Build and gate locally, deploy the prebuilt output from the CLI** *(recommended)* | a Vercel token on the owner's machine; each deployment is a deliberate act. `vercel build` writes `.vercel/output`, and `vercel deploy --prebuilt` uploads exactly that directory | the gate runs on the exact bytes that ship, which is phase 1 step 7's lesson: a rehearsal does not substitute for the gate on the published artifact. No third party gains any access to the public repository |
   187	| B. Vercel's Git integration, production from `main` | Vercel builds from source, so the gate has to run inside Vercel's build and fail it. ⚠ The Vercel for GitHub app's documented repository permissions include write access to `Administration`, `Contents`, `Pull Requests` and `Web Hooks`. That is a third party with write access to the public repository, which D8's option C exists to restrict | deploys follow merges. The integration deploys every push by default, previews included. A pull request from a fork needs a team member's authorization before it deploys, and that protection can be switched off in the project's settings. A fork's preview is a page under the owner's project saying whatever the fork says, which is E-209's presentation-integrity shape |
   188	
   189	**Why A.** It keeps the gate on the shipped bytes and keeps the public
   190	repository's write access where D8 put it.
   191	
   192	## Devnet resets
   193	
   194	The demo accounts are throwaway. When Devnet is reset they stop existing, and the
   195	page must say so:
   196	
   197	> *This account does not exist on Devnet at ledger N. Devnet is reset from time to
   198	> time and the demo accounts are throwaway. No other source is substituted.*
   199	
   200	A failed read must never render as an empty account, which reads exactly like a
   201	true statement about the ledger (`web/api/demo-accounts.ts` already guards the
   202	typo version of this). Under D-H3 B, a snapshot whose ciphertexts no longer match
   203	is shown as void, never as current. Old transaction hashes are not a foundation
   204	either, because the public endpoint stopped serving them without a reset (E-319).
   205	
   206	## Abuse, rate limiting and caching
   207	
   208	Under H1 nothing of ours runs per request, so there is nothing of ours to rate
   209	limit. The node's limits are Ripple's, not this project's. The page must render a
   210	refused or failed read, a rate-limit refusal included, as a named failure, not as
   211	an empty account. Static files cache normally. Ledger values are not cached; each view
   212	names the index it read.
   213	
   214	Under H2 all of this becomes ours, which is part of H2's cost.
   215	
   216	## Rotation triggers
   217	
   218	`ADR-0003`'s five triggers apply to the hosted build unchanged. Three more are
   219	specific to hosting:
   220	
   221	1. **Anything that runs code of ours per request**: a serverless function, edge
   222	   middleware, or on-demand rendering. That is H2, and it needs its own rotation.
   223	2. **Any datum in the shipped output that is not public ledger state or this
   224	   repository's own text.** D-H3's snapshot is the only exception that can be
   225	   ruled, and only by name.
   226	3. **A second network endpoint**, a fallback, or any widening of the page's
   227	   permitted connection targets.
   228	
   229	## What phase 2 step 4's gate must check
   230	
   231	The local gate, `tools/check-web-readonly.ts`, asserts the local shape. The
   232	hosted build needs its own part, run over the **built output**, not the source:
   233	
   234	- no server function, API route, serverless function or middleware in the output;
   235	- no socket client and no signer socket path;
   236	- no decryption routine and no secret-shaped value, with `check-no-secrets` run
   237	  over the output directory;
   238	- one permitted connection target, the named Devnet WebSocket, enforced by the
   239	  page's Content Security Policy and checked against it;
   240	- no account or issuance a visitor looks up carried in a URL the host receives;
   241	- every view that renders a ledger value also renders the source disclosure, the
   242	  network check and the ledger index.
   243	
   244	It is verified by breaking it, in both directions, with its controls run through
   245	the real detector, and it says in its own output what it does not check. Gates in
   246	this repository have false-passed before they worked, `ADR-0003`'s own among them
   247	(E-165, E-182, E-226).
   248	
   249	## What this decision does not claim
   250	
   251	- **That the hosted page is secure.** P-5 governs. It is a static demonstration.
   252	- **That the values it shows are true.** TLS to Ripple's node authenticates the

    54	## The decision proposed: a static site with no server of ours
   150	### D-H3. The issuer view: what, if anything, the hosted page says the issuer reads
   151	
   152	`ADR-0003`'s ruled position for the local tier is E-241: the route reports
   153	whether a balance is decryptable and never the figure, because *"the plaintext
   154	integer only ever demonstrated that THIS PROJECT holds the demo issuer key."* The
   155	launch plan's phase 2 text proposes the opposite for the hosted page: the
   156	snapshot *"is the point of the demo and becomes a deliberately published static
   157	artifact over throwaway Devnet accounts, with the disclosure beside it."* The two
   158	cannot both stand without a ruling.
   159	
   160	| option | cost | what it buys |
   161	|---|---|---|
   162	| **A. No figure** *(recommended)*. The page shows `IssuerEncryptedBalance` present and populated under the issuance's `IssuerEncryptionKey`, and says the issuer can always decrypt it (P-9) | none beyond the static page | E-241's position, carried unchanged to a public audience. The ciphertext and the key it is under are ledger readings a visitor can check. That the issuer can decrypt it is the specification's, cited as P-9, and no figure is asserted |
   163	| B. A published snapshot of the issuer's figures | run `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` locally and publish its results with the ledger index and, for each holder, the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` the figure was decrypted from. The tool does not record that ciphertext today (`tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts:220-262`), so B changes the one tool that holds the issuer key | the contrast the plan wants: the observer sees a ciphertext, the issuer sees a number. ⚠ Nothing a visitor can run shows the figures are true. Re-reading each ciphertext live shows only that the snapshot is current. Comparing the figures' sum with `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` shows nothing about the key either: that total is public plaintext, and anyone can split it among holders without decrypting anything (M9) |
   164	| C. B, plus a proof of correct decryption that the browser verifies | unpriced. XLS-0096's clawback proof is exactly a proof that the mirror encrypts a stated amount (M9), and M7 shows its verifier compiled into the WASM build but not wrapped by the typed API. Whether a clawback-shaped proof can be published safely, given that it is bound to a transaction context nobody submits, is a cryptographic question for the rotation | the only option under which a visitor can check each figure, and so the only one that answers E-241 |
   165	
   166	**Why A.** E-241's reasoning holds with more force on a public page. A figure no
   167	visitor can check is a claim with no falsifier, published under this project's
   168	name. B's checks cannot change that, because both pass for a publisher who never
   169	held the key. C is the way to show the number, and it needs the rotation's
   170	answer first. Launching with A costs nothing, and C can be added later without
   171	reopening the rest of this decision.
   172	
   173	⚠ **This recommendation departs from the launch plan**, whose phase 2 text calls
   174	the snapshot *"the point of the demo"*. An earlier draft of this document
   175	recommended B with the sum check as its falsifier. Preparing the self leg showed
   176	that the check passes for a forger, so the draft changed before review.
   177

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * check-no-secrets.ts — no secret and no personal datum in what would be published.
     3	 *
     4	 *   bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts                 # tree + history + local store
     5	 *   bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts --no-local-store  # shape classes only (CI)
     6	 *   bun run tools/check-no-secrets.ts --repo <dir> --rev <commit>   # one exact tree
     7	 *
     8	 * # Why this exists
     9	 *
    10	 * Phase 1 of `docs/PLAN-PUBLIC-LAUNCH-2026-09-22.md` makes this repository
    11	 * public, and its gate is *"the secret sweep passing on the exact tree that will
    12	 * be published"*. The plan says why it must be a gate and not a paragraph: **a
    13	 * sweep recorded in prose is a claim with no falsifier** (`CLAIMS.md` E-137,
    14	 * E-141). And this repository has shipped four gates that false-passed
    15	 * (E-165, E-168, E-182, E-201), so this one runs its controls through the same
    16	 * `scanRepo()` the real check calls, against a throwaway git repository it
    17	 * builds with planted secrets, every time it runs.
    18	 *
    19	 * # What it looks for
    20	 *
    21	 * | class | what | allowed when |
    22	 * |---|---|---|
    23	 * | `seed` | an XRPL family seed in base58, either algorithm, checksum valid **or not** | its 16 bytes of entropy come from a published rule (`PUBLIC_ENTROPY`) |
    24	 * | `seed-entropy-hex` | 32 hex digits after a `seed` or `entropy` label | same rule |
    25	 * | `labelled-scalar` | 64 or 66 hex digits after a label with an `sk`, `secret` or `private` component | it is derived from public entropy, or is a cited test vector |
    26	 * | `known-value` | **an exact value from the local secret store**, in every encoding listed in `needlesForSeed` | never |
    27	 * | `credential` | PEM private keys and common API-token shapes | its variable part, after the pattern's fixed prefix, is one character repeated: a placeholder. A PEM header never |
    28	 * | `secret-file` | a path named like a secret store (`state.json`, `profile.json`, `.env`, `*.pem`, ...) | never |
    29	 * | `personal-path` | a home directory or a macOS per-user temp directory, in five encodings | the user name is generic (`runner`, `user`, ...) |
    30	 * | `email` | an email address | reserved domains and three role addresses |
    31	 *
    32	 * **`known-value` is the only class that can see an UNLABELLED ElGamal secret.**
    33	 * A bare 64-hex scalar is indistinguishable from a transaction hash, so no
    34	 * shape rule can find one. That is why the default run REFUSES without the
    35	 * local store: a run that skipped it would look like the phase 1 gate and not
    36	 * be it. `--no-local-store` runs the shape classes and says so in its verdict
    37	 * word (`PASS-SHAPES-ONLY`), so a CI log cannot be mistaken for the real gate.
    38	 *
    39	 * # What "published" means here
    40	 *
    41	 * * **The tree:** every file `git add -A` would commit — tracked plus
    42	 *   untracked-not-ignored — read from disk. A gitignored file is not scanned,
    43	 *   because it is not published; that is a property, and a control pins it.
    44	 * * **`--rev`:** the blobs of exactly one commit's tree, read from git objects.
    45	 *   ⚠ It scans no history. The publication is two commits, so the phase 1 gate
    46	 *   is NOT this form: it is `--repo <export> --strict-history` on a clean export
    47	 *   (`REVIEW-057` R1-7 and R2-7).
    48	 * * **History:** every blob, commit message and tag message reachable from any
    49	 *   ref, and every path any commit ever touched. ⚠ **Personal paths and emails
    50	 *   in history are REPORTED, not failed**, because the launch plan rules that
    51	 *   history is squashed at publication (`CLAIMS.md` E-309) and this repository
    52	 *   stays the private archive. Secrets fail in history like anywhere else.
    53	 *   `--strict-history` fails on both, and is the form for the export: its two
    54	 *   commits are published history, so nothing in them may be merely reported.
    55	 *
    56	 * **No path is exempt, including `docs/reviews/` and this file.** Exempting a
    57	 * file is how a gate false-passes. This file contains no planted secret
    58	 * literally: the controls generate theirs at runtime.
    59	 *
    60	 * # Exit codes and the verdict line
    61	 *
    62	 * `0` pass, `1` the scanned tree has findings, `2` a control failed or the gate
    63	 * refused to run. **The last line of output is always
    64	 * `check-no-secrets: verdict=<WORD> exit=<N>`**, and a caller must require it,
    65	 * because Bun exits 1 on a crash too (`CLAIMS.md` E-259).
    66	 *
    67	 * # What it does NOT catch
    68	 *
    69	 * Printed every run by `printLimits()`, and each lexical limit is an
    70	 * ASSERTED-UNCAUGHT control: if a later change starts catching one, the control
    71	 * fails and the disclosure has to be updated rather than going stale
    72	 * (E-203's shape, from `check-zeroize-bounds` part 6).
    73	 */
    74	
   638	    i = nl + 1 + n + 1;

   641	
   642	/**
   643	 * ⚠ **Refuse what this gate cannot read as published** (`REVIEW-056` S2-1, S2-4).
   644	 * A `filter` attribute means `git add` stores bytes a clean filter produced, or,
   645	 * for Git LFS, a pointer while the content is published elsewhere. A
   646	 * `working-tree-encoding` attribute re-encodes on the way in. A gitlink or a
   647	 * nested repository publishes none of its content here. In each case the bytes
   648	 * on disk are not the bytes published, so the gate refuses rather than scanning
   649	 * the wrong ones. `git check-attr` reads `.gitattributes`, `.git/info/attributes`
   650	 * and `core.attributesFile` alike.
   651	 */
   652	function refuseTransformedTree(repo: string, paths: string[], gitlinks: string[], source?: string): void {
   653	  if (gitlinks.length) throw new Error(`a submodule or nested repository at ${gitlinks.slice(0, 5).join(", ")}: its content is another repository and is not scanned`);
   654	  if (!paths.length) return;
   655	  // ⚠ For `--rev`, the attributes are read FROM THAT COMMIT with `--source`, and
   656	  // parsed by git, so a comment that mentions `filter=` is not a filter (R2-6).
   657	  const src = source ? ["--source", source] : [];
   658	  const out = gitText(repo, ["check-attr", "-z", "--stdin", ...src, "filter", "working-tree-encoding"], paths.join("\0") + "\0").split("\0");
   659	  const bad: string[] = [];
   660	  for (let i = 0; i + 2 < out.length; i += 3) if (out[i + 2] !== "unspecified" && out[i + 2] !== "unset") bad.push(`${out[i]} (${out[i + 1]}=${out[i + 2]})`);
   661	  if (bad.length) throw new Error(`content-transforming attributes on ${bad.length} path(s), e.g. ${bad.slice(0, 3).join(", ")}: the published bytes are not the bytes on disk`);
   662	}
   663	
   664	/** What `git add -A` would commit, read from disk. Symlinks are not followed. */
   665	export function treeUnits(repo: string): Unit[] {
   666	  const names = gitText(repo, ["ls-files", "-z", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard"]).split("\0").filter(Boolean);
   667	  const staged = gitText(repo, ["ls-files", "-z", "-s"]).split("\0").filter(Boolean);
   668	  const gitlinks = staged.filter((l) => l.startsWith("160000 ")).map((l) => l.slice(l.indexOf("\t") + 1));
   669	  // An untracked nested repository is listed as a directory: `git add -A` would record a gitlink.
   670	  gitlinks.push(...names.filter((n) => n.endsWith("/")));
   671	  refuseTransformedTree(repo, names.filter((n) => !n.endsWith("/")), gitlinks);
   672	  const units: Unit[] = [];
   673	  for (const n of [...new Set(names)]) {
   674	    const p = join(repo, n);
   675	    units.push({ where: n, text: n, scope: "tree", kind: "path" });
   676	    if (!existsSync(p) && !lstatSync(p, { throwIfNoEntry: false })) {
   677	      // Deleted on disk, still in the index: the index copy is what a plain commit publishes.
   678	      try { units.push({ where: n, text: asText(git(repo, ["cat-file", "blob", `:${n}`])), scope: "tree", kind: "file" }); } catch { /* not in index either */ }
   679	      continue;
   680	    }
   681	    const s = lstatSync(p);
   682	    if (s.isSymbolicLink()) units.push({ where: `${n} (symlink target)`, text: readlinkSync(p), scope: "tree", kind: "file" });
   683	    else if (s.isFile()) units.push({ where: n, text: asText(readFileSync(p)), scope: "tree", kind: "file" });
   684	  }
   685	  return units;
   747	
   748	export interface Finding extends Hit { where: string; line: number; scope: "tree" | "history" }
   749	
   750	export function scanUnits(units: Unit[], store: Store): { findings: Finding[]; allowed: Map<string, number>; seenAllowed: Set<string> } {
   751	  const findings: Finding[] = [];
   752	  const allowed = new Map<string, number>();
   753	  const seenAllowed = new Set<string>();
   754	  for (const u of units) {
   755	    const hits = u.kind === "path" ? [secretFileHit(u.text), ...pathHits(u.text), ...emailHits(u.text)].filter((h): h is Hit => !!h) : scanText(u.text, store);
   756	    for (const h of hits) {
   757	      if (h.allowedBy) {
   758	        const k = `${h.cls}: ${h.allowedBy}`;
   759	        allowed.set(k, (allowed.get(k) ?? 0) + 1);
   760	        seenAllowed.add(h.allowedBy);
   761	        continue;
   762	      }
   763	      const line = u.kind === "path" ? 0 : u.text.slice(0, h.at).split("\n").length;
   764	      findings.push({ ...h, where: u.where, line, scope: u.scope });
   765	    }
   766	  }
   767	  return { findings, allowed, seenAllowed };
   768	}

   773	 * ⚠⚠ **What this gate cannot see.** Each entry is also an ASSERTED-UNCAUGHT
   774	 * control in `runControls()`: the planted secret must NOT be found. If a later
   775	 * change starts finding one, the control fails and this list must be updated,
   776	 * so the disclosure cannot go stale.
   777	 */

   779	  "a secret split across two string literals or two lines",
   780	  "a secret in UTF-16, base64, gzip or any other re-encoding",
   781	  "an unlabelled 64-hex ElGamal secret that is NOT in the local store (no shape rule can tell it from a hash)",
   782	  "a seed or key in RFC 1751 words, or as a decimal or byte array",
   783	  "a home path under a user name on the generic list",
   784	  "a labelled scalar printed in fewer than 48 hex digits: a uniform key is that short with probability below 2^-64",
   785	  "a secret listed as an array element under a label, rather than as the value of a key",
   786	  "gitignored files, the reflog and unreachable objects: none of them is published",
   787	  "a hostname, a machine serial, or a person's name — only paths and email addresses are personal-data classes",
   788	];

   793	  controlsRun++;
   794	  if (!ok) controlsFailed.push(`${name}${detail ? ` — ${detail}` : ""}`);
   795	}

   296	## Consequences
   297	
   298	- ~~**Architecture §6 step 8 is buildable now**, without a rotation, and its falsifier is the gate.~~ ⚠⚠ **STRUCK 2026-09-02.** The rotation ran (`REVIEW-036`), the claim that excused it did not survive, and **the gate is a falsifier for the SIGNING property only** — it does not check egress and now says so in its own output. **What is true: step 8 is BUILT, its signing property holds and is gated, and its disclosure boundary is open and named.**
   299	- **The signer keeps its whole AUTHORITY and gains no client that can exercise it.** `Pair` and `Prepare` remain reachable only from a process that can open the socket. ⚠ **Revision 1 said *"gains no client"* flatly and that is false** — `web/api/signer-status.ts` is the tier's UDS client and `check-web-readonly` part 5 counts exactly one (`REVIEW-036d` F4). The substance was right and the sentence was loose.
   300	- ⚠ **A future signing web tier is more expensive than it would have been**, because it must now argue against a recorded decision as well as design a mechanism. **That is the intended cost.**
   301	- **`CLAUDE.md`'s stack line becomes true rather than aspirational** once step 8 is built, and the line should say when it became true.
   302	
   303	---
   304	
   305	## Before this ADR can be accepted
   306	
   307	1. ~~**An adversarial rotation on this document's central claim.**~~ ⚠ **DISCHARGED 2026-09-02 — `REVIEW-036`, four legs, differed packets. The rotation's answer is that the claim needed REWRITING, not ratifying**, and revision 2 is that rewrite. **This item is closed; item 5 replaced it.**
   308	2. ~~**The gate verified by breaking it in both directions.**~~ **DONE, and extended.** `REVIEW-036` found the gate silent on **the loopback bind that architecture §6 step 8 names as a falsifier** (E-227) and carrying **two socket detectors that disagreed**, the narrower one wired to the whole-tier count (E-226). ⚠ **The pre-fix gate was MEASURED exiting 0 with a real second UDS client in the tree.** Both are fixed, part 6 is new, and **four** break tests against the real tree exit 1 — a `0.0.0.0` bind, a bare `listen(port)`, a LAN literal, and a `0.0.0.0` **dev-server** host. ⚠⚠ **PART 6 DOES NOT ADDRESS U1 AND MUST NOT BE READ AS DOING SO** (`REVIEW-037` F6): it checks the tier binds to **loopback**, and U1's whole point is that **loopback is not uid-scoped**. **Part 6 closes a falsifier the architecture named; the egress boundary is still open and is trigger 5's subject.** ⚠ **Round two additionally found part 6's first version checked the API and NOT `web/app/vite.config.ts`, which sets a `host` AND proxies `/api` — so a dev server on `0.0.0.0` would have published the whole API, issuer route included, to the LAN, bypassing the API's own loopback bind** (`CLAIMS.md` E-231). **Found by self-attack, not by a leg.**
   309	3. **D2's fourth reason verified at source or recorded as a gap** — the wallet codec question. **DONE** — a named, non-load-bearing gap, verified by `REVIEW-036d` at `web/api/capability.ts:59-76` and pinned by `web/api/disclosure.test.ts:443-459`.
   310	4. **A wording test over the interface's privacy claims.** **DONE as an artifact** — `web/api/disclosure.test.ts`. ⚠ **Its existence does not repair a false claim it does not name**, and `REVIEW-036` found five factual errors in this document that no wording test was ever going to catch.
   311	5. ~~**NEW, AND IT IS A RULING THIS DOCUMENT MAY NOT TAKE FOR ITSELF.**~~ ⚠ **RULED 2026-09-05 — option 2, remove the disclosure. `CLAIMS.md` E-241.** The tier serves neither the decrypted figure nor the socket path. **This was the last open precondition.**
   312	
   313	## ⚠⚠ RULED 2026-09-05 — the issuer route, and it is OPTION 2
   314	
   315	**The project owner ruled: remove the disclosure rather than guard it.** `CLAIMS.md` **E-241**.
   316	
   317	- **`GET /api/issuer/:account/:issuanceId` no longer returns `decrypted`.** It reports `decryptable` — *whether* the issuer could read the balance — and never the figure.
   318	- **`GET /api/signer` no longer returns `socketPath` or the signer's `note`**, which carried the holder address, the randomness-ledger generation id and its **entry count**.
   319	
   320	⚠ **The remedy is E-212's own, applied one layer further.** That fix moved the KEY out and left the RESULT leaving; this moves the result out too. **There is no request that reaches a decryption and none that returns one, so there is still nothing to authenticate.**
   321	
   322	⚠ **What the demonstration keeps is the point it was making.** That the issuer *can* read any holder's balance is proved by `IssuerEncryptedBalance` being present and populated on the ledger — P-9, which no configuration removes. **The plaintext integer only ever demonstrated that THIS PROJECT holds the demo issuer key.** The figure is in `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts`, which is where `CLAUDE.md` says the issuer view belongs: *"a single CLI command … Not a standalone application."*
   323	
   324	**The falsifier is a test that fails if the figure ever crosses the boundary again** — `disclosure.test.ts`, *"the decrypted balance is NEVER served, even when the snapshot has it"*, ⚠ **verified by reverting the withholding: it fails, and it is the only test that does.**
   325	
   326	⚠⚠ **This does NOT make the tier uid-safe. It makes it disclose nothing worth uid-protecting.** **Trigger 5 stays in the falsifier list**, because the next route someone adds is where it fires again.
   327	
   328	---
   329	
   330	### The options as they were priced, kept for the record
   331	
   332	**The four options put to the owner:**
   333	
   334	**`REVIEW-036` U1 established TWO sites, and revision 2 first carried only one into this section — `REVIEW-037` F4, `CLAIMS.md` E-233:**
   335	
   336	1. **`GET /api/issuer/:account/:issuanceId`** served `decrypted` confidential balances (until E-241, 2026-09-05), unauthenticated, out of a file the project writes `mode 0600`.
   337	2. ⚠ **`GET /api/signer`** returns the signer's answer **and the socket path and every candidate path probed** (`web/api/server.ts:74-78` spreads `...probe`), out from behind a directory the project sets `0700` *"to stop another **user** reaching the socket."* **Its `note` carries the holder address and the randomness ledger's count.**
   338	
   339	⚠⚠ **Dropping the second was the same narrowing E-212 records** — *"the author read their own warning as narrower than it was"* — **committed against a finding written four hours earlier. Both sites are in scope and both are handed over.**
   340	
   341	⚠ **A coding session may not rule on this, and this one did not.** The choice is a genuine trade and it belongs to the project owner:
   342	
   343	- **Authenticate the route**, and accept that the demonstration now has a credential in it — which is the shape `REVIEW-003b` killed on the signing side, and the reason *"there is nothing to authenticate"* was treated as a feature when the oracle was removed.
   344	- **Stop serving `decrypted`**, and accept that the issuer view — *"the honest counterpart to showing what the public sees"*, which `CLAUDE.md` requires — loses the number that makes the point.
   345	- **Accept it as a named risk**, the way `E-151` extended `REVIEW-019`'s acceptance to the facility demo, and stamp every issuer response with the disclosure the way every facility run record carries `underB15Conditions: false`.
   346	- **Move the API off loopback TCP and onto a Unix domain socket with `0600`**, which binds its reach to the same mode bits as the data. ⚠ **Revision 2 first wrote this as *"serve it only to a caller that can already read the file"* and called it *"the only option that ADDS no credential"*, which was a PHANTOM** (`REVIEW-037` F4b, `CLAIMS.md` E-233): **loopback TCP carries no peer credential** — `LOCAL_PEERCRED` is a Unix-socket facility — so on TCP the only way to prove a caller could read the file is to hand it a token FROM the file, **which is adding a credential**. The sound version of the option is a **transport change**, and it costs the browser: a page cannot open a Unix socket, which is the property this whole ADR rests on.
   347	
   348	## ⚠⚠ Shipping without a ruling IS a choice, and it is option 3
   349	
   350	**Round two's sharpest procedural finding, upheld** (`REVIEW-037` F3): a document cannot say *"a coding session may not rule on this"* and *"the tier ships with the boundary open"* and *"step 8 is buildable now"* — **because shipping open selects option 3 by default while keeping the appearance of having deferred.**
   351	
   352	**Stated plainly, which is what was owed** — ⚠ *true until the ruling of 2026-09-05 below, and kept as written*: ⚠ **this project currently accepts local cross-uid disclosure of a demo issuer's decrypted balances and the signer's local metadata, on a Devnet demonstration, under P-5.** That is option 3, taken by default and now written down. **It is reversible and it is the owner's to reverse** — the other three options stay open, and the register row (E-228) is the falsifier if anyone later claims the tier discloses nothing.
   353	
   354	⚠ ~~**`ADR-0003` stays `Proposed` on this item alone**~~ — **RULED 2026-09-05, option 2, and this document is now `Accepted`.** Every precondition is discharged.
8. **The web tier — a READ-ONLY, multi-role demonstration surface.** Bun · TanStack Start · Elysia · Tailwind · shadcn-idiom components. **SCOPED, BUILT AND EXERCISED 2026-08-28** — `web/api/` (Elysia, read-only) and `web/app/` (TanStack Start), **89 tests**, both type-checks clean, `make check-web-readonly` is the seventh gate, and the observer view was read against **live Devnet state** under the remote risk acceptance: two accounts at issuance `004625C9…FEE332CB25E8`, **`ConfidentialBalanceInbox` publicly identifiable as exactly zero on both** while `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` was non-zero on the same code path — **F-1 demonstrated live rather than asserted.** ⚠ **NOT under B-15's loopback condition**; the view is stamped `underB15Conditions: false` and says so on the page. **The scoping began with a correction**: ⚠ **`CLAUDE.md` told every new session that *"step 8 was written on 2026-08-26"* and it never was.** §6 had seven steps, §7 followed step 7 directly, and `grep -n "step 8"` over this document returned nothing. So the web tier had **no scope, no falsifier and no gate in the authority document** while the file every session reads first asserted that it did. **A scope note needs a re-check date exactly as much as an amendment claim does** — this document's own lesson about the `xrpl.js` blocker paragraph, landing one section over (`CLAIMS.md` **E-204**).

   **What the step is, in one sentence: it renders what is already readable, and it adds no SIGNING authority.** ⚠⚠ **THIS READ *"and it adds no authority"* UNTIL 2026-09-02, AND THAT IS THE CLAIM `REVIEW-036` RETRACTED** (`CLAIMS.md` E-230): the tier served a decrypted issuer view and signer-local status, unauthenticated, to **any local uid**, out from behind `0600`/`0700` mode bits the project sets deliberately (E-228), until E-241 removed both on 2026-09-05. ⚠ This said *"serves"* until 2026-10-18. **A DISCLOSURE boundary moved. The signing claim is the sound one and is the one this step needs.** Four decisions were put to the project owner during scoping and ruled; they are recorded in **`ADR-0003`** and summarised here because §6 is where a step's shape lives.

   - **D1 — the tier is read-only and never signs.** Browser → Elysia over localhost HTTP → a rippled node over JSON-RPC, plus **one** call into the signer's Unix socket: `Request::Status`, and nothing else. **Signing stays in the `wconf-signer` CLI**, where the holder confirms in the signer's own UI against parsed transaction fields (step 1's falsifier). ⚠⚠ ~~**This is the entire reason step 8 is buildable without an adversarial rotation** — it moves no trust boundary, because it introduces no new authority.~~ **STRUCK 2026-09-02 by `REVIEW-036`** — the rotation ran and ruled that reasoning unsound. **What is true: it introduces no new SIGNING authority**, and that is a narrower statement than *no boundary moves*. ⚠ **The rotation this sentence said was unnecessary is the one that falsified it.** The moment any path in this tier can reach a signing operation, it is a different design and §3's rotation requirement applies **before** implementation.
   - **D2 — the browser wallet is prove-and-contrast, not a participant.** Connect a wallet, prove account control, show that account's **observer view**, and then show the capability matrix explaining what it cannot do. ⚠ **A browser wallet cannot perform a confidential operation**, and that is the demonstration rather than a gap: the operation needs the ElGamal secret **and** the XRPL account key; under the ruled keygen (`REVIEW-012`) the ElGamal secret is **derived from the master seed**; and a wallet extension exists precisely in order not to export seed material. **A fourth reason — that the extension's bundled codec does not know the XLS-0096 types — is asserted by no primary source this repository holds and is carried as a NAMED GAP, not as a premise.**

  1124	export function scanRepo(repo: string, store: Store, o: { tree: boolean; history: boolean; rev?: string }): ScanResult {
  1125	  const t = o.tree ? (o.rev ? revUnits(repo, o.rev) : treeUnits(repo)) : [];
  1126	  const h = o.history ? historyUnits(repo) : [];
  1127	  const r = scanUnits([...t, ...h], store);
  1128	  // ⚠ Files are counted by PATH, not by unique blob (REVIEW-056 S5-3): two paths
  1129	  // with identical content are two published files.
  1130	  const files = (us: Unit[]) => us.filter((u) => u.kind === "file").reduce((n, u) => n + (u.paths ?? 1), 0);
  1131	  return { ...r, unitsTree: files(t), unitsHistory: h.filter((u) => u.kind !== "path").length };
  1132	}
  1133	
  1172	export function parseArgs(argv: string[]): { flags: Set<string>; repo?: string; rev?: string; stores: string[] } {
  1173	  const FLAGS = new Set(["--no-local-store", "--history-only", "--tree-only", "--strict-history", "--controls-only"]);
  1174	  const OPTS = new Set(["--repo", "--rev", "--store"]);
  1175	  const flags = new Set<string>();
  1176	  const out: { flags: Set<string>; repo?: string; rev?: string; stores: string[] } = { flags, stores: [] };
  1177	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
  1178	    const a = argv[i];
  1179	    if (FLAGS.has(a)) { flags.add(a); continue; }
  1180	    if (!OPTS.has(a)) throw new Error(`unknown argument '${a}'`);
  1181	    const v = argv[++i];
  1182	    if (v === undefined || v.startsWith("--")) throw new Error(`${a} needs a value`);
  1183	    if (a === "--store") out.stores.push(v);
  1184	    else if (a === "--repo") out.repo = v;
  1185	    else out.rev = v;
  1186	  }
  1187	  if (flags.has("--tree-only") && flags.has("--history-only")) throw new Error("--tree-only and --history-only together select nothing");
  1188	  if (out.rev && flags.has("--history-only")) throw new Error("--rev scans one tree; it cannot be combined with --history-only");
  1189	  if (flags.has("--strict-history") && (out.rev || flags.has("--tree-only"))) throw new Error("--strict-history has no effect without a history scan");
  1190	  if (flags.has("--no-local-store") && out.stores.length) throw new Error("--no-local-store and --store contradict each other");
  1191	  return out;
  1192	}

   271	## 7. What this architecture must never claim
   272	
   273	Beyond the standing prohibitions in `CLAIMS.md`, which govern:
   274	
   275	- That it hides **who** transacts, with whom, when, or how often. It hides amounts (P-4, P-11).
   276	- That a holder is private from the issuer (P-9).
   277	- That anything is secure, audited, non-custodial or production-ready (P-5).
   278	- That keys can be rotated or recovered (P-10).
   279	- That the local-signer boundary stops an adversary with code execution (§3).
   280	- That a demo of the happy path is evidence of confidentiality. It is not — that is what the observer harness is for.
   281	- **That reading the ledger over TLS makes the ledger data trustworthy.** It authenticates the channel to a node the operator chose. A hostile node substitutes a destination's `HolderEncryptionKey` just as well over TLS, and F-23 says that costs the amount, not the transaction. Added 2026-08-19 with §6 step 2, because the temptation to write "reads the ledger securely" is exactly the shape this list exists to stop.

   124	  - **Single Bulletproof (688 bytes)**: Used in `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack`. Proves the remaining balance after withdrawal is non-negative.
   125	
   126	### 5.5. Delegate Accounts
   127	
   128	The issuer may designate one or more **delegate accounts** to act on its behalf, for example, to submit `ConfidentialMPTClawback` transactions or to manage the dedicated account (Confidential Vault). Delegate accounts must be considered **fully trusted** by the issuer. A delegate account has complete operational authority within the scope delegated to it, and the protocol does not impose additional on-chain restrictions on delegate actions beyond those that apply to the issuer itself. Issuers should apply the same key-management standards to delegate accounts as to their primary issuer account.
   129	
   130	`ConfidentialMPTSend`, `ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox`, `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack`, and `ConfidentialMPTClawback` are delegable. **`ConfidentialMPTConvert` is NOT delegable.** It is the transaction that registers an account's `HolderEncryptionKey`, and that key carries exclusive spending authority over the account's confidential balance — delegating it would let the delegate register a key whose secret it alone controls, and thereby take over the account's confidential funds.
   131	
   132	## 6. Ledger Entry: `MPTokenIssuance`
   699	
   700	| Field Name          | Required? | JSON Type | Internal Type | Default Value | Description                                                                                                                           |
   701	| :------------------ | :-------- | :-------- | :------------ | :------------ | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
   702	| `TransactionType`   | Yes       | `string`  | `UINT16`      | N/A           | Identifies this as a `ConfidentialMPTClawback` transaction, which is 89.                                                              |
   703	| `Account`           | Yes       | `string`  | `ACCOUNTID`   | N/A           | The **Issuer** account sending the transaction.                                                                                       |
   704	| `Holder`            | Yes       | `string`  | `ACCOUNTID`   | N/A           | The account from which funds are being clawed back.                                                                                   |
   705	| `MPTokenIssuanceID` | Yes       | `string`  | `UINT192`     | N/A           | The unique identifier for the MPT issuance.                                                                                           |
   706	| `MPTAmount`         | Yes       | `number`  | `UINT64`      | N/A           | The plaintext total amount being removed.                                                                                             |
   707	| `ZKProof`           | Yes       | `string`  | `BLOB`        | N/A           | A 64-byte **AND-composed compact Clawback sigma proof** proving that the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` encrypts the plaintext `MPTAmount`. |
   708	
   709	This single transaction securely and verifiably burns the holder's confidential balance, removing the tokens from circulation without crediting them to any account, while ensuring the integrity of the ledger's public accounting is perfectly maintained.
   710	
   711	### 12.3. Transaction Fee
   712	
   713	**Fee Structure:** Custom
   714	
   715	This transaction requires 10 times the standard base fee because zero-knowledge proof verification costs validators significantly more than processing a public MPT transaction.
   716	
   717	### 12.4. Failure Conditions
   718	
   719	#### 12.4.1. Data Verification
   720	
   721	1. The `ConfidentialTransfer` feature is not enabled. (`temDISABLED`)
   722	2. The `Account` is not the issuer of the `MPTokenIssuanceID`. (`temMALFORMED`)
   723	3. The `Account` is attempting to claw back from itself (`Account` == `Holder`). (`temMALFORMED`)
   724	4. The `ZKProof` is not exactly 64 bytes. (`temMALFORMED`)
   725	5. `MPTAmount` is zero or exceeds the maximum limits. (`temBAD_AMOUNT`)
   726	
   727	#### 12.4.2. Protocol-Level Failures
   728	
   729	1. The `Holder` account does not exist. (`tecNO_TARGET`)
   730	2. The `MPTokenIssuance` or the holder's `MPToken` object does not exist. (`tecOBJECT_NOT_FOUND`)
   731	3. The issuance does not have the `lsfMPTCanClawback` flag set. (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
   732	4. The issuance is missing the `IssuerEncryptionKey`. (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
   733	5. The issuance does not have the `lsfMPTCanHoldConfidentialBalance` flag set. (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
   734	6. The holder's `MPToken` is missing the `IssuerEncryptedBalance`. (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
   735	7. The holder's `MPToken` is missing the `HolderEncryptionKey`. (`tecNO_PERMISSION`)
   736	8. The `MPTAmount` exceeds the global `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount`, or exceeds the global `OutstandingAmount`. (`tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`)
   737	9. The ZKP fails to prove that the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` (the mirror balance) encrypts the plaintext `MPTAmount`. (`tecBAD_PROOF`)

    24	
    25	## 0. What this is, in one paragraph
    26	
    27	REVIEW-005c struck ADR-0001 §(e)'s nonce formula and specified a replacement,
    28	but §3 recorded that the replacement was **not installable**: mpt-crypto
    29	exposes no entry point that lets a caller influence the nonce. Path A vendors
    30	mpt-crypto at a pinned commit and replaces one function. That is what this is.
    31	Six files differ from upstream; the other 44 are byte-identical, and
    32	`vendor-verify.sh` asserts it. The Fiat–Shamir challenge is **unchanged** —
    33	tested by having the pristine upstream verifier accept our proofs, and then by
    34	the ledger accepting one. *Tested, not proven*: upstream-verifier compatibility
    35	is executed at **n=3 and n=4 with a bound context** — the two shapes Send
    36	actually uses — and a passing test is not a proof for every input. The n=4 arm
    37	exists because adversarial review caught this sentence overclaiming: it
    38	previously said "with and without a context", where the no-context evidence was
    39	a nonce-KDF vector over opaque bytes, which cannot detect a
    40	statement-serialisation bug at all.
    41	
    42	## 1. What changed, and why each change
    43	
    44	Upstream's construction satisfied four of the nine properties both review legs
    45	agreed on. The five it did not:
    46	
    47	| Required by REVIEW-005c §2 | Upstream `85dbac7` | Where fixed |
    48	|---|---|---|
    49	| HKDF-**SHA-512** | HMAC-SHA-256 | `wconf_generate_nonces` |
    50	| Transcript **bytes** bound in, shared with the challenge | only `statement_hash`, a 32-byte digest built by a **second, separately written encoder** | one encoder per proof type, in the four provers |
    51	| Wide 64-byte reduction | `reduce32` of a 32-byte HMAC output, biased ~2⁻¹²⁸ | `secp256k1_mpt_scalar_reduce64`, new |
    52	| **No rejection sampling** | retries with an appended `sub_counter` byte | deleted |
    53	| **Abort on zero, never retry** | retries, up to 256 times | `return 0` |
    54	
    55	Already satisfied upstream and preserved: fixed-width 32-byte big-endian
    56	witness encoding, length framing throughout, per-proof-type domain and count
    57	separation, and the ConvertBack witness order `(b, ρ, sk_A)` — **verified in
    58	place**, not assumed, at `proof_compact_convertback.c`.
    59	
    60	### The one that mattered most
    61	
    62	Upstream built the statement bytes **twice**: once inside the challenge
    63	function, once again in the prover to make `stmt_hash`. The two agreed at
    64	`85dbac7`, but nothing *made* them agree. REVIEW-005b §1.1 requires one
    65	construction feeding both. So each prover now serialises its statement once and
    66	passes those bytes to the nonce KDF and to the challenge.
    67	
    68	The challenge hashes the same bytes in the same order as upstream. That is not
    69	a stylistic constraint — rippled recomputes this challenge, so any reordering
    70	rejects on chain. `nonce-crosscheck.ts` check 1 is what holds the line.
    71	
    72	### The construction
    73	
    74	```
    75	IKM  = "WCONF-NONCE-v1"
    76	    || u32be(k)
    77	    || u32be(domain_len)  || domain
    78	    || u32be(witness_len) || witness'
    79	    || u32be(stmt_len)    || stmt_bytes      <- the challenge's bytes, not a re-derivation
    80	    || u32be(ctx_len)     || context_id      <- ctx_len = 0, no bytes, if NULL
    81	
    82	PRK  = HMAC-SHA512("WCONF-NONCE-v1/HKDF-Extract-salt", IKM)   [fixed salt, no entropy]
    83	T(i) = HMAC-SHA512(PRK, T(i-1) || domain || i)                [64 bytes per nonce]
    84	n_i  = T(i) reduced mod the group order, wide                 [one block, one nonce]
    85	```
    86	
    87	Every variable-length field is length-framed, so no two distinct input tuples
    88	produce the same preimage. Each 64-byte Expand block feeds exactly one wide
    89	reduction, which is what removes the rejection-sampling branch entirely rather
    90	than merely making it rare.
217:/** Devnet, and nothing else. `CLAUDE.md`'s hard rule, checked before any read. */

   160	⚠ **A fourth trigger that is easy to miss, because it is a change in what is displayed rather than in what is reachable:** the demo issuer key. The issuer view decrypts balances, and `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts` already refuses to take the key on the command line. **A web tier that accepts an issuer key over HTTP has built a decryption oracle**, whatever its authority to sign. The discipline the CLI tool set is matched and not lowered.
   161	
   162	⚠⚠ **THIS TRIGGER FIRED, AND THE SESSION THAT WROTE IT DID NOT NOTICE (`CLAIMS.md` E-212).** Hours after writing the paragraph above, the same session put `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK` into the API process and decrypted per request behind an unauthenticated `GET`. ⚠ **The sentence was read as narrower than it is: it says *"accepts an issuer key over HTTP"*, and the author took that to mean the key ARRIVING in a request — while the oracle is the RESULT LEAVING in a response.** Both are the same trigger. **The wording is kept unchanged, because the defect was in the reading and rewriting it would hide what happened.**
   163	
   164	**What the trigger means, stated so it cannot be read narrowly again:** ⚠ **if any request to this tier can cause a decryption, the tier is an oracle**, regardless of where the key came from, whether the caller supplies it, or whether a browser is involved. **CORS is not a defence** — it constrains browsers, and `curl` is not a browser. The remedy taken was to **remove the capability rather than guard it**: the key lives in a CLI tool, the tier reads that tool's output, and there is no request that reaches a decryption.
   165	
   166	### 5. ⚠⚠ EGRESS — the trigger the list was MISSING, and the one that had already fired
   167	
   168	**Added by `REVIEW-036`, found independently by the self leg and the repo-access leg.**
   169	
   170	> **If this tier makes any datum reachable by a party who could not obtain that datum before the tier existed, the boundary has MOVED — regardless of whether the tier signs, holds a key, or performs any computation.**
   171	
   172	⚠⚠ **THIS FIRED THE DAY THE TIER WAS BUILT AND NOBODY LOOKED, BECAUSE TRIGGERS 1-4 ALL ASK WHAT THE TIER CAN *DO*. None asks what it PUBLISHES, or to whom.**
   173	
   174	**The project sets a per-user barrier at both sensitive resources this tier touches, deliberately, and says so at each site:**
   175
